On Self-Manifestation · Engineering Chapter · The Field Concretization Group Form · Chapter 16 v1.0

# The Discrimination Form of Sedimentation Dynamics

1\. Purpose

### 1.1  Use and Central Question

This paper is a Discrimination Form. It has three uses: for the concretized-field instances it actually studies, to discriminate, instance by instance, the dynamics types and mechanism combinations their historical dynamics use; for every declared candidate pair, to give a distinguishing observation, a proof that the discrimination is valid, bounds of Misjudgment, blind spots and breach modes; and to register the dynamical laws of each instance by type, for citation by version downstream.  
The central question is this: for two dynamical hypotheses on the same working domain (a declared candidate closed law, and a frozen comparison model or comparison class), under what protocol and with what reading can they be separated by finite observation; when they are separated, which complete set of mechanism hypotheses is excluded; and when they cannot be separated, what are the range of equivalence, its cause and the minimal supplementary protocol.  
A discrimination verdict contains no duty determination, no material certification and no argument for novelty: a discrimination states only that, under the declared protocol, the output sets of the two hypotheses are disjoint (Tier A) or their output distributions have a provable gap (Tier B); it does not state that either side is novel, irreducible, or holds on real hosts.

### 1.2  Three Matters Kept Apart

(A) The model contains a mechanism: determined, on the declared model, by the text of the law and its proof.  
(B) There is evidence that a material host has the mechanism: this can be given only by experiments or observations on that host. The discrimination theorems do not give this item; Section 7 registers only conditional applications and untested premises.  
(C) The mechanism bears Sedimentation-Field duties on the instance: given by the duty determinations of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts* v1.0, *The Necessity Form of Field-Concretization Qualification* v1.0, *The Mathematical Form of Duty Correspondence in Field Concretization* v1.0 and other papers.  
None of the three automatically implies another: that the model contains a mechanism does not imply that matter has evidence of it; that matter has a mechanism does not imply that the mechanism bears duties; that one model can explain two kinds of host does not mean that both kinds of matter are certified. Where no experiment exists, the conclusions are limited to conditional theory and breachable predictions; no negative verdict is given, and no material certification is issued in advance.

### 1.3  Composition of the Conclusions

　1\. Working domains and readings: W16-E, W16-E″, W16-F; the three grid lemmas of the first-opening reading (Section 3).  
　2\. Comparison environment: the frozen comparison model C16 v0.2 and its four invariants (Proposition 4-1); the contracts and parameter boxes of the versions v0.1, v0.2, v0.3, v0.3″, v0.4 and v0.4-F of the frozen comparison class C\_cmp; Propositions 4-2 to 4-6 (Section 4).  
　3\. The vocabulary of dynamics types Φ1–Φ17 and their subtypes (Section 5).  
　4\. Discrimination theorems: C16 against the fatigue candidate N1 (6.1); outward seepage when full, N2 (6.2); entry Retentive Containment, N3 (6.3); gated layered write, N5 (6.4); goal feedback, N11 (6.5); the blockade's own law, N12 (6.6); share-proportional write under flow division, N13 (6.7); history-modulated write by a shared neuromodulator, N14 (6.8); Structural Position generation when full, N15 (6.9). For each candidate we give the closed law, the distinguishing observation, the proof, the coverage verdict and the breach modes.  
　5\. Master table of instance dynamics types: model instances I-1–I-7, conditional applications C-1–C-4, mechanisms that cannot be separated (Section 7).  
　6\. Bounds of Misjudgment and blind spots, domain of validity, an index of where the 17 candidates went, a summary of breach modes, open items and interfaces (Sections 8–13).  
1.4  Verdicts, Strength and Coverage Grades  
Discrimination verdicts: when the output sets of the two sides under the declared protocol are disjoint, we speak of Tier A discrimination (zero misjudgment under ideal readings; it holds under the grid margins, window reachability and parameter boxes in the hypotheses of each theorem); when, under the declared reading channel, the reading distributions of the two sides have a provable gap and explicit upper bounds on misjudgment of both types are given, we speak of Tier B discrimination. Nothing is issued outside the hypotheses. Where evidence is insufficient, the readings are incompatible with the declaration, or the object lies outside the theorem's domain, ⊥ is recorded in every case; ⊥ does not imply 0, nor any direction; “not studied” is not read as negative.  
Strength: the theorems of this paper are all model conclusions (Strength A); statements about real objects are at most conditional predictions (Strength B) and must list their untested premises; empirical certification (C) is not issued. The appendix programs give only finite verification and exact witnesses, not an exhaustion of all combinations; universal conclusions come only from the proofs in the main text.  
Coverage grades (of a candidate relative to a comparison class):  
　Proven coverage (W-instance layer) — there is an item-by-item mapping under which the candidate and some member of the comparison class have identical readings and states, trial by trial, under every protocol, with the mapping and the equalities proved (and cross-checked by program). This grade is issued only at the W-instance layer; to be raised to “a renaming of a literature mechanism”, every law of the W instance must be aligned item by item with the specific laws of the cited literature, and if the alignment is incomplete the grade stays at the W-instance layer.  
　Structural clue only — the phenomena are the same or the structural names correspond, but no item-by-item equality is proved.  
　Not yet determined — neither of the above holds, or coverage depends on a reading not yet frozen.  
The same law across fields (for example, a hydrological law on a neural host) is registered as “application of a known mechanism type on a new host”, and its conditional predictions are kept.  
“Inexpressible relative to some frozen comparison class” states only that this version of the comparison class lacks the corresponding components, or that its contract does not admit the corresponding combination; it may not be compressed into “unique in reality” or “a new dynamics has been discovered”. Once the corresponding known mechanisms are added to the comparison class, the verdict must be redone, and the new verdict and the original one are each limited to their own versions.  
1.5  Conclusion on the Question of New Dynamics  
Within the scope studied in this paper, no new dynamics has yet been certified. The scope studied means: the instances listed in Section 7; the versions of the frozen comparison model and comparison class in Section 4; the baseline of disciplinary mechanisms listed in 4.2. This is not a proof that “new dynamics does not exist in general”. The candidate closed laws N2, N3, N5, N11, N12, N13, N14, N15 written in this paper, together with their breachable predictions, are all retained, as discrimination samples of dynamics types and as conditional predictions; the coverage verdict for each candidate is given at the end of the respective subsection of Section 6, and the summary in I-6 of Section 7.

2\. Upstream Inputs and Conventions

### 2.1  Citations

Main book *On Self-Manifestation* v0.7.8: Field Chapter 1, *The Mathematical Form of the Field* v2.7 (Definition 5, structural integrity; Definition 12; Definition 16, Retentive Containment; Definition 19, reaching bottom; Definition 20; Definition 21, Edge-Seepage; Definition 23; Definition 26, Tuning; Axioms 2, 7 and 12, and Axiom 16, the Edge-Seepage Axiom; Dτ and Eλ of Equation 2, Equation 3a, and Equation 10, the Edge-Seepage Generation Equation; Laws 5, 17 and 18; Theorem 17); Form Chapter 20, *The General Form of Sedimentation Dynamics* v1.2 (the direction of Eλ); the relevant passages of Inner Chapters 6, 7, 15 and 17 and Outer Chapters 3, 9 and 16 (each noted where it is used).  
Upstream papers of the group (all cited at v1.0): *The General Form of Field Concretization*; *The Mathematical Form of Field Concretization*; *The Reachability Form of Field Concretization* (the protocol levels Π₀, Π₁, Π₂ of Definition 2); *The Construction Form of Field Concretization* (Family A in 4.2, Family E in 4.3); *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts* (Chapter 11; the frozen Instance P, the 𝒦\_g member G\_SNN); *The Mathematical Form of Sedimentation-Dynamics Concretization* (Chapter 12; the four hosts of 2.4, the difference recursion); *The Necessity Form of Field-Concretization Qualification* (Chapter 14; 𝒦\_LS, C\_LS⁺, Family 1ᵍ); *The Mathematical Form of Duty Correspondence in Field Concretization* (Chapter 15; Theorem 15-4 and “mechanisms are not deemed the same because the state equations or the actual runs are the same”); *The Adjudication Form of Material Field Concretization* (Chapter 13).  
The cited conclusions are limited to the v1.0 conclusions of the papers listed above; when instances of other papers are cited, only their frozen laws are used, and duty determinations follow the original papers.

### 2.2  Four Layers Written Separately

Every statement involving a dynamical law is written in four separate layers: (i) the original meaning of the main book (citing only entries of the original text); (ii) the frozen comparison model (C16 v0.2, the versions of C\_cmp and the laws of the candidates, as written in this paper; these are model assumptions); (iii) the quantitative law of an instance (the parameters and functional forms of one specific instance); (iv) the condition domain of a theorem (the hypotheses of each theorem). The four layers do not stand in for one another: the choice of comparison model is not presented as stipulated by the original text, and the quantitative law of an instance is not presented as a universal law.

### 2.3  Notation for Formal Law Terms

This paper follows the notation for formal law terms of *The General Form of Sedimentation Dynamics* v1.2 and of the group's upstream papers, and on the working domain uses only the following six terms:  
　K1 (use-dependent write): passage deepens the sedimentation of the path (Dτ of Equation 2; Axioms 2 and 7).  
　K2 (shallowing): shallowing caused by field-quality, in the decreasing direction (Eλ of Equation 2; *The General Form of Sedimentation Dynamics* v1.2); the original text gives no functional form.  
　K3 (reaching bottom): what happens when sedimentation reaches domain capacity (Definition 19 only judges “bottom reached”; Outer Chapter 3, “it must first fill fa, and only thereafter seep into the two sides”, gives the direction of outward seepage; the original text gives no adjacency relation, conserved quantity or distribution ratio).  
　K6 (threshold order): among comparable structures of the same kind, the threshold strictly decreases with sedimentation depth, and the deeper flows first (Equation 3a, Law 5, Theorem 17).  
　K7b (selective bias, tuning, Coloring): the objects of tuning in Definition 26 are the current flow-priority order, the display level of sedimentation depth and the Domain Boundary Thresholds, and tuning deletes no existing Trace-Response; selective bias and Coloring have only their original meanings in the main book (Inner Chapter 15, Outer Chapter 9).  
　K11 (Retentive Containment): lag, remaining, accumulation; accumulate first, emit afterwards (Definition 16, Axiom 12, Inner Chapter 6); no law is established.  
K\_F denotes the frozen formal law composed of K1, K2, K3 and K6 on the working domain (that is, C16 v0.2 of 4.1).

### 2.4  Four Branch Points

(1) K3 (where the surplus goes when full): C16 v0.2 takes “discard the increment when full”; this is a model choice, not a stipulation of the original text. Instances with outward seepage (N2, fill-and-spill FS, the store of Family A) state separately where the surplus goes and their conservation antecedents.  
(2) K7b (tuning): that tuning deletes no Trace-Response does not mean it leaves future sedimentation unchanged — changing the flow direction and the number of passages indirectly changes subsequent writes; this is a different claim from directly changing the plasticity coefficient or selecting the layer written. Every model that uses direct write gating or rate modulation (N5, N11, N14) declares its law and antecedents model by model, and does not announce that the original text uniquely implies them.  
(3) K11 (Retentive Containment): h ≡ 0 in C16 v0.2 is only a simplifying branch that does not represent Retentive Containment; it is not presented as the original text stipulating that Retentive Containment has no dynamics. Instances with Retentive Containment (N3, LIF, the gating membrane potential of s1, the store of Family A) state their rules of accumulation, leakage, release and reset; the ∅-absorption of one branch is not extended to the whole family.  
(4) K2 (shallowing): C16 v0.2 takes a constant rate (a model special case); the quantity meant by “hard to change” in Outer Chapter 16, “deep sediment is hard to change, shallow is easy to move”, is undetermined. The proofs concerning exponential sums and the sign of the eventual outcome keep the constant-rate antecedent and are not extended to λ(d).  
Wherever this paper says “a candidate is not in K\_F”, it always means not in the frozen C16 v0.2 (K3 clip-and-discard, K7b following only the current reading of Definition 26, K11 with h ≡ 0, K2 at a constant rate). Branch-dependent cases are written separately: N2 is the completion of C16 when K3 takes conserving outward seepage; N3 is the completion of C16 when K11 takes the N3 law; N5 and N11 are partly or wholly absorbed when K7b allows tuning to control writes; the “deep sediment is hard to change” of Outer Chapter 16 is a parameter change when K2 takes a depth-dependent function. The choice among these branches is undetermined.

3\. Working Domain, Protocols and Readings  
3.1  Definition 3-1 (Working Domain W16-E v0.1)  
Object: a single entry e and two paths a, b that share no Structural Position. A theoretical model; no material realization is required.  
Input of one trial (input side; Π₀ of *The Reachability Form of Field Concretization* v1.0): peak entry flow pressure P \> 0; reinforcement input u ∈ {0, 1}; presentation set Pres ⊆ {a, b} (a path not presented is not open in this trial, and its state does not change through opening); r ≥ 0 rest beats after the trial. There may also be a structural blockade Brk\_x ∈ {0, 1} (set or lifted by the protocol at the start of a trial; Π₂).  
Entry-flow-pressure grid: p\_t = h + t·P/T (t = 0, 1, …, T), where h ≥ 0 is the member's entry store (h ≡ 0 for members without a K11 law) and T is the reading resolution.  
First-opening step: τ\_x = min{t : x ∈ Pres, Brk\_x = 0, p\_t ≥ θ\_x}, and τ\_x = ∞ if there is no such t (recorded as None in the programs). Stop convention: if min τ is finite, Y = {x : τ\_x = min τ}; otherwise Y = ∅. Each path in Y records one passage.  
Reading: (Y, τ\_min) trial by trial, written {a}@k and so on.  
Parameters and states are rational, windows are finite, and inputs are deterministic.  
Input character of the presentation set: the presentation set is an input operation only on hosts with path-separate inputs (two odors, two independent input fibres, two distributary channels that can be supplied with water separately). On hosts where the two paths share a single entry, “present only a” and “block b for one trial” give the same readings trial by trial, and belong to Π₂.  
3.2  Definition 3-2 (Multi-Arm Protocols and Reading Channels)  
Multi-arm protocol: several independent runs of the same member from the same declared initial state, each under one protocol, with their readings compared. For real objects it corresponds to “several groups of objects with the same initial conditions” or to “resettable to the declared initial state”, which is an untested premise of conditional application.  
Reading channel: Tier A takes ideal readings (the report is the true value). Tier B declares the channel class R\_ε: the rewrite events of the trials are mutually independent, each with probability at most ε; when a trial is rewritten, the reported value may be anything (it may depend on history, and may take the value of the other side); when it is not rewritten, the report is faithful; both sides use the same random kernel, and the channel does not read the identity of the candidate.  
3.3  Definition 3-3 (W16-E″) and Definition 3-4 (W16-F)  
W16-E″: W16-E plus an affective input e ∈ {−1, 0, +1} (dislike, none, like; declared each trial; input side, Π₀). W16-E″ has only one image (the input pattern of the entry).  
W16-F: W16-E plus one latent Structural Position ℓ on the edge of a. Before it is generated ℓ does not exist; once generated it is a path with its own depth and blockade flag, presented if and only if a is presented (ℓ lies on the domain edge of a, and only flow entering a can reach it); readings follow W16-E, and the first-opened set may contain ℓ. A protocol may block a for a single trial (Π₂).

### 3.4  Grid Lemmas

Lemma 3-1 (closed form and monotonicity of the first-opening step): when h \< θ\_x and x can open, τ\_x = ⌈(θ\_x − h)T/P⌉, reachable when τ\_x ≤ T and otherwise τ\_x = ∞; when h ≥ θ\_x, τ\_x = 0. τ\_x is monotone nondecreasing in θ\_x and monotone nonincreasing in h and P.  
Proof: p\_t ≥ θ\_x if and only if t ≥ (θ\_x − h)T/P; take the least nonnegative integer. Monotonicity follows from that of the ceiling function. ∎  
Lemma 3-2 (a threshold gap exceeding the grid step makes the lower-threshold path lead alone): suppose both paths can open in this trial, h \< θ\_ℓ \< θ\_ℏ, θ\_ℏ − θ\_ℓ \> P/T, and τ\_ℓ is reachable. Then Y = {ℓ}.  
Proof: when h \< θ\_ℓ, τ\_ℓ ≥ 1, and τ\_ℓ is the first step with p\_t ≥ θ\_ℓ, so p(τ\_ℓ − 1) \< θ\_ℓ and p(τ\_ℓ) = p(τ\_ℓ − 1) + P/T \< θ\_ℓ + P/T \< θ\_ℏ. Hence ℏ has not reached its threshold at τ\_ℓ, τ\_ℏ \> τ\_ℓ, and Y = {ℓ}. ∎  
(This lemma is the grid form of the argument of Theorem 17.)  
Lemma 3-3 (ceiling difference): for real numbers x, y ≥ 0, ⌈x + y⌉ ≥ ⌈x⌉ + ⌊y⌋; hence ⌈x⌉ − ⌈x − y⌉ ≥ ⌊y⌋. When y ≥ 1, two ceilings whose arguments differ by y are strictly different. ∎  
3.5  Declaration Format of Discrimination Tasks  
Each discrimination theorem is declared under the following five items: (1) the candidate pair — Candidate A (a declared subclass of a candidate closed law, with its parameter box) and Candidate B (some version of the frozen comparison model or comparison class, with its parameter box and contract); (2) the distinguishing observation — the protocol (for a multi-arm protocol, each arm written out) and the reading; (3) the proof of validity — a characterization of the output sets of the two sides and of their disjointness; (4) nonemptiness of both sides — one member given for each; (5) breach modes. Closure criterion for candidates: a candidate must have a positively written law and a forward relation computable trial by trial; it does not cite outputs of the comparison class, does not take “not belonging to some comparison class” as its content, and invokes no higher-level qualification and no names such as “new” or “known”.

4\. Comparison Environment  
4.1  Definition 4-1 (C16 v0.2, the Frozen K\_F Comparison Model)  
Members: (g0\_x, c\_x, α\_x, λ\_x, D̄\_x), x ∈ {a, b}; 0 \< g0 ≤ 1, c \> 0, α ≥ 0, λ ∈ (0, 1\], D̄ ∈ ℚ\>0 ∪ {∞}. State d\_x ∈ \[0, D̄\_x\], h ≡ 0.  
Laws: threshold θ\_x = g\_x(d\_x) = g0\_x/(1 + c\_x·d\_x); on passage d\_x ← min(d\_x + α\_x, D̄\_x); on each rest beat d\_x ← (1 − λ\_x)d\_x; u is not read.  
Composition: K1, K2, K3 (clip-and-discard) and K6 are combined by c1 (component-wise superposition on the same Structural Position: Dτ and Eλ) and c2 (gating: Dτ is gated by the opening event of K6), with depth ≤ 2; c3 (parallel Trace-Responses) and c4 (event switching) are not included.  
Proposition 4-1 (the four invariants of C16 v0.2)  
　(I1) For two adjacent trials k and k + 1 with zero rest between them, the same P, both paths presented and no blockade: Y\_k = {x} ⇒ Y\_{k+1} = {x}.  
　(I2) P ≥ 1 and some path presented and not blocked ⇒ Y ≠ ∅.  
　(∅-absorption) From some ∅ trial on, if every trial has the same P, both paths presented and no blockade (rest arbitrary) ⇒ all subsequent trials are ∅.  
　(u-independence) The reading sequence does not depend on u.  
Proof: (I2) θ\_x ≤ g0\_x ≤ 1 ≤ P = p\_T, so a presented path has τ\_x ≤ T. (I1) The only change between the two trials is the passage of x: d\_x does not decrease (clipping does not make it decrease), so θ\_x does not rise; the other path did not pass and there is no rest, so θ\_o is unchanged; with h = 0, Lemma 3-1 gives τ\_x(k+1) ≤ τ\_x(k) \< τ\_o(k) = τ\_o(k+1). (∅-absorption) A ∅ trial has no passage; rest only decreases d and raises θ; with P unchanged τ does not fall, and still no path reaches its threshold. (u-independence) u does not occur in the laws. ∎  
Verification: Appendix B.2 R1 (3000 random protocols; none of the three is violated).  
Reading: each of the four invariants is an exclusionary reading for a class of dynamics — “the sole lead changes with zero rest” excludes C16 v0.2 (indicating outward seepage when full Φ3b, cross-path coupling Φ8, use-induced threshold rise Φ6, etc.); “∅ appears with P ≥ 1” excludes it (indicating Φ6); “opening again after ∅ under constant pressure” excludes it (indicating Retentive Containment Φ4a); “the readings change with u” excludes it (indicating Φ9, Φ10, Φ16).  
4.2  Definition 4-2 (Components of the Disciplinary Mechanism Baseline on W16-E)  
Each component is given in four parts: “state variables, threshold term, update after passage, rest law”; the threshold term may only be a function of the component's own state; an unwritten rest law means “unchanged”. The literature relied on and the level of alignment are noted with each item; any item marked “W reading” is a trial-based instance written on W from the textual content of the literature, and does not claim equation-by-equation transcription.  
　N1 (fatigue type of spike-frequency adaptation and inhibition): θ\_x = g(d\_x) + f\_x; on passage f\_x += φ (bounded variant N1b: f\_x ← min(f\_x + φ, f\_cap)); at rest f ← (1 − μ)f.  
　STF (short-term facilitation): θ\_x = g(d\_x)(1 − F); after every trial with a presentation F ← F + κ\_F(F̄ − F); at rest F ← (1 − μ\_F)F; opening does not reset it.  
　HOM (time-based simplification of homeostatic plasticity): θ\_x = g(d\_x)(1 − H\_x); on a rest beat H\_x ← H\_x + κ\_H(H̄ − H\_x); on passage H\_x ← (1 − ν)H\_x.  
　HS (inter-synaptic crosstalk, paired with K1): when x passes, o unconditionally gets d\_o += κ (clipped at D̄\_o).  
　HSsat (c4 combination: switching to crosstalk at the clipping event of x): if x is already full before passage, α is credited to o; otherwise x itself deepens; at the crossing step the excess is not split.  
　TF3 (three-factor learning rule): on passage with u = 1, d += α; otherwise unchanged; shallowing at rest.  
　RW (Rescorla–Wagner rule): on passage d ← d + η(u·V̄ − d).  
　TWO (two-timescale): d = d\_f + d\_s; on passage, with error ε = u·V̄ − d, d\_f += B\_f·ε and d\_s += B\_s·ε; at rest the two shallow at l\_f and l\_s respectively.  
　FS (fill-and-spill with first-opening competition; independent implementation): storage S\_x ≤ Cap\_x; the inflow V goes to the first path to open, and the excess after filling spills into the other path; leakage rate leak; outflow threshold g(S). Alignment: qualitative alignment with two items of Tromp-van Meerveld and McDonnell 2006, “water spills once its level reaches the edge of the depression” and “before filling, water is only stored, not released”; the paper has no storage equation, and spill into a competing path of the same entry has no counterpart there.  
　LIF (entry leaky integrate-and-fire; independent implementation): the entry membrane potential V serves as the bias h shared by the two paths; without firing, V ← ρ\_V(V + P); on firing (either path opens), V ← 0; at rest V ← (1 − μ\_V)V. Alignment: the linear summation, leak, fixed threshold and reset of Burkitt 2006 match item by item; the absence of leak within a trial is an approximation; first-arrival allocation between the two outlets is a combination on W.  
　IFB (integral feedback): one variable m ∈ \[0, m̄\] per path; in each trial m ← clip(m + k\_R(1 − a) − k\_B·a, 0, m̄), where a ∈ {0, 1} is the declared “achieved” signal. Alignment: follows the standard formulation of Barkai–Leibler-type integral feedback; no equation-by-equation alignment with the original paper is established.  
Not instantiated on W (reason: the cited literature has no law that can be aligned equation by equation, or only a bibliographic record is available): synaptic tagging and capture; the recurrent dopamine circuit of Huang et al. in *Drosophila* (its equations are in a supplementary appendix and were not included in the alignment); and several non-neural entries.  
Two identities are kept apart: (1) explicit model member of a formal comparison class — a comparison class admits only components whose laws have been written out item by item on W (the items above and those listed in 4.4–4.6), whether their source is literature equations, the qualitative content of the literature, or this paper's W readings; (2) literature identity as a verified published mechanism — conferred only when the laws of the component are aligned item by item with specific equations of the cited literature, and limited to the level of alignment noted with each item (for example, KIR is partly aligned equation by equation with Tero et al. 2007, LIF is partly aligned item by item with Burkitt 2006, IZH is partly of the same form as Izhikevich 2007). FS, IFB and DAM, and VST, BCM, TON, PIT, MSP and the others written from qualitative content or standard formulations, are explicit model members on W; they do not acquire literature identity by being admitted to a comparison class, and any coverage given through them is issued only at the W-instance layer. Umbrella names such as “metaplasticity” and “heterosynaptic plasticity” are not members (otherwise every “history-gated write” would be covered by definition, and the comparison would lose its content).

### 4.3  Definition 4-D1 (C\_cmp v0.1)

D1.1  Component library L₁ (11 items): the C16 v0.2 core (K1, K2, K3, K6 as one unit); N1, STF, HOM, HS, TF3, RW, TWO, FS, LIF, IFB (4.2).  
D1.2  Composition trees: the leaves are components of L₁ (the same component may occur several times), and each internal node is one of the following operators — c1 (threshold contributions or depth contributions on the same path are added, θ = Σ of the threshold terms of the components, truncated to ≥ 0); c2 (a coefficient of one component is multiplied by a gating function φ(z), where z is the state of another component or the value of a declared channel); c3 (parallel Trace-Responses: an additional depth variable, whose sign of action must be taken from the component's own law; K\_F components may only lower thresholds); c4 (the law of a component is switched at a declared event). The depth is the number of operator nodes on the longest root-to-leaf path; the declared scope is depth ≤ 2 and at most 4 leaves. The limits on depth and number of leaves are the declared scope of comparison, not a bound on novelty; any candidate expressible by deeper compositions or by feedback common in the disciplines must still be registered.  
　Admissible gating functions: the threshold indicator functions 1\[z ≥ σ\], 1\[z \< σ\] (σ rational, within the declared box) and bounded rational affine functions a + b·z truncated to \[0, 1\] (\|a\|, \|b\| ≤ the declared upper bound); no other function is admissible.  
D1.3  Parameter boxes (closed rational intervals, declared before comparison): g0 ∈ \[1/2, 1\], c ∈ \[1/2, 2\], α ∈ \[0, 1\], λ ∈ \[1/12, 1\], D̄ ∈ \[1, 4\] ∪ {∞}; N1: φ, μ ∈ \[0, 1\], f\_cap ∈ \[0, 1\] ∪ {∞}; STF: κ\_F, μ\_F ∈ \[0, 1\], F̄ ∈ \[0, 11/12\]; HOM: κ\_H, ν ∈ \[0, 1\], H̄ ∈ \[0, 11/12\]; HS: κ ∈ \[0, 1\]; RW: η ∈ \[0, 1\], V̄ ∈ \[0, 3\]; TWO: B\_f, B\_s ∈ \[0, 1/2\], l\_f, l\_s ∈ \[0, 1\], V̄ ∈ \[0, 3\]; FS: V ∈ \[0, 1\], Cap ∈ \[1, 4\], leak ∈ \[1/12, 1\]; LIF: ρ\_V, μ\_V ∈ \[0, 1\]; IFB: k\_R, k\_B ∈ \[0, 1\], m̄ ∈ \[0, 1\]; for c2 gating, the threshold σ and the affine coefficients \|a\|, \|b\| ≤ 4. The random values in the appendix programs are only samples from these boxes and do not represent the contract. The proofs of Lemmas N12-L1 and N12-L2 do not use the endpoints of the boxes and hold for all parameters in the domain of each law.  
D1.4  Execution semantics (event-driven with a global Y), in each trial: ① Threshold: the threshold θ\_x of each presented and unblocked path is composed from the threshold terms of the leaves according to the tree, reading only the pre-trial state (not this trial's P, u, Pres, z); the entry store (h of N3, V of LIF) enters the grid as the bias h shared by both paths. ② First opening: τ\_x and Y are uniquely given by the grid and the stop convention; Y is the unique opening event shared by all components — no component may set up a threshold event, local opening or local non-opening of its own, nor integrate over P separately when Y is nonempty. ③ Update after passage: in the fixed order “own-position deepening → spill → entry store → gating components”, each component reads only Y, u, Pres, z (intention input), the events in the event set E and the pre-trial state; the only update that reads P is the accumulation of LIF when Y = ∅; gating functions read neither P nor τ. ④ Rest beats: each component follows its own rest law.  
D1.5  Event set E and timing: opening and non-opening (after ②); reaching bottom (clipping takes effect in ③); rest beat (④); u = 1; presentation; setting and lifting of a blockade (before ①, given only by the protocol). The switching events of c4 are taken from E, and a switch takes effect after the step at which its event occurs.  
D1.6  Two readings: the strict reading — the gating variables of c2 are taken only from the declared channels {u, Y, Pres, z}, and c4 takes only events of the switched component itself; the broad reading — gating variables may be the state of any component, and c4 may take any event in E. Both readings are constrained by D1.4.  
Proposition 4-2 (a sufficient condition for expressibility by composition): under the broad reading, if the state of a candidate N can be split into (z, w), where z evolves autonomously by the law of some component ℓ₁ in L (taking inputs only from the channels of W) and w evolves by the law of some component ℓ₂ in L with coefficients that are admissible gating functions of z, then N belongs to c2(ℓ₁, ℓ₂), of depth 2; if N follows ℓ\_A before some event in E and ℓ\_B after it, then N belongs to c4(ℓ\_A, ℓ\_B).  
Proof: by construction from the definitions. ∎  
Reading: this is only a sufficient condition for expressibility by composition, not a necessary one; it cannot be used to infer that whatever is not covered can lie only in a few classes of structure.

### 4.4  Definition 4-D2 (C\_cmp v0.2)

D2.1  Component library L₂ = L₁ ∪ {VST, BCM, CMP, DAM, PF, ERO, DIS, THR} (states per path):  
　VST (LTP branch of voltage-based STDP, Clopath type; W reading): a low-pass drive ū\_x per path; after a trial in which x is presented, ū\_x ← (1 − μ\_u)ū\_x + μ\_u·P and d\_x ← clip(d\_x + γ\_x·A\_p·\[P − θ₊\]₊·\[ū\_x − θ₋\]₊); at rest ū ← (1 − μ\_u)ū. Instantaneous membrane potential ↔ this trial's drive P, low-pass membrane potential ↔ ū, presynaptic trace ↔ “presented”; γ\_x ≡ 1 (ungated type), or γ\_x = 1\[Brk\_x = 1 at the start of this trial\] (gated by Brk, c2). The LTD branch is MINUS (Φ1b in Section 5) and is not admitted separately.  
　BCM: y\_x = P·1\[x ∈ Y\]; d\_x ← clip(max(0, d\_x + η·y\_x(y\_x − θ\_M,x))); θ\_M,x ← (1 − μ\_M)θ\_M,x + μ\_M·y\_x²; at rest θ\_M ← (1 − μ\_M)θ\_M.  
　CMP (resource competition, Triesch type): when x ∈ Y and o ∉ Y, d\_o ← max(0, d\_o − κ\_c).  
　PF (pressure failure): an accumulator H\_x per path; after a trial in which x is blocked and presented, H\_x ← H\_x + ρ\_H·P; at the start of a trial, if Brk\_x = 1 and H\_x ≥ B̄\_x, the blockade releases itself: Brk\_x ← 0, H\_x ← 0; at rest H ← (1 − μ\_H)H.  
　ERO (erosional breakthrough, piping type): a counter n\_x per path; after a trial in which x is blocked and presented, n\_x ← n\_x + 1; at the start of a trial, if Brk\_x = 1 and n\_x ≥ K\_b, self-release, with n\_x ← 0.  
　DIS (disinhibition): at the start of a trial, if Brk\_x = 1 and u = 1 in this trial, self-release (u is read as a disinhibition signal).  
　THR (overload release): at the start of a trial, if Brk\_x = 1 and P ≥ P\_rel in this trial, self-release (nothing accumulates; only the current drive is read).  
　DAM (dam failure and overtopping scour): the accumulation and self-release of PF, plus, at any release (self-release or lifting by the protocol), first a scour d\_x ← clip(d\_x + κ\_s·H\_x) and then H\_x ← 0. DAM is a trial-based law written from the qualitative content of dam-failure geomorphology (peak discharge increases with storage volume and head; a single flood incises a channel rapidly; Walder and O'Connor 1997, Lamb and Fonstad 2010, from the bibliographic records); it is not aligned with any literature equation and is not called the standard formulation of this mechanism.  
D2.2  Composition trees and depth: as in D1.2, with leaves from L₂; the declared scope is still depth ≤ 2 and at most 4 leaves.  
D2.3  Parameter boxes: the L₁ part as in D1.3; A\_p ∈ \[0, 1\], θ₊, θ₋ ∈ \[0, 1\], μ\_u ∈ \[1/12, 1\]; η ∈ \[0, 1/2\], μ\_M ∈ \[1/12, 1\]; κ\_c ∈ \[0, 1\]; B̄ ∈ ℚ\>0 ∪ {∞}, ρ\_H ∈ \[0, 1\], μ\_H ∈ \[0, 1\], κ\_s ∈ \[0, 2\]; K\_b ∈ {1, …, 8}; P\_rel ∈ \[1, 3\]. B̄ has no upper bound (a subclass in Theorem N12-4 uses large B̄); the equality of Theorem N12-5 holds for all parameter values, and the choice of box is not load-bearing.  
D2.4  Execution semantics: D1.4 applies, with the addition of ①′, setting, lifting and self-release of blockades — at the start of a trial the settings of the protocol are executed first; then, for each path with Brk\_x = 1, the following are checked in turn: lifting by the protocol, the pressure condition of PF/DAM, the count condition of ERO, the disinhibition condition of DIS, the overload condition of THR; if any of them holds, the path is released (DAM scours at the same time), and this is recorded as an event in E; only then does ① compute thresholds. Brk thereby becomes a declared channel: under the strict reading the gating variables of c2 gain 1\[Brk\_x\] (its value read after ①′ and before ②). The fixed order in ③: own-position deepening (K1/K3) → CMP → VST → BCM → accumulation of PF/DAM/ERO. There is still only one opening event, the global Y; self-release is a write to Brk, not a local opening.  
D2.5  Event set E₂ = E ∪ {pressure release, erosion release, disinhibition release, overload release}, all occurring in ①′ and taking effect before ①.  
D2.6  The two readings are as in D1.6; the declared channels of the strict reading are {u, Y, Pres, z, Brk}.  
Proposition 4-4 (I\_B and I\_PD no longer hold in v0.2; for the definitions of I\_B and I\_PD see Lemmas N12-L1 and N12-L2 in 6.6): (1) PF, ERO, DIS, THR and DAM each make a blocked path self-release and open while the protocol has not lifted it, so I\_B does not hold. (2) VST, BCM and DAM each read P during a blockade period with Y ≠ ∅, so I\_PD does not hold.  
Proof: witnesses are constructed by the laws of D2.1. I\_B — PF (B̄ = 2, ρ\_H = 1/2) is pressure-released at trial 5, ERO (K\_b = 3) is erosion-released at trial 4, DIS is disinhibition-released at trial 5 with u = 1, THR (P\_rel = 3/2) is overload-released at trial 5 with P = 2, DAM (B̄ = 1) is released at trial 3; in each case, when only a is presented afterwards, Y = {a}. I\_PD — P = 1 versus P = 5 during the blockade period (m = 4, the blockade period being {b}^4 in both arms); after release, with only a presented, the first-opening step is 355 versus 45 for VST and 200 versus 67 for DAM; in the release trial with both paths presented, BCM gives {b} versus {a, b} and DAM gives {a, b} versus {a}. ∎ (Appendix B.5 E2)  
Proposition 4-5 (in v0.1 and v0.2 thresholds are computed only from the pre-trial state): for any member (under both the strict and the broad reading, of any depth and number of leaves), in any trial the threshold θ\_x of each path is a function of the pre-trial state (after the blockade writes of ①′) and does not read this trial's Pres; the two paths share the same bias h; τ\_x is the first-arrival step on the grid and is monotone nondecreasing in θ\_x.  
Proof: Definition 4-2 (4.2) stipulates that threshold terms are only functions of component states; c1 adds; the gating of c2 acts on updates in ③ and in ① reads only the pre-trial state; the Trace-Responses of c3 are written into the state; the switches of c4 are taken from events that have already occurred; ①′ writes only Brk, the accumulation of PF/DAM/ERO and the scour of DAM, all completed before ①, and its conditions read only the state, u (DIS) and P (THR), not Pres. ∎  
This property does not depend on the depth limit; Theorem N13-4 rests on it.  
4.5  Definition 4-D3 (C\_cmp v0.3) and Definition 4-D3″ (C\_cmp v0.3″)  
D3.1  Component library L₃ = L₂ ∪ {KIR, DN}:  
　KIR (reinforcement under parallel flow division; W instance of Tero, Kobayashi and Nakagaki 2007): pool Π = the paths presented and unblocked in this trial (after ①′); conductance D\_x = (1 + d\_x)^ν; share s\_x = D\_x/Σ\_{y∈Π} D\_y; opening read-out: x opens at the first grid step with s\_x·p\_t ≥ θ₀ (equivalent threshold term θ₀/s\_x); reinforcement: every path in the pool gets d\_x += α·s\_x^μ; at rest d ← (1 − λ)d.  
　DN (threshold term of divisive normalization): R\_x = D\_x^n/(σ^n + Σ\_{y∈Π} D\_y^n), D\_x = 1 + d\_x; threshold term θ\_N·(1/R\_x − 1) (when σ = 0 this term is 0 if a single path is presented); this component has no write law. Its c1 composition with the C16 core is denoted DNC.  
　When μ = 1, the sum of the increments in the pool is α in every trial; that is, KIR distributes a fixed amount of resource by shares. One form of competitive resource allocation is thus already contained in KIR, and no separate component is set up for it.  
D3.2  Composition trees and depth: as in D1.2, with leaves from L₃; members are not required to contain the C16 core (KIR carries its own opening read-out and write law).  
D3.3  Parameter boxes: the L₂ part as in D2.3; KIR: ν ∈ {1, 2, 3, 4}, θ₀ ∈ \[1/12, 1/2\], α ∈ \[0, 1\], μ ∈ {1, 2}, λ ∈ \[1/12, 1\]; DN: n ∈ {1, 2, 3, 4}, σ ∈ \[0, 1\], θ\_N ∈ \[0, 1/2\].  
D3.4  Execution semantics: D2.4 applies, with only ① changed — thresholds or opening conditions may, through KIR and DN, read this trial's pool Π and the pre-trial states of the paths in the pool; the threshold terms of the other components still read only the pre-trial state. ② is still the globally unique first-opened set; in ③ the reinforcement of KIR comes after own-position deepening and before CMP; KIR does not read P (the entry flow is normalized), and DN writes nothing.  
D3.5  The two readings are as in D2.6; gating the reinforcement of KIR by 1\[x ∈ Y\] (c2) is a valid composition under the strict reading.  
D3.6  Identities outside the parameter boxes are kept apart from membership within the boxes: every equality of the form “candidate ≡ some construction” (Theorems N12-5, N13-5, N14-5) holds for all parameter values; only a construction whose parameters lie in the respective boxes is a member of the fixed C\_cmp. For parameters outside the boxes one may say that the equality still holds, but not that the construction outside the boxes belongs to the fixed C\_cmp.  
Literature alignment (KIR): Eq. (1) of Tero et al. 2007, Q\_ij = D\_ij(p\_i − p\_j)/L\_ij, together with node conservation and a constant source–sink flow I₀, gives for two parallel tubes of equal length the flux Q\_x = I₀·D\_x/Σ D\_y, identical equation by equation with s\_x (I₀ normalized); ν = 1 is read as D = 1 + d, ν = 4 reads d as the relative increment of the tube radius, ν = 2 as the relative increment of the cross-sectional area, and ν = 3 is only a parameter extension; Eq. (4), dD/dt = f(\|Q\|) − rD with f(Q) = Q^μ, coincides equation by equation with d\_x += α·s\_x^μ as one Euler step only when ν = 1. What does not match: in the paper the decay acts continuously on the whole conductance, while KIR decays the adapted part only on rest beats; the paper has no threshold read-out; the paper reinforces every tube that carries flux.  
Literature alignment (DN): R\_j = γ·D\_j^n/(σ^n + Σ\_k D\_k^n) of Carandini and Heeger 2012 is cited in its standard formulation; no equation-by-equation alignment is established, only structural alignment; the drive D\_x = 1 + d\_x and the threshold term θ\_N(1/R − 1) are readings on W.  
Definition 4-D3″ (C\_cmp v0.3″, on W16-E″): v0.3 plus e as a declared channel of the strict reading (the gating variables of c2 may take this trial's e), together with two components that read e: TF3e (signed reading of the three-factor rule: on passage d\_x += α\_e·e, clipped to ≥ 0) and RWe (signed reading of the RW rule: on passage d\_x ← d\_x + η(e·V̄ − d\_x), clipped to ≥ 0); parameter boxes α\_e, η ∈ \[0, 1\], V̄ ∈ \[0, 2\]. The other components of L₃ do not read e.  
Proposition 4-6 (Proposition 4-5 no longer holds in v0.3): (1) the thresholds of KIR and DN read this trial's pool, so “thresholds are independent of this trial's presentation set” does not hold; witness: in state (1, 1), KIR gives τ = 100 when only b is presented and τ\_min = 200 when both paths are presented (Appendix B.6 F2). (2) KIR and DN neither write Brk nor read P, and add no new violation of I\_B or I\_PD. ∎  
4.6  Definition 4-D4 (C\_cmp v0.4, on W16-E″) and Definition 4-D4-F (C\_cmp v0.4-F, on W16-F)  
D4.1  Component library L₄ = L₃″ ∪ {IZH, IZHc, TON, PIT}. A same-beat write gated by e is c2(C16 core, 1\[e = +1\]) or TF3e, and is already in v0.3″.  
　IZH (W instance of the dopamine-modulated STDP of Izhikevich 2007, with immediate eligibility): a shared neuromodulator level D (attached to the image); after a trial with an opening, D ← clip(D + ε\_D·e, −D̄, D̄) (D is read as the deviation from baseline and can be negative); eligibility c\_x = 1\[x ∈ Y\]; write d\_x += α\_I·c\_x·D (reading the pre-trial D); D decays only on rest beats, D ← (1 − μ\_D)D.  
　IZHc (a variant closer to the same paper): eligibility trace c\_x ← (1 − μ\_el)c\_x + 1\[x ∈ Y\], with the write d\_x += α\_I·c\_x·D applied to every path with c\_x \> 0; D is additionally multiplied by (1 − μ\_D) at the end of each trial, and likewise on rest beats.  
　TON (W reading of tonic dopamine; Niv, Daw, Joel and Dayan 2007, from the bibliographic record): a shared average reward R ← R + η\_R(\[e\]₊ − R) (after a trial with an opening); it acts on performance: the threshold of each path is multiplied by (1 − ψ\_T·R) (clipped to \[0, 1\]), and writes are not changed.  
　PIT (W reading of Pavlovian–instrumental transfer; Holmes, Marchand and Coutureau 2010, from the bibliographic record): a shared image value V ← V + η\_V(e − V); it acts on performance: thresholds are multiplied by (1 − ψ\_V·V), and writes are not changed.  
D4.2  Composition trees: as in D3.2; when c1 composes depth increments, the sum is clipped once to \[0, D̄\].  
D4.3  Parameter boxes: α\_I ∈ \[0, 2\], ε\_D ∈ \[0, 1\], D̄ ∈ \[1/2, 3\], μ\_D ∈ \[0, 1\], μ\_el ∈ \[1/12, 1\]; η\_R, η\_V ∈ \[0, 1\], ψ\_T, ψ\_V ∈ \[0, 1/2\].  
D4.4  Execution semantics: D3.4 applies; in ① the threshold factors of TON and PIT read the pre-trial R and V; in ③ the write of IZH comes after own-position deepening, and the updates of D, R and V come at the end of ③ (after the writes); rest beats follow each law.  
D4.5  e is a declared channel of the strict reading.  
Literature alignment (IZH): Izhikevich 2007 (Cereb Cortex 17: 2443–2452), Eqs. (1)–(3): eligibility trace ċ = −c/τ\_c + STDP increment (τ\_c = 1 s), weight ṡ = c·d, dopamine concentration ḋ = −d/τ\_d + DA(t) (τ\_d = 0.2 s), where d is the same for all synapses and c and s differ synapse by synapse. The write equation has the same form as Δd\_x = α\_I·c\_x·D; the leaky integration of dopamine with a jump at reward has the same form as the release-plus-decay of D. What does not match: in the paper d is a nonnegative concentration, while the D of IZH can be negative; the paper has no clipping at ±D̄; release only in trials with an opening is a W reading; the D of IZH decays only on rest beats and eligibility is immediate, these two items being aligned with the law of N14; in the paper eligibility arises from STDP pairing, which is replaced here by “passage”.  
Definition 4-D4-F: v0.4-F = the lifting of v0.4 to W16-F (the existing components generate no Structural Position, and ℓ never exists) plus the following three generating components:  
　GNG (Growing Neural Gas of Fritzke 1995; original algorithm: each input adds the squared distance between the nearest unit and the input to that unit's error counter; every λ inputs, a new unit is inserted between the unit q with the largest error and its neighbor f with the largest error, with its weight at their midpoint; the errors of q and f are multiplied by α, and the error of the new unit takes the new value of q; all errors are multiplied by d at every step). W reading: when x passes, err\_x += θ\_x² (the pre-trial threshold read as mismatch); every λ trials with a presentation, if the largest error belongs to a and err\_a \> 0, ℓ is inserted with d\_ℓ = (d\_a + d\_b)/2, everything else following the paper.  
　GWR (the Grow When Required network of Marsland, Shapiro and Nehmzow 2002; original: activity a = exp(−‖x − w\_s‖); if a \< a\_T and the firing counter of the best unit h\_s \< h\_T, a new unit is inserted between the best and second-best units; the firing counter decreases with firing according to a habituation equation). W reading: the firing counter h of a (initial value 1) is updated when a passes, h ← max(0, h + (α\_h(1 − h) − 1)/τ\_h); “mismatch” is read as “entering although already full before passage”; on mismatch with h \< h\_T, ℓ is inserted with d\_ℓ = d\_a/2.  
　MSP (the structural plasticity of Butz and van Ooyen 2013; the growth curve in the standard linear formulation, with no equation-by-equation alignment to the original equations established): the activity of a, A ← (1 − μ\_A)A + μ\_A·1\[a ∈ Y\]; the free structural elements on the edge of a, z ← max(0, z + ν\_M(1 − A/ε\_M)); ℓ is generated when z ≥ 1, with d\_ℓ = 0.  
　Parameter boxes: λ\_G ∈ {1, …, 8}, α\_G ∈ \[1/4, 3/4\], d\_G ∈ \[9/10, 1\]; h\_T ∈ \[1/12, 11/12\], τ\_h ∈ \[1, 8\], α\_h ∈ \[1/12, 1\]; ν\_M ∈ \[1/12, 1/2\], μ\_A ∈ \[1/4, 1\], ε\_M ∈ \[1/4, 3/4\].  
　Not instantiated: river crevasse splays and avulsion (overbank flow after bankfull, with a crevasse gradually incised into a new channel) — the cited literature has no generation law that can be aligned equation by equation; if a component were written as “a new channel forms once accumulated overbank flow reaches a threshold”, its law would be identical to that of N15 and would give only a self-written constructive coverage, not evidence of coverage; it is therefore not admitted, and is registered as a structural clue.  
4.7  Signature Table (Finite Verification on W16-E)  
Appendix B.2 R5 gives the violation counts of C16 v0.2, N1, N2, N3, STF, HOM, HS, TF3, RW, TWO and N5 for I1, I2, ∅-absorption and u-dependence on random protocols (800 each). Reading: a 0 shows only that no violation was seen within the generated domain; a nonzero entry is the existence of an explicit violation. The nonzero I1 column of N3 agrees with Theorem N3-1 (without “the previous trial is non-∅”, I1 can fail through the grid phase); the 0 in the ∅-absorption column of N5 means only that the sampling did not hit a case (the spontaneous recovery of Proposition N5-6 is a counterexample type to ∅-absorption).

5\. Vocabulary of Dynamics Types

### 5.1  Use and Limits

The table below registers the laws on the working domain by dynamics type; codes may be assigned in multiples, are not required to be mutually exclusive, and are not claimed to be exhaustive. The type vocabulary is only a descriptive tool: two models registered under the same type do not automatically obtain a transfer of theorems between them; a transfer of theorems must be proved separately under the correspondence conditions of *The Mathematical Form of Duty Correspondence in Field Concretization* v1.0, or proved item by item in this paper for the specific pair of models. The column “original meaning in the main book” is only a correspondence, not the unique reading.

### 5.2  Code Table

　Φ1  Use-dependent write (broad type).  
　　Φ1a  Use-dependent deepening: on passage d += α (K1); or writing according to the path's own flux or according to the drive. Original meaning: Axioms 2 and 7, Dτ of Equation 2.  
　　Φ1b  Use-dependent weakening (depression branch of STDP, denoted MINUS): presynaptic firing triggers a weakening of the weight according to the postsynaptic trace, the amount being A₋ times the postsynaptic trace after this beat's update; the trace contains a current term from postsynaptic firing in this beat and an exponential residual term from earlier postsynaptic firing, and the residual term decays under rest by the trace decay factor without vanishing within finitely many beats (7.2 \[I-1\]). Disciplinary law: the depression branch of pair-based STDP.  
　Φ2  Shallowing: at rest d ← (1 − λ)d (K2). Original meaning: Eλ of Equation 2, Definition 23.  
　Φ3  Reaching bottom and where the surplus goes when full (broad type).  
　　Φ3a  Clip-and-discard (the frozen reading of K3 in C16 v0.2).  
　　Φ3b  Outward seepage when full: the excess after fullness goes to another path or elsewhere (FS, N2). Original meaning: Outer Chapter 3, “seep into the two sides”.  
　　Φ3c  Generation by accumulation along the edge when full (N15). Original meaning: Axiom 16, Equation 10.  
　Φ4  General accumulation and release (broad type; Retentive Containment).  
　　Φ4a  Entry accumulation — reset on opening — leakage: the total of the entry store h enters the opening criterion, opening resets it to zero, and leakage follows a declared law (LIF, N3, the gating membrane potential of s1). The accumulation feeds back into the opening read-out. Original meaning: Definition 16, Axiom 12, Inner Chapter 6, “gathering comes — and waiting appears. Joining comes — and emergence follows”.  
　　Φ4b  Side-branch material store: by-products of a process enter a store and are then output beyond the boundary by a declared law (the loose-material store of Family A: material shed by relaxation enters the store and is carried out of the boundary by the carrier fluid). The store does not feed back into the writing and relaxation of Structural Positions. Φ4b and Φ4a are not the same feedback mechanism: the accumulation of Φ4a changes opening, while the store of Φ4b changes no coefficient, occurrence or route of any Structural Position law.  
　Φ5  Threshold read-out (broad type).  
　　Φ5a  Threshold-gated read-out: the gate opens when a single gating quantity reaches threshold (g\_j = \[vt\_j ≥ θ\_j\] of s1); it judges only whether the gate opens, with no depth-based comparison among several paths.  
　　Φ5b  Depth-dependent threshold order and first-opening competition: θ = g(d) strictly decreases in depth, several paths compete by first-arrival step, and the deeper flows first (K6).  
　　Relation: Φ5b is one special case of Φ5, requiring several comparable paths of the same kind and depth-dependent thresholds; Φ5a does not imply Φ5b, and the opening criterion of each path in Φ5b is itself a Φ5a-type threshold.  
　Φ6  Use-induced threshold rise (adaptation, fatigue): on passage f += φ, recovery at rest (N1).  
　Φ7  Shared facilitation and homeostasis: F of STF, H of HOM.  
　Φ8  Cross-path coupling: HS (crosstalk deepens the other path), CMP (competition weakens the other path), the spill of FS/N2.  
　Φ9  Reinforcement-gated write (same-beat signal): TF3, TF3e, RW, RWe, TWO.  
　Φ10  History-modulated write by a shared neuromodulator: IZH, IZHc, IZD, N14 (the broadcast integrator family BIM). Original meaning: the Coloring of Inner Chapter 17 and Outer Chapter 9.  
　Φ11  Value or neuromodulator acting on performance: TON, PIT (threshold factors; writes unchanged).  
　Φ12  Goal feedback: IFB, N11. Original meaning: Inner Chapter 15, “To select is to bias the flow”.  
　Φ13  Own laws of structural events: PF, ERO, DIS, THR, DAM, N12 (self-release of blockades and scour on release). Original meaning: Inner Chapter 15, “Encounter obstruction it detours; encounter dam it swells; deposition and scouring form the path”.  
　Φ14  Intensity-dependent write: VST, BCM; the Hebbian write of Family E. Original meaning: Inner Chapter 15, “Earlier flow rushes and opens the way; later flow finds passage easier”.  
　Φ15  Flow division and share-proportional write: KIR, DN, N13. Original meaning: Inner Chapter 15, “By tendency shape is constructed; by shape the distribution of tendency changes”.  
　Φ16  Gated layered write: N5 (a short trace gates a long trace; parallel opposing layers). Original meaning: K7b, Law 18.  
　Φ17  Structural Position generation: GNG, GWR, MSP, N15. Original meaning: Axiom 16, Equation 10, Definition 21.  
Notation for coupling modes: ⊕ (c1 addition), ⊗g (c2 gating by some quantity), ∥ (c3 parallel trace), →e (c4 switching at an event); sharing the global Y is the default coupling.

6\. Discrimination Theorems  
Each subsection of this section is declared in the format of 3.5. After each theorem the corresponding section of the appendix programs is noted; the programs serve only as finite verification and as witnesses.  
6.1  C16 v0.2 and the Fatigue Candidate N1; the Symmetric Blind Spot and Presentation Input  
Candidate pair: Candidate A = C16 v0.2 (Definition 4-1); Candidate B = N1\[φ ≥ φ\_min\] (N1 of 4.2, φ\_min \> 0; g from the threshold family of 4.1, α, λ, D̄ ranging as in C16 v0.2, μ ∈ \[0, 1\]). Protocol domain Q₀: in every trial both paths are presented, there is no blockade, and P is constant within a protocol; only entry signals and rest are used (Π₀). N1 is a methodological witness constructed at the model layer, not a candidate arising from a clue; that N1 and C16 v0.2 are separable shows only that K\_F lacks a threshold-raising passage term, not that N1 is novel.  
Theorem 16-3 (passage budget of N1)  
　Hypotheses: an N1 member (φ \> 0); initial state d, f ≥ 0; the protocol belongs to Q₀, with all rests 0 and P constant.  
　Conclusion: there are at most L(φ, P) = 2(⌊P/φ⌋ + 1) non-∅ trials; once some trial has Y = ∅, every subsequent trial is ∅.  
　Proof: with all rests 0, f\_x increases by φ only on passage and does not decay, so before the k-th opening of path x, f\_x ≥ (k − 1)φ and θ\_x ≥ (k − 1)φ (g ≥ 0). Opening requires θ\_x ≤ P, which gives k ≤ ⌊P/φ⌋ + 1. Every non-∅ trial has at least one path opening, so the two paths together allow at most 2(⌊P/φ⌋ + 1) of them. A trial with Y = ∅ has no passage and is followed by no rest, so the state is unchanged, every θ of the next trial is the same, and that trial is again ∅. ∎  
Theorem 16-4 (Tier A: C16 v0.2 against N1\[φ ≥ φ\_min\])  
　Distinguishing observation: q\*, consisting of n = L(φ\_min, P) + 1 trials, with rest 0, P ≥ 1, T arbitrary and both paths presented; the reading is the Y sequence.  
　Validity: by Proposition 4-1 (I2), Out\_{C16 v0.2}(q*) ⊆ A := {sequences containing no ∅}; by Theorem 16-3 and L(φ, P) ≤ L(φ\_min, P), Out\_{N1\[φ ≥ φ\_min\]}(q*) ⊆ B := {sequences with Y\_n = ∅}. A ∩ B = ∅. ∎  
　Both sides nonempty: on the C16 side take g = 1/(1 + d), α = 1 and the zero initial state, giving {a, b} n times; on the N1 side take the same g and α, φ = 1/4, the zero initial state, P = 1, T = 10: in trial k, θ = 1/k + (k − 1)/4, so θ ≤ 1 for k ≤ 4 and θ \> 1 from trial 5 on; hence {a, b} × 4 followed by ∅ × 7 (n = 11).  
　Blind spot (quantifiers of reading and protocol): N1 members with φ \< φ\_min are not covered by Theorem 16-4. We now prove that for the whole N1 class (requiring only φ \> 0), under the full reading (Y, τ\_min), Q₀ contains no single uniform finite protocol that separates it from C16 v0.2.  
　　Proposition: for any given finite protocol q in Q₀ (length n, P constant, grid T, arbitrary rest after each trial), there exist a member m\_C of C16 v0.2 and a member m\_N of N1 (φ \> 0) whose full readings on q are identical trial by trial.  
　　Construction (first-grid-cell construction): take θ\* = min(1/2, P/(2T)); on both sides take α = 0, g0 = θ*, c = 1, the zero initial state d₀ = f₀ = 0, and any legal λ; N1 additionally takes μ = 0 and φ = (P/T − θ*)/(2n).  
　　Proof: θ\* ≤ P/(2T) \< P/T, so φ \> 0; θ\* ≤ 1/2 ≤ 1, so m\_C is legal. C16 side: α = 0 keeps d at 0, rest does not change a zero depth, θ stays θ*, and 0 \< θ*T/P ≤ 1/2, so by Lemma 3-1 τ is always 1 on both paths and Y = {a, b}. N1 side: the two paths pass symmetrically and synchronously; before trial k, f = (k − 1)φ (μ = 0, and rest does not change f), so θ = θ\* + (k − 1)φ \< θ\* + nφ = θ\* + (P/T − θ\*)/2 \< P/T, and θ \> 0, so τ is always 1 and Y = {a, b}. The readings of both sides are always ({a, b}, 1). ∎  
　　Reading: the quantifier is “for every finite protocol there exists an N1 member that depends on that protocol”; this shows that the whole N1 class has no uniform finite Tier A distinguishing observation in Q₀, not that some fixed N1 member is inseparable; Tier A holds only for subclasses with a positive lower bound on φ (Theorem 16-4 itself). If only the Y sequence is taken as the reading, the same-parameter witness (g = 1/(1 + d), α = 1, φ ≤ 1/n) already makes Y identical over the first n trials; but under the full reading this witness fails — Example J3a: with n = 2, P = 1, T = 2, φ = 1/100, Y is {a, b} {a, b} on both sides, while the first-opening steps are 2, 1 for C16 and 2, 2 for N1. The blind spot under the full reading is given only by the first-grid-cell construction.  
　　Verification: Appendix B.10 J3 (Example J3a; the first-grid-cell construction with 36 sets of fixed parameters and 1000 random finite protocols, the full readings of the two sides being identical throughout and always ({a,b}, 1)).  
Proposition 16-5 (non-coverage requires the K6 sign law): add to C16 v0.2 a parallel Trace-Response e\_x (on passage e\_x += φ, at rest e\_x ← (1 − μ)e\_x) that enters the threshold with coefficient s, θ\_x = g\_x(d\_x) + s·e\_x. (1) When s = +1, the state recursion of this member and that of the N1 member with the same parameters are literally identical under the renaming e ↔ f, the outputs are identical trial by trial under every protocol, and no observation can separate them. (2) When s = −1, the Trace-Response only lowers thresholds, in accordance with the K6 sign law, and the proof of I2 in Proposition 4-1 applies unchanged. Hence if the composition operators of the comparison class were not restricted by the K6 sign law, they would cover N1, and discrimination would be out of the question; this is also why, in D1.2, c3 allows K\_F components only to lower thresholds. ∎  
Theorem 16-6 (Tier B: a counting decision rule under the reading channel R\_ε)  
　Statistic and decision rule: under q\*(n) (n \> L := L(φ\_min, P), all rests 0), let S = the number of trials reported as ∅; decide for the N1 side if S ≥ τ, and for the C16 side otherwise.  
　Bounds of Misjudgment: for any member of C16 v0.2 and any initial state, P(S ≥ τ) ≤ P(Bin(n, ε) ≥ τ); for any member of N1\[φ ≥ φ\_min\] and any initial state, P(S \< τ) ≤ P(Bin(n − L, 1 − ε) \< τ).  
　Proof: on the C16 side the true value is never ∅ (Proposition 4-1), so a report of ∅ can occur only in a rewritten trial, and S ≤ the number of rewrites; the latter is a sum of independent indicators each with probability ≤ ε, and is stochastically smaller than Bin(n, ε) (by term-by-term coupling). On the N1 side the true value is ∅ in trials L + 1 to n (Theorem 16-3), and each of these trials that is not rewritten reports ∅, so S is stochastically larger than Bin(n − L, 1 − ε). ∎  
　Gap between the distributions: E\_C\[S\] ≤ nε and E\_N1\[S\] ≥ (n − L)(1 − ε); when n \> L/(1 − 2ε) (ε \< 1/2) the gap is positive. For finite n the discrimination error must be reported together with n, τ and ε; this is a discrimination with an error bound, not called error-free discrimination, and it is not an attribution of instances.  
Proposition 16-6′ (the channel class and the quantifiers of Tier A): (1) R\_ε contains the zero-rewrite channel, under which the readings are the true values and the zero misjudgment of Tier A in Theorem 16-4 holds as usual. (2) When ε \> 0, R\_ε also contains full-support rewrite channels (each trial is rewritten with probability p ∈ (0, ε\], and the rewritten value is uniform on {∅, {a}, {b}, {a, b}}): the probability of any reading sequence of length n is ∏\_k \[(1 − p)·1{y\_k = truth\_k} + p/4\] ≥ (p/4)^n \> 0, so the support of a single reading on either side is the whole set, and Tier A does not hold under such a channel. (3) Therefore “some admissible channel makes Tier A fail” holds, while “every admissible channel makes Tier A fail” does not; an upper bound ε alone cannot guarantee a zero misjudgment uniform over the whole channel class, and in that case the Tier B upper bounds on misjudgment (valid for the whole channel class) should be given. ∎  
Proposition 16-7 (symmetric blind spot)  
　Members: m\_C ∈ C16 v0.2 with g = 1/(1 + d) (the same on both paths), α = 1, λ = 1/10, D̄ = ∞; m\_B ∈ N1b with the same g, α, λ, D̄, and φ = 1/2, μ = 1, f\_cap = 1/2. Zero initial state.  
　Conclusion: for every protocol in Q₀ (any n, rests, T, P \> 0), m\_C and m\_B have the same Y sequence.  
　Proof: the parameters and initial states of the two paths are symmetric, Q₀ has only signals of the shared entry and rest, and the recursion keeps the states of the two paths equal, so in every trial τ\_a = τ\_b and Y ∈ {∅, {a, b}}. P \< 1: the initial θ = 1 \> P, so the first trial of both models is ∅; there is no passage, the zero state is unchanged under rest, and every later trial is ∅. P ≥ 1: m\_C has θ ≤ 1 and gives {a, b} in every trial. For m\_B, θ = 1/(1 + d) + f: f \> 0 only when no rest has followed the last passage (μ = 1 clears f after a single rest beat), and then d ≥ 1 and θ ≤ 1/2 + 1/2 = 1; when f = 0, θ ≤ 1; hence {a, b} in every trial. ∎  
　Reading: the N1b class has no Tier A distinguishing observation under Q₀; the outputs of the two sides are deterministic and identical, and the reading channel uses the same random kernel for identical output histories and does not read the identity of the candidate, so Tier B is unreachable as well. If the channel were allowed to use different kernels depending on the candidate's identity, a difference between distributions would come only from the channel and not from the candidates, and would not constitute discrimination. The τ of the two models can differ (m\_B has higher thresholds); this proposition concerns only the Y-sequence reading. By Theorem 15-4 of *The Mathematical Form of Duty Correspondence in Field Concretization* v1.0, identical readings do not imply identical mechanisms; conversely, separability does not imply a new type.  
　Verification: Appendix B.2 R1 (m\_C and m\_B with λ = 1/10, symmetric zero initial state, both paths presented; among 3267 protocols with n ≤ 5, rest ∈ {0, 1, 3}, T ∈ {3, 4, 10}, P ∈ {1/2, 1, 3/2}, 0 have differing Y sequences).  
Proposition 4-3 (presentation input breaks the symmetry): on W16-E, m\_C and m\_B of Proposition 16-7 are separated by the protocol \[Pres = {a}, P = 1\] → \[Pres = {a, b}, P = 1\] (T ≥ 3).  
　Proof: in the first trial a opens alone, and in both models d\_a = 1, with m\_B additionally having f\_a = 1/2; in the second trial m\_C has θ\_a = 1/2 \< θ\_b = 1, and for T ≥ 3 the threshold gap 1/2 \> 1/T, so by Lemma 3-2 Y = {a}; m\_B has θ\_a = 1/2 + 1/2 = 1 = θ\_b, so Y = {a, b}. ∎  
　Verification: Appendix B.2 R1 (T = 4: m\_C {a}@4 {a}@2; m\_B {a}@4 {a,b}@4).  
　Reading: the presentation set is an input operation only on hosts with path-separate inputs; on single-entry hosts, \[present only a\] and \[block b for one trial\] give the same readings and belong to Π₂. This proposition is a discrimination obtained after enlarging W, and does not close the blind spot within Q₀.  
Theorem 16-3, Theorem 16-4, Proposition 16-5, Theorem 16-6 and Proposition 16-6′ of this subsection are analytic results, and the appendix sets up no separate program for them (the blind-spot construction of Theorem 16-4 is in Appendix B.10 J3); the binomial-tail computation for the Tier B numbers uses the same method as Appendix B.2 R6.  
6.2  Outward Seepage When Full: Candidate N2, “Seep-Overflow” (Φ3b)  
Problem: in C16 v0.2, when a path that has reached bottom is passed again, the part exceeding capacity is erased by clipping. This conflicts with Axiom 2 (an effective pulse necessarily produces a Trace-Response) and with Outer Chapter 3, “it must first fill fa, and only thereafter seep into the two sides” — in the original text the seepage goes somewhere. In C16 v0.2, once one path has reached bottom, the other path never changes through the use of the former, and the sole lead is absorbing forever (I1) — a strong prediction that is sensitive to where K3 sends the surplus.  
Definition 6-1 (N2): the state of each path is d\_x ∈ \[0, D̄\_x\], D̄\_x ∈ ℚ\>0. On passage, first compute the own-position value d′\_x = min(d\_x + α\_x, D̄\_x) and the excess e\_x = d\_x + α\_x − d′\_x; then spill: for x ∈ Y, if e\_x \> 0, then d″\_o = min(d′\_o + e\_x, D̄\_o) (o being the other path; when both paths pass at once, each spills into the other on the basis of the result of the first step, and any part exceeding the other path's capacity is discarded). At rest d\_x ← (1 − λ\_x)d\_x, λ ∈ (0, 1\]. Threshold θ\_x = g\_x(d\_x). u is not read. That is, N2 = C16 v0.2 + “when a path that has reached bottom is passed, the excess spills into the other path with conservation”.  
Theorem N2-1: N2 satisfies I2, ∅-absorption and u-independence; I1 does not hold.  
　Proof: thresholds are determined only through d; passage (on the own position or by spill) only increases d; rest only decreases d; a ∅ trial has no passage and no spill. The proofs of I2 and ∅-absorption are as in Proposition 4-1. Counterexample to I1: g = 1/(1 + d), α = 1, D̄ = 2, initial state (1, 0), P = 1, T = 10, rest 0: N2 gives {a}@5 {a}@4 {a}@4 {a,b}@4 {a,b}@4 {a,b}@4, with final state (2, 2); C16 v0.2 with the same parameters always gives {a}, with final state (2, 0). ∎ (Appendix B.2 R2(a)(b))  
Theorem N2-2 (Inclusion after reaching bottom; equal capacity, same mechanism)  
　Hypotheses: a member of N2₌—g\_a = g\_b, α\_a = α\_b = α \> 0, D̄\_a = D̄\_b = D̄ \< ∞; arbitrary initial state; protocol with both paths presented, P ≥ 1, rest 0, arbitrary T; first trial Y\_1 = {x}.  
　Conclusion: let K = 1 + 2⌈D̄/α⌉. There is k ≤ K with o ∈ Y\_k; and from trial K on, Y = {a, b}.  
　Proof: as long as x is the sole lead, x passes once per trial. x needs n₁ = ⌈(D̄ − d\_x)/α⌉ passages to become full. Once x is full, θ\_x = g(D̄) is the smallest possible threshold, so x is in Y on every later trial; each passage leaves an excess of α, all of which overflows into o (clipped at D̄); if o is also in Y, it gains its own α in addition. Hence after x is full, o gains at least α per trial (until it is full), and o is full after at most n₂ ≤ ⌈D̄/α⌉ further trials. n₁ + n₂ ≤ 2⌈D̄/α⌉. After that both paths are full, the thresholds are equal, Y = {a, b}, the excess is discarded, and the state no longer changes. o first enters Y no later than the moment the two paths become equal. ∎  
　Verification: Appendix B.2 R2(c) (1899 runs of random N2₌ with α ≥ 1/6, D̄ ≤ 3 and a sole lead on the first trial: first inclusion later than the bound 37 in 0, latest observed 29; not {a, b} from K on in 0).  
Theorem N2-3 (Capacity takeover)  
　Hypotheses: g\_a = g\_b = g, α\_a = α\_b = α \> 0, D̄\_x \< D̄\_o \< ∞; both paths presented, P ≥ 1, rest 0; the grid satisfies P/T \< g(D̄\_x) − g(D̄\_o); Y\_1 = {x} (the path of smaller capacity is the sole lead first).  
　Conclusion: there is K′ ≤ 1 + ⌈D̄\_x/α⌉ + ⌈D̄\_o/α⌉ such that Y = {o} from trial K′ on.  
　Proof: until x is full, as in Theorem N2-2; after x is full, o gains at least α per trial. While o has not passed D̄\_x, θ\_o ≥ θ\_x and o cannot be the sole lead; once o passes D̄\_x, Y is {o} or {a, b}; in the latter case both paths pass, the full x overflows into o, and o keeps deepening. When o is full, θ\_x − θ\_o = g(D̄\_x) − g(D̄\_o) \> P/T, so Y = {o} by Lemma 3-2; from then on o is the sole lead and neither path changes (the excess overflowing into the already full x is discarded), so Y = {o} holds permanently. o needs ≤ ⌈D̄\_x/α⌉ + ⌈D̄\_o/α⌉ trials to become full. ∎  
　Verification: Appendix B.2 R2(d)(d′) (witness with D̄\_a = 1, D̄\_b = 3, α = 1/2, initial state (1/2, 0), T = 20; 1874 random runs without exception).  
　Reading: the path of larger capacity is never the sole lead and in C16 stays behind forever; in N2 it deepens passively on the other path's excess and finally takes over.  
Theorem N2-4 (Two-arm probe: overflow only after reaching bottom)  
　Class bounds Θ: for both paths α ∈ \[α\_min, α\_max\], D̄ ∈ \[D̄\_min, D̄\_max\] (D̄\_min \> α\_max), g0 ∈ \[g0\_min, 1\], c ∈ \[c\_min, c\_max\]; zero initial state.  
　Protocol q\_I4(m) (two arms): arm 1 \[Pres = {b}, P = 1\]; arm 2 \[Pres = {a}, P = 1\] × m, then \[Pres = {b}, P = 1\]; rest 0. Reading: τ\_b on the last trial of each arm.  
　Take m\_s = ⌈D̄\_min/α\_max⌉ − 1 (so that m\_s·α\_max \< D̄\_min), m\_ℓ = ⌈(D̄\_max + δ)/α\_min⌉ (δ \> 0 declared), T ≥ T\* = max{1/Δ\_N2, 1/Δ\_HS, 1/Δ\_STF}, where Δ\_N2 = g0\_min·c\_min·δ′/(1 + c\_max·δ′), δ′ = min(δ, D̄\_min); Δ\_HS = g0\_min·c\_min·κ′/(1 + c\_max·κ′), κ′ = min(κ\_min, D̄\_min); Δ\_STF = g0\_min·κ\_F,min·F̄\_min.  
　Conclusion: (1) C16 v0.2, N1, N3, HOM, TF3, RW, TWO, N5 (u ≡ 0): for every m, τ\_b is the same in both arms. (2) N2\[Θ\]: for m = m\_s the two arms are equal; for m = m\_ℓ, τ\_b in arm 2 is strictly smaller than in arm 1. (3) HS\[κ ≥ κ\_min\] and STF\[κ\_F ≥ κ\_F,min, F̄ ≥ F̄\_min\]: for m ≥ 1, τ\_b in arm 2 is strictly smaller than in arm 1. Hence the reading pair (changed at m\_s?, changed at m\_ℓ?) is (no, no), (no, yes) and (yes, yes) on the three groups respectively, and these are pairwise disjoint.  
　Proof: (1) On the trials that present only a, b does not pass and there is no rest; in these members the state of b changes only through b's own passages or rests (the entry store of N3: when only a is presented and P = 1 ≥ θ\_a, a opens and the store is zeroed, so on the last trial of arm 2, as in arm 1, h = 0). (2) In arm 2, a opens on each of the m trials presenting only a (P = 1 ≥ g0 ≥ θ\_a), and d\_a rises to min(mα, D̄\_a); m\_s·α ≤ m\_s·α\_max \< D̄\_min ≤ D̄\_a, so there is no excess and b is unchanged; m\_ℓ·α ≥ D̄\_max + δ ≥ D̄\_a + δ, so the total overflow into b is ≥ δ, d\_b ≥ δ′, θ\_b ≤ g(δ′), and g(0) − g(δ′) ≥ Δ\_N2; with P = 1, τ = ⌈θT⌉, so by Lemma 3-3 the τ\_b of the two arms differ by ≥ ⌊Δ\_N2·T⌋ ≥ 1. (3) HS: m ≥ 1 passages of a give d\_b ≥ κ′, and the argument is the same; STF: after the first trial with a presentation F ≥ κ\_F·F̄, so on the last trial of arm 2 θ\_b ≤ g0\_b − g0\_b·κ\_F·F̄, a difference of ≥ Δ\_STF. ∎  
　Verification: Appendix B.2 R2(h) (α ∈ \[1/4, 1/2\], D̄ ∈ \[1, 2\], g0 ≥ 3/4, c ∈ \[1/2, 2\], m\_s = 1, m\_ℓ = 9, T = 400, 400 runs per class).  
Theorem N2-5 (Tier B): N2₌ under the class restriction “sole lead on the first trial” versus C16 v0.2; protocol as in Theorem N2-2 (n ≥ K); statistic S = the number of trials from K to n reported as both paths open together; decision rule S ≥ τ. On the C16 side the true value is never both paths open (I1), so S is stochastically smaller than Bin(n − K + 1, ε); on the N2 side the true value is both paths open on every trial from K on, so S is stochastically larger than Bin(n − K + 1, 1 − ε). The proof is as for Theorem 16-6. ∎ For numbers see Appendix B.2 R6 and Section 8.  
Coverage verdict (N2):  
　(1) Versus K\_F: N2 is not a member of C16 v0.2 (I1 is violated), nor a parameter variation of it (no parameter in C16 lets the passage of one path change the other). If K3 takes the conserving outward-seepage reading of “seep into the two sides”, N2 is simply the completion of K\_F itself, not a new mechanism.  
　(2) Versus FS: proven coverage (W-instance layer). Under the map d ↔ storage S, α ↔ inflow V, D̄ ↔ capacity Cap, λ ↔ leakage rate, threshold g(d) ↔ outflow threshold g(S), the recursions of the two sides are identical term for term (in Appendix A.1 the two are independent implementations; Appendix B.2 R2(f): readings and final states identical over 2000 random runs). At the literature layer there is only a qualitative two-item match (the FS entry of 4.2), and the result is not promoted to a renaming of a literature mechanism.  
　(3) Versus the neural baselines: resource competition (Triesch et al. 2018) runs in the opposite direction; a synapse that reaches its upper limit simply stops potentiating, and resources return to the pool only on depression; crosstalk acts on the induction threshold of neighbouring spines even before saturation. Under the broad reading, HSsat (c4: at a's clipping event, a's write is switched to a write into b) has readings and final states identical to N2 on lattice initial states, and on general initial states differs only at the crossing step (HSsat discards part of the excess on the crossing step, while N2 transfers it with conservation; Appendix B.2 R2(g): lattice initial states, 2000 of 2000 identical; general initial states, of 2000 runs, readings identical in 1744, readings and final states identical in 1527). Hence on a baseline containing only neural members: approximate coverage under the broad reading (differing at the crossing step), no coverage under the strict reading.  
　(4) Versus synaptic tagging and capture: cross-input capture requires the captured pathway to have a tagging event of its own; N2's overflow requires no event of o's own at all, and the two separate on protocols in which “o is never presented” (arm 2 of Theorem N2-4).  
　Conclusion: at the W-instance layer N2 is proven covered by FS; at the literature layer there are only qualitative structural clues; it is not reported as a new dynamics. Its substantive significance: the destination chosen for K3 is a load-bearing antecedent of I1—if K3 takes the outward-seepage reading, I1 no longer holds, while I2 still holds.  
Conditional predictions (B): premise set—objects with a single entry and two paths, whose pressure can be raised to read the first opening and which admit consecutive trials without rest, have dynamics of N2 type. P-N2-1 (Theorem N2-2): the path that is the sole lead on the first trial opens together with the other path after at most 1 + 2⌈D̄/α⌉ consecutive trials, and always together thereafter; rules out C16 v0.2 and HOM. P-N2-2 (Theorem N2-3): the path of larger capacity, never having been the sole lead, eventually takes over the sole lead. P-N2-3 (Theorem N2-4): presenting only a a few times leaves b's first-opening step unchanged, while many presentations bring it forward; rules out C16, N1, N3, HOM, TF3, RW, TWO (never changes) and HS, STF (changes after a few). Side effect: consecutive trials themselves deepen both paths to the bottom, so tests must report their testing history. Neural reading (saturated excess passed to sister synapses on the same entry): the cited domain literature contains no rule of this kind; it is logged as an unregistered conditional prediction.  
Breach: an N2₌ member and initial state such that one path is the sole lead on the first trial while the other path has not entered the first-opened set within 1 + 2⌈D̄/α⌉ trials; a run satisfying the hypotheses of Theorem N2-3 that does not end at {o}; an N2 member in Θ whose τ\_b changes under q\_I4(m\_s) or does not change under q\_I4(m\_ℓ). Not a breach: with unequal capacities and the sole-lead path having the larger capacity, N2 and C16 give the same output under the first-opening reading (a blind spot of the first-opening reading, closed by the probe of Theorem N2-4).  
6.3  Entry Retentive Containment: Candidate N3, “Gather and Wait, Join and Emerge” (Φ4a)  
Problem: in C16 v0.2 a trial on which nothing opens leaves no trace at all (∅-absorption). This conflicts with Definition 16, Retentive Containment (“lag, remain, accumulate”), and with Inner Chapter 6: “Thus gathering comes — and waiting appears. Joining comes — and emergence follows. This is Latency Nature. It is one foundational nature of signal-flow within Sedimentation-Field.” In C16, subthreshold drive has no effect however often it is repeated, which is wrong for any host with temporal summation.  
Definition 6-2 (N3): the state is the (d\_a, d\_b) of C16 v0.2 plus an entry store h ≥ 0 (initial value h₀ ∈ \[0, min θ); both branches, h₀ = 0 and h₀ \> 0, lie in the domain). Let h\_k be the store at the start of trial k (h\_1 = h₀). Within a trial p\_t = h + t·P/T; if some path opens, passage follows C16 and h ← 0 (joining comes, and emergence follows); if no path opens and Pres ≠ ∅, h ← ρ·(h + P) (gathering comes, and waiting appears; ρ ∈ \[0, 1\] is the end-of-trial retention ratio); at rest h ← (1 − μ\_h)h, and d shallows as in C16. u is not read. That is, N3 = C16 v0.2 + one closed law for K11.  
Theorem N3-1: N3 satisfies I2, u-independence and I1′; ∅-absorption fails; I1 itself fails.  
　I1′: if h\_k = 0 at the start of trial k, Y\_k = {x}, and between trials k and k + 1 there is rest 0, the same P, both paths presented and no blockade, then Y\_{k+1} = {x}. Sufficient sources of h\_k = 0: (i) k = 1 and h₀ = 0; (ii) k ≥ 2 and Y\_{k−1} ≠ ∅ (the opening on trial k − 1 zeroes h, and the rest after it keeps it at 0). Indexing: Y\_k ≠ ∅ yields only h\_{k+1} = 0, not h\_k = 0.  
　Proof: I2: p\_T = h + P ≥ P ≥ 1 ≥ θ. I1′: h\_k = 0; Y\_k = {x} ≠ ∅ gives h\_{k+1} = 0; h is 0 on both trials, and the argument is that of I1 in Proposition 4-1. ∎  
　Two branches: with zero store on the first trial (h₀ = 0) the first pair lies in the domain of I1′; with nonzero store on the first trial (h₀ \> 0) the first pair lies outside it, and I1 can fail—Example J4a: θ = (19/20, 1), α = 0, h₀ = 3/50, P = 1, T = 10: on the first trial τ\_a = ⌈8.9⌉ = 9, τ\_b = ⌈9.4⌉ = 10, Y = {a}, and h is zeroed; on the second trial τ\_a = τ\_b = 10, Y = {a, b} (with the same parameters and zero initial store, both trials give {a, b}). For a counterexample to ∅-absorption see Theorem N3-2.
