　Verification: Appendix B.2 R3(a) (3000 random runs with zero initial store, 0 violations of I1′; the declared domain of the checking function viol\_I1p is “the first pair is included only when h₀ = 0, other pairs only when the preceding trial is non-∅”, and the first pair is handled according to the given h₀); Appendix B.10 J4 (Example J4a; 3000 random runs with zero initial store, 0 violations; in the same group with nonzero initial stores, 0 violations among in-domain pairs, and I1 fails on the first pair in 22 runs).  
Theorem N3-2 (Closed form of subthreshold summation)  
　Hypotheses: both paths presented; P constantly equal to P\_lo; rest between trials r = 0; h = 0 before the first trial; θ\_min = min(θ\_a, θ\_b) \> P\_lo.  
　Conclusion: for every j ≥ 1, conditional on no opening on trials 1 through j − 1, trial j opens if and only if P\_lo·(1 + ρ + … + ρ^{j−1}) ≥ θ\_min. The trial of first opening j\* is the least j satisfying this inequality; if ρ \< 1 and P\_lo/(1 − ρ) ≤ θ\_min there is never an opening; if ρ \< 1 and P\_lo/(1 − ρ) \> θ\_min, j\* is finite; if ρ = 1, j\* = ⌈θ\_min/P\_lo⌉. The closed form does not extend past a zeroing event: after the first opening h is zeroed and the opened path deepens, so the count must restart from the new thresholds with h = 0.  
　Proof: if trials 1 to j − 1 do not open, there is no passage and no rest among them, and θ is unchanged; h\_{i+1} = ρ(h\_i + P\_lo), h\_1 = 0, and induction gives h\_j + P\_lo = P\_lo·Σ\_{i\<j} ρ^i; on trial j, p\_T = h\_j + P\_lo, and the trial opens if and only if p\_T ≥ θ\_min. The partial sums increase monotonically, and for ρ \< 1 the supremum P\_lo/(1 − ρ) is not attained. ∎  
　Verification: Appendix B.2 R3(b) (902 random runs with r = 0; the closed-form j\* agrees with every run); Appendix B.3 Q4 (ρ = 9/10, α = 1/10, P\_lo = 1/4: first opening at j\* = 5, in agreement with the closed form; the next actual opening is on trial 10, whereas the same geometric sum counted from trial 1 satisfies the inequality on trials 6–10—it may not be carried past a zeroing event).  
Proposition N3-2′ (r \> 0): with the other hypotheses as above but r \> 0, let q = ρ(1 − μ\_h)^r and let θ\_min(j) be the minimum threshold on trial j (nondecreasing in j because of shallowing). Conditional on no earlier opening: a necessary condition for trial j to open is P\_lo·Σ\_{i\<j} q^i ≥ θ\_min(1), and a sufficient condition is P\_lo·Σ\_{i\<j} q^i ≥ max\_x g0\_x; in particular, if P\_lo/(1 − q) ≤ θ\_min(1) there is never an opening.  
　Proof: without an opening, h\_{j} + P\_lo = P\_lo·Σ\_{i\<j} q^i (each interval first multiplies by ρ, then by (1 − μ\_h) once for each of its r beats); θ\_min(j) ∈ \[θ\_min(1), max g0\]. ∎  
　Verification: Appendix B.2 R3(f) (ρ = 1/2, P\_lo = 1/3, θ = 1: all 30 trials ∅; ρ = 9/10, μ\_h = 1/2, P\_lo = 1/4, θ = 1: first opening on trial 5 when r = 0, never an opening when r = 1, 3).  
Theorem N3-3 (The store shifts only the starting point, not the first opener)  
　Hypotheses: h \< θ\_min at the start of the trial, θ\_a ≠ θ\_b, P/T \< \|θ\_a − θ\_b\|. Conclusion: if the trial is non-∅, then Y = {the lower-threshold path}.  
　Proof: by Lemma 3-2. Moreover, if the initial value h₀ \< θ\_min, then “h \< θ\_min at the start of the trial” is maintained throughout—without an opening h + P \< θ\_min, so ρ(h + P) \< θ\_min; after an opening h = 0; rest only decreases h, and θ decreases only after a passage, at which point h = 0. ∎  
　Verification: Appendix B.2 R3(c) (no disagreement in 710 random cases).  
　Reading: N3 keeps K6's “the deeper flows first” (when the threshold difference exceeds the grid step) and only brings the first-opening step forward; it changes “when” and “whether”, not “who”.  
Proposition N3-4 (Intermittent opening): under the class bound θ ≥ θ\_floor \> P\_lo, rest 0 and constant pressure P\_lo, the trial following each opening of N3 is necessarily ∅ (h = 0, P\_lo \< θ), after which summation starts afresh; the interval between openings does not exceed j\*\_max (Theorem N3-2 with θ\_min ≤ 1 substituted) and does not increase as the opened path deepens. ∎ (Appendix B.2 R3(g): with α = 0, P = 1/2, ∅ ∅ {a,b} ∅ ∅ {a,b} …)  
Proposition N3-5 (The interval–leak condition runs opposite to silence sensitization): by Proposition N3-2′, a longer rest between trials makes q smaller, and summation fails once P\_lo/(1 − q) drops below θ\_min; the excitability of HOM, by contrast, rises only on rest beats, so at rest 0 the threshold is unchanged (∅-absorption), while after a sufficiently long rest the threshold falls below P\_lo and the path opens. The two are in reverse order with respect to “long or short rest”. ∎ (Appendix B.2 R3(g): P = 1/2; HOM: all 12 trials ∅ at rest 0, and with 6 rest beats after each trial, ∅ followed by {a,b} throughout; N3: intermittent opening at rest 0, all ∅ with 6 rest beats after each trial)  
Theorem N3-6 (Tier A: subthreshold-summation protocol)  
　Class bounds: g0 ∈ \[g0\_min, 1\], c ≤ c\_max, D̄ ≤ D̄\_max; initial thresholds ≥ θ\_floor := g0\_min/(1 + c\_max·D̄\_max) (each baseline additionally requires the effective threshold of its initial state to be ≥ θ\_floor, e.g. through H for HOM, F for STF, s + L − E for N5). Take P\_lo \< θ\_floor, ρ\_min \> 1 − P\_lo, j*\_max = min{j: P\_lo(1 − ρ\_min^j)/(1 − ρ\_min) ≥ 1}, n = j*\_max + 1. Protocol q\_N3: n trials, P = P\_lo, both paths presented, rest 0, u = 0. Reading property Π: “there is k \< n with Y\_k ≠ ∅ and Y\_{k+1} = ∅”.  
　Conclusion: every output of N3\[ρ ≥ ρ\_min\] (with any initial store h₀ ∈ \[0, θ\_min)) has Π; no output of the baseline union C16 v0.2 ∪ N1 ∪ HOM ∪ STF ∪ TF3 ∪ RW ∪ TWO ∪ N5 ∪ N2 ∪ HS (each within its class bounds) has Π. Hence the output sets of the two sides are disjoint, and both sides are nonempty. The theorem does not claim that the baselines can be separated from one another: C16 and STF, for example, can both output ∅^n (overlap example in Appendix B.3 Q1).  
　Proof: (N3) by Theorem N3-2, j\* ≤ j*\_max ≤ n − 1 (θ\_min ≤ 1; when h₀ \> 0, an unopened trial j has h\_j + P\_lo = P\_lo·Σ\_{i\<j} ρ^i + ρ^{j−1}h₀ ≥ P\_lo·Σ\_{i\<j} ρ^i, so the first opening can only come earlier); on trial j* + 1, h = 0 and P\_lo \< θ\_floor ≤ θ (passage only lowers the threshold, and not below θ\_floor), so the trial is ∅. (C16, N1, HOM, TF3, RW, TWO, N5, N2, HS) on the first trial p\_T = P\_lo \< θ\_floor ≤ θ, so it is ∅; these members do not change state without passage or rest, so every trial is ∅, and Π fails. (STF) every trial with a presentation leaves F nondecreasing, and passage leaves d nondecreasing, so the threshold does not rise; with P unchanged, once a trial opens every later trial opens, so “an opening followed by ∅” never occurs, and Π fails. ∎  
　Verification: Appendix B.2 R3(d) (g0 ≥ 3/4, c ≤ 1, D̄ ≤ 2, θ\_floor = 1/4, P\_lo = 1/5, ρ\_min = 7/8, j\*\_max = 8, n = 9, 1500 runs per class); Appendix B.3 Q1 (600 runs per class).  
　Blind spot: a member can sum if and only if P\_lo \> θ\_min·(1 − ρ), while the protocol requires P\_lo \< θ\_floor ≤ θ\_min. Hence fast-leaking members with ρ ≤ 1 − θ\_floor/θ\_min give ∅^n, just as C16 does, under every constant-pressure protocol with P\_lo \< θ\_floor (fast-leak blind spot, U16-12).  
Theorem N3-7 (Tier B): the comparison subclass is C16 v0.2, N1, HOM, TF3, RW, TWO, N5, N2, HS within the class bounds of Theorem N3-6 (STF excluded: the threshold of STF falls with presentations, so on a lengthened protocol it can eventually open, and the count upper bound for an all-∅ true value does not hold for it). The protocol q\_N3 is lengthened to n = j*\_max·m, and the statistic is S = the number of trials reported non-∅. On q\_N3, members of the comparison subclass have p\_T = P\_lo \< θ\_floor ≤ θ on the first trial, and without passage or rest their state does not change, so for any n the true value is all ∅ (proof of Theorem N3-6), and S is stochastically smaller than Bin(n, ε); the true value of N3 is non-∅ at least m times (Proposition N3-4), and S is stochastically larger than Bin(m, 1 − ε). ∎ For numbers see Appendix B.2 R6; ε must be smaller than about 1/(j*\_max + 1) for the gap to be positive.  
Coverage verdict (N3):  
　(1) Versus K\_F: not a member of C16 v0.2 (∅-absorption is violated), nor a parameter variation of it; it is one closed law for K11.  
　(2) Versus entry LIF: proven coverage (W-instance layer). Map h ↔ membrane potential V, ρ ↔ ρ\_V, μ\_h ↔ μ\_V, “opening” ↔ “firing (the exit is whichever reaches threshold first)”, “h ← 0” ↔ “reset” (Appendix B.2 R3(e): readings and final states of independent implementations identical over 2000 random runs). At the literature layer the match is partial, item by item (the LIF entry of 4.2: no leak within a trial is an approximation, and the first-arrival assignment between the two exits is a composition), and the result is not promoted to a renaming of a literature mechanism. On s1 this is exactly the membrane potential v of the gating units (λ = 3/4, v\_reset = 0).  
　(3) Versus STF, HOM, N1: the three fail on different sides—N1 does not sum, STF does not reset, HOM relies on silence rather than drive (Theorem N3-6, Proposition N3-5).  
　Conclusion: N3 is not reported as a new dynamics. Its substantive significance: K11 must have a law; on hosts with temporal summation the ∅-absorption of C16 is necessarily breached, and every discrimination that rests on ∅-absorption has to be rewritten for such hosts; on s1, the I2-like and I1-like invariants that take the first opener as the reading need only be asserted under the condition “the preceding trial is non-∅ (the membrane potential has been reset)” (I1′).  
Conditional predictions (B): P-N3-1 (Theorem N3-2): repeated subthreshold drive first causes an opening at the j*-th repetition; j* grows as the interval between drives lengthens, and beyond a critical interval there is never an opening. P-N3-2 (Proposition N3-4): subthreshold drive immediately after an opening has no effect, and summation must start afresh; rules out STF. P-N3-3 (Proposition N3-5): the same subthreshold drive opens at short intervals and fails to open at long intervals, while pure silence does not cause an opening; rules out HOM-type silence sensitization. On neural hosts these are the known phenomena of temporal summation of the membrane potential; in the riverbed reading h is water impounded behind a dam (Inner Chapter 15: “encounter dam it swells”); all of this is known physics.  
Breach: a run satisfying the hypotheses of Theorem N3-2 whose trial of first opening disagrees with the closed form; h \< θ\_min and P/T \< \|θ\_a − θ\_b\| while the first opener is not the lower-threshold path; members of C16, N1 or HOM within the class bounds showing a non-∅ trial under q\_N3, or N3\[ρ ≥ ρ\_min\] showing no “non-∅ followed by ∅” under q\_N3.  
6.4  Gated Layered Write: Candidate N5, “Carrying the Trace into Depth” (Φ16)  
Problem: reports on the *Drosophila* mushroom body (Huang et al., preprint 2022; Felsenberg et al. 2018): short-term depression gates long-term depression through a recurrent dopamine pathway; unpaired presentations early after training strengthen long-term plasticity, and late ones weaken it; extinction sets up a separate, parallel opposing Trace-Response while the original Trace-Response is retained. In C16 v0.2, non-reinforced tests can only deepen the trained path (I1); in TF3, non-reinforced tests change nothing; in RW and TWO, non-reinforced tests can only weaken. None of the three can give both “strengthening early” and “weakening late”. Original meaning in the main book: Law 18, “new Trace-Responses do not overwrite old Trace-Responses; new structures only accumulate, bias, and reorganize”; tuning, “closing is suppression; opening is the removal of suppression”.  
Definition 6-3 (N5): the state of each path consists of a short trace s ∈ \[0, s̄\], a long trace L ≥ 0 and an opposing layer E ≥ 0; effective depth δ\_x = max(0, s\_x + L\_x − E\_x); θ\_x = g\_x(δ\_x). Reinforced passage (x ∈ Y and u = 1): s ← min(s + α\_s, s̄). Non-reinforced passage (x ∈ Y and u = 0): if s ≥ σ\* (σ\* \> 0), L += β (disinhibition write gate: while the short trace is present, the use of the path itself becomes the write signal for the long trace); otherwise E += β\_E (parallel opposing layer; L is not rewritten). At rest s ← (1 − μ\_s)s, E ← (1 − μ\_E)E, L ← (1 − μ\_L)L (μ\_L may be 0). That is, N5 = tuning-as-disinhibition controlling the write layer + a parallel opposing layer + a two-layer Trace-Response. It goes beyond the present text of Definition 26 (tuning does not change history): the gate controls which layer is written.  
Two-arm protocol: zero initial state; j training trials \[Pres = {a}, u = 1, P ≥ g0\] with rest 0, and rest R after the last training trial; n test trials \[both paths presented, u = 0, P ≥ g0, rest 0\]. Let s\_R = min(j·α\_s, s̄)·(1 − μ\_s)^R. Short arm R = R\_s, long arm R = R\_ℓ.  
Theorem N5-1 (Short arm: non-reinforced tests deepen, and the sole lead is not lost)  
　Hypotheses: g\_a = g\_b = g; s\_R ≥ σ\*; Y = {a} on the first test. Conclusion: Y = {a} on all tests; τ\_a does not increase; if g(s\_R) − g(s\_R + (k − 1)β) ≥ P/T, τ\_a strictly decreases at least once within the first k tests.  
　Proof: rest is 0 during the test phase, so s\_a stays at s\_R ≥ σ\* and E\_a stays 0; each passage of a gives L\_a += β, so δ\_a increases and θ\_a decreases. For b, s\_b = 0 \< σ\*, so even when it passes it only increases E\_b; δ\_b stays 0 and θ\_b = g(0) is unchanged. Hence τ\_a does not increase and τ\_b is unchanged; since τ\_a \< τ\_b on the first test, this holds from then on. The strict decrease follows from Lemma 3-3. ∎  
Theorem N5-2 (Long arm: non-reinforced tests weaken, and the sole lead is lost)  
　Hypotheses: g\_a = g\_b; s\_R \< σ\*; Y = {a} on the first test. Conclusion: Y = {a, b} occurs within at most ⌈s\_R/β\_E⌉ + 1 tests.  
　Proof: training writes only s, so L\_a = 0; during the test phase s\_a stays at s\_R \< σ\*, each passage of a gives E\_a += β\_E, and δ\_a = max(0, s\_R − E\_a) decreases; a is always in Y while θ\_a ≤ θ\_b, so after at most ⌈s\_R/β\_E⌉ passages δ\_a = 0 and θ\_a = θ\_b, and the next test gives Y = {a, b}. ∎  
　Verification: Appendix B.2 R4 (witness α\_s = 1/2, s̄ = 2, σ\* = 1/2, β = β\_E = 1/4, μ\_s = 1/4, μ\_E = 1/2, 4 training trials, T = 40; 1200 random runs: strict decrease of τ on the short arm in 1200; on the long arm, all 947 runs with s\_R \< σ\* and a sole lead on the first test lose the sole lead).  
Proposition N5-3 (Invariants of the baselines on the two-arm protocol): (1) C16 v0.2 and TF3: I1 holds in the test phase, so a sole lead on the first test remains the sole lead. (2) TF3: there is no write in the test phase, so τ is constant and never strictly decreases. (3) RW (η ∈ (0, 1\], V̄ ≥ 0): in the test phase each passage of a gives d\_a ← (1 − η)d\_a, so τ\_a does not decrease. (4) TWO (B\_f + B\_s ≤ 1, zero initial state, V̄ ≥ 0): during training both components increase from 0, and they remain ≥ 0 after rest; in the test phase d ← (1 − B\_f − B\_s)d, so τ does not decrease.  
　Proof: each item follows directly from the law ((1) as in Proposition 4-1; in the TF3 test phase u = 0, so there is no write). ∎ (Appendix B.2 R4)  
Theorem N5-4 (Tier A: two-arm protocol)  
　Subclass N5\[Θ5\]: the two paths share the threshold mechanism g = g0/(1 + c·d), g0 ∈ \[g0\_min, 1\], c ∈ \[c\_min, c\_max\]; α\_s ∈ \[α\_s,min, α\_s,max\], s̄ ∈ \[s̄\_min, s̄\_max\], σ\* ∈ \[σ*\_min, σ*\_max\] (σ\*\_min \> 0), β ∈ \[β\_min, β\_max\] (β\_min \> 0), β\_E ∈ \[β\_E,min, β\_E,max\] (β\_E,min \> 0), μ\_s ∈ \[μ\_s,min, μ\_s,max\] ⊂ (0, 1), μ\_E arbitrary, μ\_L = 0.  
　Protocol q5(j, R\_s, R\_ℓ, n, P, T): each arm starts from zero initial state, with j training trials \[Pres = {a}, u = 1, P\], rest R after the last one (R\_s on the short arm, R\_ℓ on the long arm), then n test trials \[both paths presented, u = 0, P, rest 0\]; P ≥ 1. Let s\_short,min = min(j·α\_s,min, s̄\_min)(1 − μ\_s,max)^{R\_s}, s\_long,max = s̄\_max(1 − μ\_s,min)^{R\_ℓ}, s\_long,min = min(j·α\_s,min, s̄\_min)(1 − μ\_s,max)^{R\_ℓ}.  
　Conditions of Θ5: (i) s\_short,min ≥ σ*\_max; (ii) s\_long,max \< σ*\_min; (iii) long-arm horizon condition n ≥ 1 + ⌈s\_long,max/β\_E,min⌉; (iv) first-test grid condition: g(0) − g(s) \> P/T holds for s ∈ {s\_long,min, s\_short,min} (sufficient condition g0\_min·c\_min·s/(1 + c\_max·s) \> P/T); (v) short-arm descent condition: with the worst-case endpoints of g0 and c substituted, g(s\_short,min) − g(s\_short,min + (n − 1)β\_min) ≥ P/T.  
　Conclusion: the reading pair (τ\_a strictly decreases on the short arm?, sole lead lost on the long arm?) is always (yes, yes) on N5\[Θ5\]; (yes, yes) never occurs on C16 v0.2 ∪ TF3 ∪ RW ∪ TWO. Hence the two-arm outputs of the two sides are disjoint. The theorem does not claim that the four baselines can be separated from one another.  
　Proof: N5 side—by (i), (iv), (v) and Theorem N5-1, the short arm has a sole lead on the first test and τ\_a strictly decreases at least once; by (ii), (iv) and Theorem N5-2, the long arm has a sole lead on the first test and loses it within at most ⌈s\_long/β\_E⌉ + 1 ≤ n tests. Baseline side—by Proposition N5-3: on the long arm, C16 v0.2 and TF3 do not lose the sole lead; on the short arm, τ does not strictly decrease for TF3, RW and TWO; hence every member of the union has at least one coordinate equal to no. ∎  
　Verification: Appendix B.3 Q2 (Θ5 box with j = 4, R\_s = 1, R\_ℓ = 12, T = 200: all 300 N5 runs give (yes, yes); C16 all (yes, no); TF3 all (no, no); RW (no, no) 271, (no, yes) 29; TWO all (no, no)); critical example in the same section (R = 1, s\_long = 1/4, β\_E = 1/1000, T = 100): with n = 2 the long arm keeps the sole lead throughout—condition (iii) is load-bearing; by (iii) n\_req = 251, and with n = 250 and with n = 251 the sole lead is lost on the 241st test (the grid makes the actual loss come earlier than the bound).  
Proposition N5-5 (Two arms with u flipped): zero initial state, protocol \[Pres = {a}, u = 1\] × j, then tests; the other arm inverts every u. For the u-independent classes (C16, N1, N2, N3, STF, HOM, HS) the readings of the two arms necessarily coincide; in N5, training with all u = 0 writes only E (s = 0 \< σ\*), δ\_a stays 0, θ\_a = θ\_b on the first test, and Y = {a, b}; training with u = 1 makes s \> 0, and on the first test (when the threshold difference exceeds the grid step) Y = {a}, so the two arms differ. ∎ (last row of Appendix B.2 R4)  
Proposition N5-6 (Recovery after extinction: eventual sign, exact reading and full horizon)  
　Antecedent: the state s₀, L₀, E₀ ≥ 0 at some moment after extinction on the long arm, followed by R rest beats; μ\_s, μ\_L, μ\_E ∈ \[0, 1\], with decay factors r\_s = 1 − μ\_s, r\_L = 1 − μ\_L, r\_E = 1 − μ\_E. The untruncated value is u\_R = s₀·r\_s^R + L₀·r\_L^R − E₀·r\_E^R (with the convention 0⁰ = 1), and the effective depth is δ\_R = max(0, u\_R). The two paths share the threshold mechanism, path b has effective depth constantly 0, and tests use P ≥ g0.  
　Terminology: “recovery” is used only where an actual failure came first—there is R\_f with δ\_{R\_f} = 0, and some later R \> R\_f with δ\_R \> 0; a case that starts with u\_0 \> 0 and later falls to 0 is called “eventual failure”, not recovery.  
　(1) Eventual-sign criterion: merge the terms of u\_R by decay factor (coefficients with equal decay factors are added; a term with decay factor 0 acts only at R = 0 and does not enter the eventual outcome), take the largest decay factor r\* whose merged coefficient is nonzero, and denote that merged coefficient by c*. If c* \> 0 there is R₀ with δ\_R \> 0 for all R ≥ R₀; if c\* \< 0 there is R₀ with δ\_R = 0 for all R ≥ R₀; if all merged coefficients are 0, u\_R ≡ 0 for R ≥ 1. If the merged coefficient at some decay factor (factor 1 included) is 0, that factor is not r*, and one must go on to the next decay factor whose merged coefficient is nonzero. Proof: u\_R = Σ c\_i·r\_i^R (r\_i distinct); after division by r*^R the other terms decay as (r\_i/r*)^R → 0, so u\_R/r*^R → c*. ∎ Scope: the criterion decides only the sign for sufficiently large R; it does not decide transient recovery or transient failure within a finite horizon, nor does it give R₀. Branches: strictly faster or slower—if s₀ \> 0 and r\_s is strictly larger than r\_E and than r\_L (when L₀ \> 0), then c* = s₀ \> 0; likewise, if L₀ \> 0 and r\_L is strictly largest, c\* = L₀. Equal rates—if r\_L = r\_E is the largest decay factor and the s term is faster or 0, c\* = L₀ − E₀, and for L₀ \< E₀ the eventual value is 0 (Example V1b: s₀ = 0, L₀ = 1, E₀ = 2, μ\_L = μ\_E = 1/2, c\* = −1, δ\_R ≡ 0); for r\_s = r\_E decide likewise by s₀ − E₀; when all three factors are equal, decide by s₀ + L₀ − E₀. The claim “L₀ \> 0 and μ\_L ≤ μ\_E imply positive recovery” merges the equal-rate branch into the strict branch and does not hold.  
　(2) Resolvable sole-lead recovery (exact reading): at a given R, Y = {a} if and only if ⌈θ\_a(R)·T/P⌉ \< ⌈θ\_b·T/P⌉, where θ\_a(R) = g(δ\_R) and θ\_b = g(0); both are ≤ g0 ≤ P and hence reachable within the window. g(0) − g(δ\_R) \> P/T is a sufficient condition for this inequality, not a necessary one; “threshold difference ≤ P/T for all R” does not imply that there is no resolvable recovery (Example V1a: g0 = 19/20, T = 10, δ = 1/10 give a threshold difference of 19/220 \< 1/10, yet τ\_a = 9 \< τ\_b = 10 and a is the sole lead). ∎  
　(3) Sufficient condition for no recovery (same grid cell): let k\_b = ⌈θ\_b·T/P⌉; b's grid cell is ((k\_b − 1)P/T, k\_b·P/T\]. If g(sup\_R δ\_R) \> (k\_b − 1)P/T, then θ\_a(R) lies in the same cell for every R (θ\_a ≤ θ\_b and θ\_a ≥ g(sup δ)), so ⌈θ\_a T/P⌉ = k\_b and Y is always {a, b}. An upper bound on sup\_R δ\_R can be obtained by explicit computation over a finite segment plus the tail bound s₀ r\_s^R + L₀ r\_L^R (R ≥ R₁). ∎  
　(4) Persistent terms and the limit: a term with decay factor 1 (μ = 0) does not decay. Let the persistent coefficient be A = s₀·1\[μ\_s = 0\] + L₀·1\[μ\_L = 0\] − E₀·1\[μ\_E = 0\]. Then lim u\_R = A and lim δ\_R = max(0, A). If A \> 0, c\* = A, and eventually δ\_R \> 0 with δ\_R → A; if A \< 0, eventually δ\_R = 0; if A = 0, the merged coefficient at factor 1 is 0, and by (1) the eventual sign is fixed by the nonzero merged coefficient at the next-largest decay factor, in which case δ\_R → 0. Proof: u\_R = A + Σ\_{r\_i \< 1} c\_i r\_i^R, where each term of the sum → 0; max(0, ·) is continuous. ∎ Boundary example: s₀ = L₀ = 1, E₀ = 3, μ\_s = μ\_L = 0, μ\_E = 1/2: A = 2, δ\_0..δ\_3 = 0, 1/2, 5/4, 13/8, limit 2—here δ\_0 = 0 while δ\_1 \> 0, a genuine recovery after failure; taking only L₀ as the persistent term would miss the persistent short-trace term when μ\_s = 0 and s₀ \> 0. Continued-search example: s₀ = E₀ = 1 (μ\_s = μ\_E = 0), L₀ = 1/2 (μ\_L = 1/2): A = 0; continue to factor 1/2, coefficient +1/2, eventual sign positive, δ\_R = 2^{−R−1} → 0.  
　(5) No recovery over the full horizon when the starting point has already failed: if u\_0 ≤ 0 and the eventual sign in (1) is ≤ 0 (c\* \< 0, or all merged coefficients 0), then u\_R ≤ 0 for all R ≥ 0. Proof: the only negative term in u\_R is −E₀ r\_E^R. If c\* \< 0, the merged group at r\* has a negative coefficient, so it must contain the E term, and r\_E = r*; every s or L term with nonzero coefficient has r\_s, r\_L ≤ r\_E (otherwise the largest of them would form a group of its own with positive coefficient, contradicting the choice of r*); hence s₀ r\_s^R + L₀ r\_L^R ≤ (s₀ + L₀) r\_E^R, and u\_R ≤ (s₀ + L₀ − E₀) r\_E^R = u\_0·r\_E^R ≤ 0. If all merged coefficients are 0, u\_R ≡ 0 for R ≥ 1, and u\_0 ≤ 0. ∎ The converse reaches only the eventual outcome: if the starting point has failed and the eventual sign is positive, recovery must occur, but (1) does not give where it starts; that must be computed explicitly on the concrete parameters. This item applies to the structure “two nonnegative exponential terms minus one nonnegative exponential term” and is not extrapolated to general exponential sums.  
　(6) Cases with early L: (1)–(5) apply equally when L₀ \> 0; for μ\_L = 0 the persistent term is given by A in (4) (which includes the persistent s and E terms), and one does not simply say “the recovery floor is L₀”; for μ\_L \> 0 one does not call L₀ the recovery floor either.  
　Verification: Appendix B.4 V1 (Example V1a, exact first-opening steps 9 vs 10; Example V1b, eventual sign −1, no positive depth for R = 0..100; the eventual-sign criterion agrees with the actual sign at R = 400..409 over 3000 random runs; Example V1c, the whole trajectory in the same cell, no sole lead; Example V1d, trajectory not confined to the same cell, sole lead at R = 1, 2); Appendix B.5 E1 (boundary example and continued-search example; 4000 random runs including the μ = 0 boundary all satisfy \|δ\_300 − max(0, A)\| ≤ 10⁻⁶; 855 random runs for (5) show no positive depth for R = 0..119); Appendix B.3 Q3 (same-cell example: s = 1/400, E = 1/200, μ\_s = 1/2, μ\_E = 3/4, T = 40; for R = 1..79 the depth is positive 78 times and a is the sole lead 0 times; positive depth and resolvable recovery are determined separately). Verification at finite R does not replace the asymptotic proof of (1).  
Coverage verdict (N5):  
　(1) Versus K\_F: not a member of C16 v0.2 (u-dependence, use-induced weakening). The two items it relies on—tuning that controls writing, and a parallel opposing layer—have only their original meaning in K\_F.  
　(2) Versus the *Drosophila* model of Huang et al.: structural clue only; not yet determined. The main text of the preprint: the model describes firing rates and synaptic weights by ordinary differential equations, KC→MBON is rewritten by an anti-Hebbian rule, γ1 is the short-term unit and α2, α3 are long-term units, and an isolated unpaired presentation shortly after training drives extinction of plasticity within the short-term unit while strengthening long-term α3 plasticity; the equations and parameters are given in the supplementary appendix and were not included in the comparison. One difference is visible at the mechanism level: in the model of Huang et al. an early unpaired presentation also makes the short-term unit extinguish, whereas in N5 the short trace does not fall through a non-reinforced passage. The two give a testable divergence in the reading of the short-term unit (P-N5-4).  
　(3) Using general laws only: under the broad reading, N5 = c1(TF3-type write of s, c2(s → gate on a K1-type write of L), c2(1 − \[s ≥ σ\*\] → opposing write of E))—depth-2 coverage; under the strict reading, “gating the long-trace write by the path's own short trace” is not a declared channel, so there is no coverage.  
　Conclusion: N5 is not reported as a new dynamics, nor judged novel. Its substantive significance: it gives a closed set of conditions for “recurrent gating protects the long trace” together with their provable consequences; if the tuning of K7b is to carry “disinhibition opens the gate” on neural hosts, it must be allowed to control the write layer, which goes beyond the present text of Definition 26; the “accumulate, bias” of Law 18 has a formal reading in N5 (the parallel opposing layer only biases and does not rewrite).  
Conditional predictions (B, *Drosophila* reading): premise set—some combination of mushroom-body compartments can be declared to be of type W16-E (the two odours are the two paths, T-maze choice is the first-opening reading, stimulation of dopaminergic neurons or electric shock is u), its dynamics are of N5 type, and the short trace, the long trace and the opposing layer belong to compartments that can be read out separately. P-N5-1 (Theorems N5-1, N5-2): after training, non-reinforced tests at a short interval make the choice stronger and faster, while tests at a long interval weaken it until there is no preference; every test writes, so the number of tests must be reported as an independent variable; rules out C16 v0.2, TF3, RW and TWO. P-N5-2 (Proposition N5-6): after extinction has caused an actual failure, a long rest makes the preference reappear if the eventual sign is positive, and if it is ≤ 0 there is no recovery over the full horizon; rules out single-layer models in which “extinction is erasure”. P-N5-4: after the short-arm tests, the depression of the short-term unit is maintained (N5) or weakened (the verbal description of the Huang et al. model). P-N5-3: after extinction, blocking the output of the compartment that holds the opposing layer makes the original memory reappear at once—this corresponds to the phenomenon reported by Felsenberg et al. 2018, for which a corresponding experiment already exists; it is not a new prediction. Untested premises: the correspondence of compartments to layers, the correspondence of T-maze choice to the first-opening reading, and the amount written by a test.  
Breach: an N5 member with s\_R ≥ σ\* and a sole lead on the first test that loses the sole lead on some test or whose τ\_a rises; one with s\_R \< σ\* and a sole lead on the first test that is still the sole lead throughout the first ⌈s\_R/β\_E⌉ + 1 tests; an RW or TWO member (within the hypotheses) whose τ strictly decreases in the test phase.  
6.5  Goal Feedback: Candidate N11, “Selective Bias Maturing” (Φ12)  
Problem: in K7b, selective bias has only its original meaning (Inner Chapter 15: “to select is to bias the flow”; “when a thing receives, it does not of itself follow its flow but is forced to bias it; therefore this is selection”), and the original text also links selective bias with tuning (“thus selection—is it not tuning?”). C16 v0.2 does not read an intention input, and once one path is deeper than the other, the shallow path can never become the sole lead; K\_F cannot express “how forcibly biased use turns into sedimentation, and when the forced bias can be withdrawn”.  
Definition 6-4 (N11): W16-E plus an intention input z\_k ∈ {a, b, none} (declared for each trial, input side, Π₀). State: for each path, d (the law of C16 v0.2) and a selective-bias effort e ∈ \[0, ē\]. Threshold θ\_x = max(0, g(d\_x) − e\_x). After each trial: if z = x, then e\_x ← max(0, e\_x − κ\_d) when Y = {x}, and e\_x ← min(ē, e\_x + κ\_u) otherwise; if z ≠ x, then e\_x ← max(0, e\_x − κ\_d). At rest, d shallows and e is unchanged.  
Theorem N11-1 (Habit formation)  
　Hypotheses: g\_a = g\_b = g (g0 ≤ 1); α\_b \> 0; D̄\_a \< ∞, D̄\_b ≥ D̄\_a + δ, δ \> 0 and g(D̄\_a) − g(D̄\_a + δ) \> P/T; both paths presented, P = 1, rest 0, z constantly equal to b; κ\_u \> 0, κ\_d \> 0, ē ≥ g0; initial state e = 0.  
　Conclusion: let K = (⌈g0/κ\_u⌉ + 1)·⌈(D̄\_a + δ)/α\_b⌉ + 1. From trial K on, Y is always {b}, and within at most ⌈ē/κ\_d⌉ trials after that e\_b = 0 (the effort is fully withdrawn while the choice persists).  
　Proof: when e\_b ≥ g0, θ\_b = 0 and τ\_b = 0, while θ\_a \> 0 and τ\_a ≥ 1, so b is the sole lead; on every trial on which b is not the sole lead, e\_b increases by κ\_u, so between any two sole leads of b there are at most ⌈g0/κ\_u⌉ failures. Each sole lead of b increases d\_b by α\_b (up to D̄\_b). The depth of a is ≤ D̄\_a. Hence after at most ⌈(D̄\_a + δ)/α\_b⌉ sole leads of b, d\_b ≥ D̄\_a + δ ≥ d\_a + δ, and then, whatever e is, θ\_a − θ\_b ≥ g(D̄\_a) − g(D̄\_a + δ) \> P/T (g is decreasing, and e ≥ 0 only lowers θ\_b), so b is the sole lead by Lemma 3-2; from then on b is always the sole lead, d\_b does not decrease (rest 0), and e\_b falls by κ\_d per trial down to 0. ∎  
　Verification: Appendix B.3 Q6 (800 random runs within the hypotheses, no exception; witness with a at depth 1 and capacity 1, b at depth 0 and capacity 3, α = 1/4, κ\_u = 1/4, κ\_d = 1/8: {a} {a} {a,b} {b} {b} …, final state d = (1, 3), e\_b = 0; C16 v0.2 from the same initial state: b is the sole lead in 0/300 over 20 trials).  
Proposition N11-2 (Course after the goal is withdrawn: a beat-by-beat sufficient criterion and two conditional predictions)  
　After the goal is withdrawn (z changed to “none”), e\_b falls by κ\_d per trial (down to 0), and if b is the sole lead on some trial, d\_b still increases by α\_b—the depth difference at withdrawal is not a constant.  
　(1) Beat-by-beat sufficient criterion: let θ\_b(k) = max(0, g(d\_b(k)) − e\_b(k)) and θ\_a(k) = g(d\_a(k)). If θ\_b(k) − θ\_a(k) \> P/T on trial k, then a is the sole lead on that trial (b is not); if θ\_a(k) − θ\_b(k) \> P/T, then b is the sole lead (by Lemma 3-2 when θ\_b(k) \> 0; when θ\_b(k) = 0, τ\_b = 0 \< τ\_a). Both statements concern only the current beat and imply nothing about later beats.  
　(2) Conditional prediction after short training: if at withdrawal d\_b + ⌈e\_b/κ\_d⌉·α\_b \< d\_a and g(d\_b + ⌈e\_b/κ\_d⌉·α\_b) − g(d\_a) \> P/T, then within at most ⌈e\_b/κ\_d⌉ + 1 trials of the withdrawal b loses the sole lead and never regains it (the effort is already 0, b no longer deepens, and a deepens only through passage). When the condition fails, no prediction is made.  
　(3) Conditional prediction after long training: if at withdrawal d\_b \> d\_a and g(d\_a) − g(d\_b) \> P/T, then b is always the sole lead thereafter (I1 of C16; e\_b only falls and never rises, and once at 0 has no further effect).  
　Proof: (1) by Lemma 3-2; (2) the effort returns to zero within ⌈e\_b/κ\_d⌉ trials, during which b deepens by at most ⌈e\_b/κ\_d⌉·α\_b; afterwards b's threshold is no lower than g(d\_b + ⌈e\_b/κ\_d⌉·α\_b) and a's threshold no higher than g(d\_a), so the threshold difference exceeds the grid step; (3) by I1 of Proposition 4-1 and the fact that e\_b only falls. ∎  
　Verification: the goal-withdrawal witness of Appendix B.3 Q6, and Appendix B.4 V4 (α = 1/16, κ\_d = 1/2, κ\_u = 1/4: on withdrawal after 6 training trials, d\_a = 1, d\_b = 3/16, e\_b = 0, the short-training condition holds, and the next 12 trials are all {a}; after 60 training trials d\_b = 21/8, the long-training condition holds, and the 12 trials are all {b}).  
Coverage verdict (N11): (1) Versus K\_F: not in C16 v0.2 (it reads z); if the K7b branch subsumes selective bias under tuning, N11 is a completion of K\_F. (2) W-instance layer: N11 coincides item by item with “IFB superposed by c1 on the threshold of C16 v0.2” (denoted IFBC; in IFB, k\_R ↔ κ\_u, k\_B ↔ κ\_d, m̄ ↔ ē, a = 1\[not the goal, or the goal already the sole lead\])—proven coverage (W-instance layer), and a valid composition under the strict reading (the gating signal is taken from z and Y) (Appendix B.3 Q6: readings and final states of independent implementations identical over 800 runs). Literature layer: the law form of integral feedback is matched according to the standard formulation; at the behavioural level, “the shift from goal-directed to habitual control, and insensitivity to devaluation after overtraining” (Miller et al. 2019; Adams 1982) is a structural clue and has not been matched item by item against the main texts of those works. Not reported as a new dynamics.  
Conditional predictions (B): P-N11-1 (Theorem N11-1): under the goal, the shallow path becomes the sole lead after a period of effort, after which the effort (separately readable, e.g. as an extra component of response latency) falls back to 0 while the choice is unchanged; rules out the whole class C16 v0.2. P-N11-2 (Proposition N11-2): withdrawing the goal after short training returns the choice to the deep path, while after long training it does not; rules out single-controller models in which “choice is determined only by the current goal”.  
Breach: within the hypotheses, a trial after trial K that is not {b}, or effort that does not return to zero once b is permanently the sole lead; the conditions of Proposition N11-2 (2) or (3) hold but the course disagrees with the conclusion.

6.6  The Blockade's Own Law: Candidate N12, “Clog, Swell, Scour a Channel” (Φ13)  
Problem: Inner Chapter 15: “encounter obstruction it detours; encounter dam it swells; deposition and scouring form the path; earlier flow rushes and opens the way; later flow finds passage easier”. In K\_F, Brk is a direct fact of structural integrity (Definition 5), only frozen or set and lifted by the protocol, with no law of its own; neither “encounter dam it swells” nor “deposition and scouring form the path” is formalized. In C16, only the detour path deepens during a blockade, and the blocked path at most shallows; the longer the blockade, the more the bias after release favours the detour path—the opposite direction to the original meaning, “water held behind the dam scours open a new channel”.  
Definition 6-5 (N12): W16-E plus structural blockade (the protocol sets Brk\_x = 1 at the start of some trial, Π₂) and, for each path, a head H ≥ 0 impounded behind the dam. At the start of a trial: if Brk\_x = 1 and H\_x ≥ B̄\_x (breakthrough level), breakthrough occurs: Brk\_x ← 0, d\_x ← min(d\_x + κ\_s·H\_x, D̄\_x), H\_x ← 0; when the protocol lifts the blockade on this trial, the scour is released in the same way. During a trial: a path with Brk\_x = 1 does not open; otherwise as in C16 v0.2. After a trial: if Brk\_x = 1 and x is presented, H\_x ← H\_x + ρ\_H·P. At rest: d shallows, H ← (1 − μ\_H)H. Controls: C12 (same protocol, Brk set and lifted only by the protocol, no head) and HOMB (the excitability of the blocked path rises on rest beats).  
Theorem N12-1 (Breakthrough time)  
　Hypotheses: initial head H\_a,0 ≥ 0 (the rest of the initial state valid and arbitrary); the protocol sets Brk\_a = 1 once, at the start of trial 1 only, and never lifts it; a is presented on every trial, P is constant, ρ\_H·P \> 0, rest 0.  
　Conclusion: breakthrough occurs at the start of trial k\_br = 1 + max(0, ⌈(B̄\_a − H\_a,0)/(ρ\_H·P)⌉). Before breakthrough, H\_a = H\_a,0 + j·ρ\_H·P after the j-th blocked trial; at breakthrough H\_a is zeroed and accumulates no further.  
　Zero-accumulation branch: if ρ\_H·P = 0, or a is not presented during the blockade, H\_a stays at H\_a,0: if H\_a,0 ≥ B̄\_a, breakthrough occurs on trial 1; otherwise it never occurs.  
　Proof: the breakthrough check is made at the start of each trial. If H\_a,0 ≥ B̄\_a, breakthrough occurs on trial 1. Otherwise, at the start of trial j + 1, H\_a = H\_a,0 + j·ρ\_H·P (rest 0, no leak), and the first j making this ≥ B̄\_a is ⌈(B̄\_a − H\_a,0)/(ρ\_H·P)⌉. ∎  
　Verification: Appendix B.4 V2 (Example V2a: H₀ = B̄ = 5/2, breakthrough on trial 1; no disagreement over 1500 random runs; zero-accumulation branch, no breakthrough in 12 trials); Appendix B.3 Q7 (H₀ = 0, ρ\_H = 1/2, B̄ = 5/2: breakthrough on trial 6).  
Theorem N12-2 (Sign of the bias after release: versus C12 and HOMB only)  
　Hypotheses: zero initial state; g\_a = g\_b = g; m drive trials with a blocked (both paths presented, P = 1, rest 0, m \< ⌈B̄/ρ\_H⌉, no breakthrough), and the protocol lifts the blockade on trial m + 1; let γ = κ\_s·ρ\_H − α\_b, D̄ = ∞.  
　Conclusion: on the release trial d\_a = κ\_s·ρ\_H·m and d\_b = α\_b·m. If γ \> 0 and g(α\_b m) − g(κ\_s ρ\_H m) \> P/T, the release trial gives Y = {a}; for C12 (same parameters) the release trial gives Y ∈ {{b}, {a, b}}; HOMB (rest 0) behaves as C12.  
　Proof: during the blockade b is the sole lead on every trial, and d\_b increases by α\_b; a has no passage, rest 0 causes no shallowing, and the head accumulates to m·ρ\_H; at release a is scoured deeper by κ\_s·m·ρ\_H. Y = {a} by Lemma 3-2. C12 has no head and no scour, so d\_a = 0 ≤ d\_b; the excitability of HOMB changes only on rest beats, so with rest 0 it does not change. ∎  
　Verification: Appendix B.3 Q7 (511 random runs with γ ≥ 1/6, m = 3 and ample margin: N12 gives {a} in all of them, C12 and HOMB in none; witnesses for m = 1–4).  
　Reading: for γ \> 0, the longer the blockade, the larger a's advantage after release (d\_a − d\_b = γ·m); in C12, the longer the blockade, the larger b's advantage. This sign separates N12 only from C12 and HOMB; N1 (fatigue of the detour path), HS (crosstalk) and the valid composition HSP (c2: the crosstalk increment gated by 1\[a ∈ Pres\], valid under the strict reading) in C\_cmp v0.1 can also bias toward the blocked path after release (Appendix B.4 V3), so “bias toward the blocked path after release” is not a signature exclusive to N12.  
Proposition N12-3 (Drive-type head versus silence-type homeostasis): replace the drive trials during the blockade by “r rest beats after each trial during the blockade”: N12's head leaks by the factor (1 − μ\_H)^r; the excitability of HOMB's blocked path rises on each rest beat. Hence there are parameters for which N12's release trial is {b} (the head has leaked away) while HOMB's is {a} (silence sensitization); taken together with Theorem N12-2, the two are in reverse order with respect to “drive or no drive, rest or no rest during the blockade”. ∎ (Appendix B.3 Q7: 4 rest beats after each trial, blockade lifted after 3 trials, N12 {b}, HOMB {a})  
Distinction from N3: during the blockade b opens on every trial, so N3's entry store is zeroed on every trial and does not accumulate, and release brings no reverse strengthening; N12's head belongs exclusively to the blocked path and is zeroed only by breakthrough or release.  
Two invariants relative to C\_cmp v0.1 (for v0.1 only; for the contract see Definition 4-D1). The blockade Brk is a frozen flag set and lifted by the protocol (Π₂), not the state of any component of v0.1.  
Lemma N12-L1 (I\_B: a blockade does not release itself): for every member of C\_cmp v0.1 (under both the strict and the broad reading, every composition of depth ≤ 2), on every trial after the protocol has set Brk\_x = 1 on some trial and has not yet lifted it, x does not open.  
　Proof: D1.4 ② computes τ only for paths that are presented and not blocked; no component in L₁ has Brk as state or writes Brk (checked one by one against the update rules of each component in Appendices A.1 and A.2); c1–c4 only combine the laws and events of components, and in E “setting and lifting a blockade” is given only by the protocol (D1.5); hence Brk\_x stays 1 until the protocol lifts it, and x never opens. ∎ The lemma depends only on “blocked paths get no τ” in D1.4 ①②, “a blockade is set and lifted only by the protocol” in D1.5, and “no component has Brk as state or writes Brk” in D1.1; it does not depend on Lemma N12-L2 and can be cited on its own.  
Lemma N12-L2 (I\_PD: invariance under drive strength during blockade): for every member of C\_cmp v0.1 (under both the strict and the broad reading), let two protocols q₁, q₂ differ only in the peak flow pressure P on a stretch of trials K and agree otherwise, and suppose the two runs have the same first-opened set Y on every trial in K, with that set non-∅. Then after K the states of the two runs agree component by component, and all later readings agree.  
　Proof: induction on trials. Suppose the two runs have the same state before trial k ∈ K. Each component in L₁ takes in the trial only through Y, u, Pres, z, events in E and rest; the only update that reads P is LIF's “if there is no firing, V ← ρ\_V(V + P)”, which happens only on trials with Y = ∅ (checked one by one: K1/K3 of C16 read Y; N1, HS, TF3, RW, TWO, FS read Y and u; STF reads whether Pres is nonempty; HOM reads Y and rest; IFB reads z and Y; LIF resets to 0 when Y ≠ ∅). c1 only adds threshold terms, the parallel Trace-Responses of c3 are driven by the same events, the gating functions of c2 read only declared channels or component states (both identical under the induction hypothesis; P is not a declared gating channel), and the switching events of c4 are taken from E (which does not contain P). Hence, with Y identical and non-∅, the states after the trial agree; after K the protocols agree entirely, the states agree, and so do the readings. ∎ The lemma depends on the global-Y semantics of D1.4 and on D1.6; if the contract allowed components their own threshold events or local opening, it would have to be proved anew.  
　Verification: Appendix B.4 V3 (300 runs for each of the 11 classes in L₁, blockade-period P = 1 versus P ∈ \[1, 3\]; among runs in which the premise holds, 0 have different readings after release; since N1's threshold can exceed 1, the premise holds in only 122 of its runs; with the blockade never lifted and only a presented, all 11 classes give ∅ throughout). Random verification component by component is not exhaustive over all compositions; the universal conclusion comes only from the proof.  
　Reading: in v0.1, the “presence” of drive during a blockade can be read out by a valid composition (HSP), but its “strength” cannot.  
Theorem N12-4 (Tier A discrimination relative to C\_cmp v0.1)  
　Subclass N12\[Θ12\]: the two paths share the threshold g = g0/(1 + c·d) (g0 ∈ \[g0\_min, 1\], c ∈ \[c\_min, c\_max\]); α\_b ∈ \[α\_lo, α\_hi\], κ\_s·ρ\_H ∈ \[r\_lo, r\_hi\], r\_hi·P₁ \< α\_lo, r\_lo·P₂ \> α\_hi (P₂ \> P₁ ≥ 1), B̄\_a \> ρ\_H,max·P₂·m (no breakthrough during the blockade), D̄ = ∞; zero initial state.  
　Protocol q12 (two arms): a is blocked once, on trial 1; during the blockade, m trials with both paths presented and rest 0, with P = P₁ in arm 1 and P = P₂ in arm 2; on trial m + 1 the protocol lifts the blockade, with P = 1. Reading: the Y sequence of each arm during the blockade and Y on the release trial.  
　Conclusion: (1) For N12\[Θ12\], both arms read {b}^m during the blockade, while on the release trial arm 1 gives Y = {b} and arm 2 gives Y = {a}, provided T satisfies g(r\_lo·P₂·m) + 1/T \< g(α\_hi·m) and g(α\_lo·m) + 1/T \< g(r\_hi·P₁·m) (with the worst-case endpoints of g0 and c substituted). (2) By Lemma N12-L2, for every member of C\_cmp v0.1 on this protocol, if the blockade-period readings of the two arms agree and are non-∅, then the release-trial readings of the two arms agree. Hence the two-arm outputs of N12\[Θ12\] are disjoint from the two-arm outputs of the whole of C\_cmp v0.1 (under both the strict and the broad reading). (3) If B̄\_a is taken small (breakthrough during the blockade) and the protocol never lifts the blockade, N12 opens a while the protocol's blockade is in force; by Lemma N12-L1, no member of C\_cmp v0.1 can do so.  
　Proof: (1) during the blockade a does not open, and b has τ\_b ≤ T on every trial (θ\_b ≤ g0 ≤ 1 ≤ P), so Y = {b}; at release d\_a = κ\_s ρ\_H P\_i m and d\_b = α\_b·m; in arm 2, d\_a ≥ r\_lo·P₂·m \> α\_hi·m ≥ d\_b, and in arm 1, d\_a ≤ r\_hi·P₁·m \< α\_lo·m ≤ d\_b; Y follows by Lemma 3-2. (2), (3) by Lemmas N12-L2 and N12-L1. ∎  
　Verification: Appendix B.4 V3 (500 random runs with α\_b ∈ \[3/10, 1/2\], ρ\_H ∈ \[1/8, 1/4\], κ\_s = 1, m = 4, P₁ = 1, P₂ = 5, T = 400, B̄ = 100, all in agreement; breakthrough witness with B̄ = 1, ρ\_H = 1/2: {b} {b} {a} {a}, breakthrough on trial 3).  
　Limits: the theorem shows only that C\_cmp v0.1 lacks mechanisms of breakage, recovery and strength dependence (Proposition 4-4); it is not grounds for recognizing a new dynamics.  
Theorem N12-5 (relative to C\_cmp v0.2: N12 ≡ c1(C16 v0.2 core, DAM))  
　Construction: M\_D = c1(C16 v0.2 core, DAM), depth 1, 2 leaves, valid under the strict reading (DAM uses no gating). The parameter map is the identity: (g0\_x, c\_x, α\_x, λ\_x, D̄\_x) ↦ the parameters of the same names in the C16 core, (B̄\_x, ρ\_H, μ\_H, κ\_s) ↦ the parameters of the same names in DAM; initial state (d, H, Brk) ↦ (d, H, Brk) (including arbitrary H₀, d₀).  
　Conclusion: for every protocol (arbitrary P, Pres, rest, and times at which blockades are set and lifted) and every initial state, N12 and M\_D have the same readings (Y, τ\_min) and the same states (d, H, Brk) trial by trial.  
　Proof: by induction on trials. Suppose the two have the same state before trial k begins. ①′: both first execute the same protocol setting; for every path with Brk\_x = 1, the release condition is in both cases “the protocol lifts the blockade or H\_x ≥ B̄\_x” (M\_D contains no ERO, DIS or THR), and the effect of release is in both cases Brk\_x ← 0, d\_x ← min(d\_x + κ\_s·H\_x, D̄\_x), H\_x ← 0 (the order of checking is independent between the two paths, and the result does not depend on the order). ①②: both compute θ\_x = g\_x(d\_x) only for paths that are presented and not blocked, with the same grid and stop convention, so τ, Y and τ\_min are the same. ③: both apply d\_x ← min(d\_x + α\_x, D̄\_x) for x ∈ Y; for paths with Brk\_x = 1 that are presented, H\_x += ρ\_H·P. ④: the rest law is the same. ∎  
　Membership (D3.6): only those M\_D that correspond to an N12 falling inside the D2.3 box (κ\_s ∈ \[0, 2\], ρ\_H, μ\_H ∈ \[0, 1\]) are members of the fixed C\_cmp v0.2; outside the box the identity still holds.  
　Verification: Appendix B.5 E3 (1500 random runs with arbitrary setting and lifting of blockades, presentation, rest and initial accumulated head: readings and final states all identical).  
　Reading: DAM's laws were written on W from the qualitative content of dam-break scour, and they are written identically to N12's—the identity holds because both were constructed by the same recursion. It shows that on W the laws of N12 coincide with a component written from the qualitative content of dam-break scour, so N12 does not constitute a new mechanism relative to v0.2; by itself it does not constitute an independent proof of literature coverage (U16-24).  
Proposition N12-6 (a depth-2 composition without DAM reproduces both signatures but is not equal to N12)  
　Construction: M\_VP = c1(C16 v0.2 core, c2(VST, 1\[Brk\]), PF), with θ₊ = θ₋ = 0 and μ\_u = 1 (a path that is blocked and presented has A\_p·P² written into it each trial), depth 2, valid under the strict reading (1\[Brk\] is a declared channel of v0.2).  
　(1) I\_PD-type signature: zero initial state; both arms of q12; B̄ of PF large enough. If A\_p·P₁² \< α\_lo and A\_p·P₂² \> α\_hi, and g(m·A\_p·P₂²) + 1/T \< g(m·α\_hi), g(m·α\_lo) + 1/T \< g(m·A\_p·P₁²), then in both arms the blockade period is {b}^m, and on the release trial arm 1 gives {b} and arm 2 gives {a}. Proof: during the blockade a does not open; VST writes depth A\_p·P\_i² into a each trial, while b opens alone first and deepens by α\_b each trial; at release the depth orders of the two arms are opposite, and Lemma 3-2 applies. ∎ (2) I\_B-type signature: give PF a small B̄; when the protocol does not lift the blockade, a opens after self-release. ∎  
　Non-equality: N12's accumulated head does not enter depth before release, leaks at rate μ\_H, and is transferred all at once at release; M\_VP's writes enter depth already during the blockade and shallow at rate λ. Rest 6 beats after the last blockade trial and then lift (μ\_H = 1/2, λ = 1/10, A\_p = 1/4, ρ\_H = 1/4, κ\_s = 1): at release N12 has d\_a = 1/64 and M\_VP has d\_a = 781441/1000000; on the release trial N12 gives {b} and M\_VP gives {a, b}. Hence M\_VP reproduces N12 only at the signature level and is not an exact expression of N12.  
　Verification: Appendix B.5 E4.  
Coverage verdict (N12): relative to C\_cmp v0.1: not expressible (Theorem N12-4), read only as v0.1 lacking mechanisms of breakage, recovery and strength dependence. Relative to C\_cmp v0.2: proven coverage (W-instance layer, c1(C16, DAM), depth 1); at the signature level the signatures can also be reproduced by a depth-2 composition of neural-type components. Literature layer: structural clues (dam-break peak discharge increasing with reservoir volume and head; single floods cutting channels). Not reported as a new dynamics.  
Conditional predictions (B): P-N12-1 (Theorems N12-1, N12-2): for channel or flow-path objects with a single entry and two paths, in which one path can be blocked while the inflow is maintained and first opening can be read: the longer the blockade, the greater the advantage of the formerly blocked path after release (when γ \> 0), or, if the blockade lasts long enough, the path breaks through by itself; the detour path is deepened during the blockade, so a test must read both paths; excludes C16 v0.2, HOMB and N3. P-N12-2 (Theorem N12-4): two groups with different inflow intensities during the blockade give the same detour readings, while the bias after release varies with the inflow intensity during the blockade; excludes all of C\_cmp v0.1, but not the intensity-dependent components of v0.2. On hydraulic hosts this is a prediction of a known mechanism type; on neural hosts (a pathway whose output is blocked while it continues to be driven is stronger after release, depending on the drive intensity during the blockade) it is entered as a not-yet-registered prediction of the application of a known mechanism type to a new host.  
Breach: under the hypotheses, a release trial other than {a} (Theorem N12-2), or a release trial inconsistent with Theorem N12-4 (1); with rest 0, a time of breakthrough during the blockade that disagrees with the closed form; a run on which N12 and M\_D differ in reading or state on some protocol (breaching Theorem N12-5); a member of C\_cmp v0.1 that violates I\_B or I\_PD (breaching the lemmas or exposing a gap in the contract).  
6.7  Share-Proportional Write under Flow Division: Candidate N13, “Mutual Construction of Tendency and Shape” (Φ15)  
Problem: Inner Chapter 15: “Terrain shapes the flow; flow in return shapes the form”; “By tendency shape is constructed; by shape the distribution of tendency changes. This indeed is the nature of the Sedimentation-Field.” This runs both ways: shape (the depth of each path) determines how tendency is distributed among the paths, and the tendency each path receives in turn constructs shape. K\_F contains only the one-way half: K6 lets a path's depth set only that path's threshold, and K1 lets passage deepen only the path passed.  
Definition 6-6 (N13): W16-E with no new input. State d\_x ≥ 0 (no capacity limit). Parameters ν ∈ {1, 2, 3}, θ₀ ∈ (0, 1/2\], α \> 0, λ ∈ (0, 1\], the same for both paths. Each trial: entry flow pressure p\_t = t·P/T; a presented path receives the share w\_x = (1 + d\_x)^ν / Σ\_{y ∈ Pres}(1 + d\_y)^ν; x opens at the first grid step with w\_x·p\_t ≥ θ₀, i.e. τ\_x = ⌈θ₀·T/(w\_x·P)⌉ (when ≤ T); Y and the stop convention as in W16-E; when x ∈ Y, d\_x ← d\_x + α·w\_x; rest d ← (1 − λ)d. When the presentation set is empty, read Y = ∅ with the state unchanged. Equivalently, x has the effective threshold θ\_x^eff = θ₀·Σ\_{y ∈ Pres}(1 + d\_y)^ν/(1 + d\_x)^ν, which depends on this trial's presentation set and on the depth of the other path.  
Proposition N13-1 (single-path presentation does not read depth): when Pres = {x}, w\_x = 1 and τ\_x = ⌈θ₀T/P⌉ (when ≤ T), independent of all state; on passage d\_x increases by α. ∎ Control: in C16 v0.2, τ\_x under single-path presentation is nonincreasing in d\_x. (Appendix B.5 E5: with d\_a = 3 and with d\_a = 0, only a presented for three trials each; the first-opening steps are all 20)  
Proposition N13-2 (order under two-path presentation)  
　Notation: Pres = {a, b}; continuous first-arrival quantity q\_x = θ₀·T/(w\_x·P); discrete first-opening step τ\_x = ⌈q\_x⌉, reachable when q\_x ≤ T, otherwise written ∞.  
　(1) Strict order of the continuous quantities: d\_a \> d\_b ⇔ w\_a \> w\_b ⇔ q\_a \< q\_b; d\_a = d\_b ⇔ q\_a = q\_b.  
　(2) Weak order of the discrete first-opening steps (forward direction): d\_a ≥ d\_b ⇒ τ\_a ≤ τ\_b; if τ\_b is reachable then τ\_a is reachable; if τ\_a = ∞ then τ\_b = ∞. Proof: by (1), q\_a ≤ q\_b; the ceiling is monotone; reachability depends only on whether ⌈q⌉ ≤ T. ∎  
　(3) The converse fails: τ\_a ≤ τ\_b does not imply d\_a ≥ d\_b, because rounding maps different continuous quantities to the same beat. Example: ν = 1, θ₀ = 13/50, P = 1, T = 10, d = (0, 1/100): q\_a = 2613/500, q\_b = 2613/505, τ\_a = τ\_b = 6, Y = {a, b}, yet d\_a \< d\_b. Hence, as long as at least one path is reachable, Y contains every path of maximal depth (by (2), a path of maximal depth is then reachable), and it may also contain a shallower path (a rounding tie); when neither path is reachable, Y = ∅ (see (6)).  
　(4) The strict discrete order can be inverted: τ\_a \< τ\_b ⇒ d\_a \> d\_b. Proof: when τ\_b is reachable, ⌈q\_a⌉ \< ⌈q\_b⌉ implies q\_a \< q\_b; when τ\_b = ∞ and τ\_a is reachable, q\_a ≤ T \< q\_b; then apply (1). ∎  
　(5) A grid condition that excludes ties: if q\_b − q\_a ≥ 1 and τ\_a is reachable, then τ\_a \< τ\_b and Y = {a} (Lemma 3-3). Example: with the same parameters and d = (0, 1), q\_a = 39/5, q\_b = 39/10, τ\_a = 8, τ\_b = 4, Y = {b}.  
　(6) Reachability: the path whose depth is not smaller has w ≥ 1/2, so when P ≥ 2θ₀ its q ≤ T and it is reachable; the shallower path may be unreachable, in which case Y contains only the deeper one; both paths are unreachable if and only if the q of the path whose depth is not smaller exceeds T, in which case Y = ∅.  
　(7) Equal depth: when d\_a = d\_b and the paths are reachable, Y = {a, b}, and each path increases by α/2.  
　(8) I1 holds under two-path presentation: if Y\_k = {x} (rest 0, the same P, both paths presented), then d\_x \> d\_o by (4); after passage d\_x increases and d\_o is unchanged, so q\_x falls and q\_o rises, hence τ\_x does not increase and τ\_o does not decrease, and Y\_{k+1} = {x}. ∎  
　Verification: Appendix B.6 F1 (both examples match number for number; among 20000 random pairs there are no counterexamples to (2), (4), (5) or (6), while the converse case “τ\_a ≤ τ\_b but d\_a \< d\_b” occurs 2755 times).  
Theorem N13-3 (four-arm signature S13)  
　Protocol q13(m, d\_b0, P′, P, T): the same initial state (d\_a, d\_b) = (0, d\_b0), four independent runs. Arm 1A: one direct test with both paths presented, drive P; Arm 1B: one direct test with only b presented, drive P; Arms 2A and 2B: first present only a m times (drive P′, rest 0), then perform the two-path test and the b-only test, respectively. Readings: (Y, τ\_min) of each test trial. Signature S13: b ∈ Y in Arm 1A; τ\_min of Arm 2A is strictly greater than that of Arm 1A; Arms 1B and 2B have the same τ.  
　Hypotheses: N13 member; P′ ≥ θ₀, P ≥ 2θ₀; d\_b0 ≥ m·α; θ₀·T·\[(1 + mα)^ν − 1\]/((1 + d\_b0)^ν·P) ≥ 1.  
　Conclusion: N13 exhibits S13 on q13.  
　Proof: each pretraining trial of arm 2 presents only a, so w\_a = 1 and τ\_a = ⌈θ₀T/P′⌉ ≤ T; a opens and increases by α, so after m trials d\_a = mα, while d\_b = d\_b0 is unchanged. The b-only test: by Proposition N13-1, τ\_b is ⌈θ₀T/P⌉ in both arms. The two-path test: d\_b0 ≥ d\_a (in both arms), so by Proposition N13-2 (2) τ\_b ≤ τ\_a, b ∈ Y and τ\_min = τ\_b = ⌈θ₀T(1 + ρ^ν)/P⌉, with ρ = 1/(1 + d\_b0) in arm 1 and ρ = (1 + mα)/(1 + d\_b0) in arm 2. The two quantities inside the ceilings differ by θ₀T\[(1 + mα)^ν − 1\]/((1 + d\_b0)^ν P) ≥ 1, so by Lemma 3-3 arm 2 is strictly greater. ∎  
　Verification: Appendix B.5 E5 (witness ν = 2, θ₀ = 1/4, α = 1/4, d\_b0 = 2, m = 2, P = P′ = 1, T = 400: two-path test {b}@112 \| {b}@125, b-only test 100 \| 100; of 600 random runs, the hypotheses hold in 590, and all of these exhibit S13).  
Theorem N13-4 (relative to C\_cmp v0.1 and v0.2: no member exhibits S13)  
　Conclusion: no member of C\_cmp v0.1 or v0.2 (under both the strict and the broad reading, with any composition depth and number of leaves, and any parameters) exhibits S13 on q13, for any parameters of q13 and any initial state.  
　Proof: by Proposition 4-5, in any trial the threshold θ\_x of each path of any member is a function only of the pre-trial state S and does not read this trial's Pres; both paths share the same bias h(S), and τ\_x(S) is uniquely given by the grid. In q13 the states before the test trials of Arms 1A and 1B are both S₁, and those of Arms 2A and 2B are both S₂; there is no blockade in the tests, and the self-release in ①′ does not depend on Pres. Hence τ of the b-only test is τ\_b(S\_i), and τ\_min of the two-path test is min(τ\_a(S\_i), τ\_b(S\_i)). If b ∈ Y in Arm 1A, then τ\_min of Arm 1A is τ\_b(S₁); if the b-only tests of the two arms agree, τ\_b(S₂) = τ\_b(S₁); then τ\_min of Arm 2A ≤ τ\_b(S₂) = τ\_b(S₁) = τ\_min of Arm 1A, contradicting the second clause of S13. ∎  
　Reading: the proof uses only the single fact that “the threshold is computed only from the pre-trial state”, and does not use the depth bound. In every model whose “per-path threshold does not read the current presentation”, the first-opening step of b presented alone bounds from above the first-opening step under two-path presentation; N13's flow division lets the shape of a press down the tendency b receives when both paths are presented together, breaking through this bound.  
　Verification: Appendix B.5 E5 (in the four-arm witness, none of C16, HS and CMP exhibits S13; of 300 runs each for the 11 classes of v0.1 and the 6 classes of v0.2, 0 exhibit S13; a schematic construction outside the contract—a threshold term κ·min(1, d\_o/D\_φ)·1\[o ∈ Pres\] added to C16's threshold, which reads this trial's presentation set—at once reproduces S13).  
　Limits: the violated property, “the threshold is independent of this trial's presentation set”, fails by definition in two classes of known mechanisms, parallel flow-division reinforcement and divisive normalization, neither of which is included in v0.1 or v0.2. So this theorem shows only that v0.2 lacks flow-division normalization; it is not evidence of a new dynamics.  
Theorem N13-5 (relative to C\_cmp v0.3: N13 ≡ c2(KIR, 1\[x ∈ Y\]))  
　Construction: M\_K = c2(KIR, 1\[x ∈ Y\]), μ = 1, depth 1, 1 leaf, valid under the strict reading; it contains no C16 core (D3.2). The parameter map is the identity: (ν, θ₀, α, λ) ↦ the parameters of the same names in KIR; initial state d ↦ d.  
　Conclusion: for every protocol whose presentation sets are nonempty (arbitrary P, Pres, rest) and every initial state, N13 and M\_K have the same readings and states trial by trial; when the presentation set is empty, both read Y = ∅ with the state unchanged.  
　Proof: by induction on trials. The shares w\_x and s\_x are given by the same formula over the same pool (N13 has no blockade, so Π = Pres); τ\_x is given by the same formula, so Y and τ\_min agree; the write is in both cases “d\_x += α·w\_x when x ∈ Y, otherwise unchanged”; the rest law is the same. ∎  
　Membership (D3.6): an M\_K corresponding to an N13 with ν ∈ {1, 2, 3}, θ₀ ∈ \[1/12, 1/2\], α ∈ \[0, 1\], λ ∈ \[1/12, 1\] is a member of C\_cmp v0.3; outside the box the identity still holds.  
　Verification: Appendix B.6 F2 (n13\_run and the independent implementation kir\_run identical on 1500 random runs).  
　Levels: at the W-instance layer coverage is proved; at the literature layer the share formula is identical, equation for equation, to equations (1)–(3) of Tero et al., and at ν = 1, μ = 1 the reinforcement is identical, equation for equation, to one Euler step of equation (4), while the object on which decay acts, the threshold read-out and the gating of reinforcement by Y are readings or compositions on W (the item “gating reinforcement by Y” is a c2 composition chosen to align with N13)—so the literature layer is a partial equation-by-equation alignment. In addition, the original KIR (every path in the pool reinforced) and DNC also exhibit S13 (Appendix B.6 F2: 293 and 41 of 300 random runs each), so S13 is not a signature exclusive to N13; the original KIR is not trial-by-trial equal to N13.  
Coverage verdict (N13): relative to C\_cmp v0.1 and v0.2, not expressible (Theorem N13-4, read only as lacking flow-division normalization); relative to C\_cmp v0.3, proven coverage (W-instance layer, depth 1); at the literature layer, partial equation-by-equation alignment with parallel flow-division reinforcement and structural alignment with divisive normalization. Propositions N13-1 and N13-2 and Theorem N13-3 are properties of N13 and have no bearing on coverage; the four-arm protocol q13, as a diagnostic of “flow-division type versus per-path-threshold type”, is effective against all of v0.1 and v0.2, but does not separate N13 from the flow-division-normalization class (no protocol separating N13 from DNC has been established). Not reported as a new dynamics.  
Conditional predictions (B): P-N13-1 (four-arm diagnostic, Theorem N13-3): for objects with two paths sharing a common entry (channel bifurcations, branches of a slime-mould tube network, two input pathways sharing a normalization pool), in which the two paths can be presented separately (split-path input), first opening can be read, and the object can be reset to the declared initial state or replaced by homogeneous independent groups: in arm 1, when both paths are tested together, b is in the first-opened set; after only a has been trained several times, τ\_min of the joint two-path test is delayed; when b is presented alone, its first-opening step is unchanged; the four arms must be four independent runs; excludes all of C\_cmp v0.1 and v0.2, including “strengthening a draws resources away from b” (CMP: the b-alone test is delayed too) and “strengthening a facilitates b” (HS: both tests come earlier). P-N13-2 (Proposition N13-1): the first-opening step under single-path presentation is independent of that path's training history; the training history shows only when both paths are presented together. On hydraulic hosts, the division of flow among parallel branches in proportion to conductance is a physical fact of Kirchhoff type, and P-N13-1 is a prediction of a known mechanism type.  
Breach: under the hypotheses, S13 does not appear (Theorem N13-3); a member satisfying Definition 4-D1 or 4-D2 exhibits S13 on q13 (breaching Theorem N13-4 or exposing a gap in ① of contract D1.4); the first opening under single-path presentation changes with the state (Proposition N13-1); N13 and M\_K differ on some protocol whose presentation sets are nonempty (Theorem N13-5).  
6.8  History-Modulated Write by a Shared Neuromodulator: Candidate N14, “Coloring Biased-Flow” (Φ10)  
Problem: in K7b, Coloring has only its original meaning. Outer Chapter 9 and Inner Chapter 17 say of Coloring: “attached to Imagery, clinging upon it”; “From bias—action: now approaching, now avoiding. This is approach and avoidance; this is the use of Coloring”; it “colors the Imagery of other circuits”; “Like and dislike stand at two ends”; “Emotion sediments in patterns within the Coloring-Field; reuse deepens, sparse use fades”; “Inclination … coloring and adding inclination. It adheres to Imagery, sinks into the Experience-Field and alters the experiential Biased-Flow.” The key points: Coloring attaches to Imagery, not to any one path; it has two ends, like and dislike; it does not decide opening directly, but changes the amount and direction written into the Experience-Field on passage; it itself deepens with reuse and fades with sparse use. C16 v0.2 reads no affective input; TF3, RW and TWO, which read reinforcement input, all write only to the path that is passed.  
Definition 6-7 (N14, on W16-E″): state d\_x ≥ 0 (the C16 v0.2 laws, capacity ∞), Coloring c ∈ \[−c̄, c̄\] (attached to Imagery, shared by both paths, initial value 0). Parameters κ \> 0, β \> 0, c̄ \> 0, μ\_c ∈ \[0, 1\]. Each trial: threshold g(d\_x) (Coloring does not change the threshold directly); Y as in W16-E; when x ∈ Y, d\_x ← max(0, d\_x + α·(1 + κ·c)) (the write reads the pre-trial c); after a trial with Y ≠ ∅, c ← clip(c + β·e, −c̄, c̄); rest c ← (1 − μ\_c)c, d ← (1 − λ)d.  
Proposition N14-1 (cross-path transfer): first present only a m times (e = σ, P ≥ g0, rest 0); then a opens every time, and afterwards c = clip(m·β·σ, −c̄, c̄); thereafter present only b (e = 0); each passage of b writes α(1 + κc).  
　Proof: under single-path presentation θ\_a ≤ g0 ≤ P, so a opens; c increases by β·σ (with clipping) in every trial with an opening; in the tests of b, e = 0 and rest is 0, so c is unchanged. ∎  
Proposition N14-2 (avoidance reversal): if σ = −1 and 1 − κ·c⁻ \< 0 (c⁻ = min(mβ, c̄)), then each subsequent passage of b decreases d\_b by α(κc⁻ − 1) (down to 0). If moreover d\_b0 + α(1 − κc⁻) ≥ 0 and T/P·\[g(d\_b0 + α(1 − κc⁻)) − g(d\_b0)\] ≥ 1, then b's first-opening step in the second test is strictly greater than in the first; in the control arm with e = 0, b's first-opening step does not increase.  
　Proof: by Proposition N14-1 and Lemma 3-3. ∎  
　Verification: Appendix B.6 F3 (three-arm witness with κ = 1/2, β = 1/4, c̄ = 2, 4 pretraining trials, initial state (0, 1), T = 40; in the e = −1 arm c = −1 and 1 − κc⁻ = 1/2 \> 0, so the rate is halved but not reversed; all 377 random runs in which the hypotheses of Proposition N14-2 hold conform).  
Theorem N14-3 (two-arm signature S14)  
　Protocol q14(m, n, d\_b0, P, T): the same initial state (0, d\_b0), one independent run per arm; pretraining presents only a m times (u = 0, rest 0), with e = +1 in Arm A and e = 0 in Arm B; thereafter only b is presented n times (e = 0, u = 0, rest 0). Readings: (Y, τ\_min) of each trial. Signature S14: the first-opened sets of the two arms' pretraining are the same trial by trial and nonempty; the readings of b's first test are the same in both arms; some later test reads differently.  
　Hypotheses: N14 member; P ≥ g0; n ≥ 2; writing c⁺ = min(mβ, c̄), T/P·\[g(d\_b0 + α) − g(d\_b0 + α(1 + κc⁺))\] ≥ 1.  
　Conclusion: N14 exhibits S14 on q14.  
　Proof: in pretraining both arms present only a, and a always opens, so the first-opened set is {a} in both; b is not written, so before the first test d\_b = d\_b0 in both arms and τ\_b is ⌈g(d\_b0)T/P⌉ in both. After the first test, Arm A has d\_b = d\_b0 + α(1 + κc⁺) and Arm B has d\_b = d\_b0 + α; by the hypotheses and Lemma 3-3, the readings of the second test differ. ∎  
　Verification: Appendix B.6 F3 (all 539 random runs in which the hypotheses hold exhibit S14).  
Definition 6-8 (whole-protocol static non-interference class Λ\_e^S)  
　Dependence: static (syntactic) dependence, counted from the member's composition tree and the laws of its leaf components, regardless of whether a branch is executed in a given protocol; every read that appears in a law counts (including gating variables, the switching events of c4 and the driving events of c3).  
　Universal domain: all members of C\_cmp v0.3″ and v0.4, all parameter values, all initial states; all trials of protocol q14 (both the pretraining and the test phase, and every state reachable in between).  
　State ownership: each state variable (leaf-component states, c3 traces, c4 mode variables together with their switching and resetting, c2 gating variables, event flags) is private to a, private to b, or shared: a variable read and written only by the laws of one path is private to that path; a variable read by the laws of both paths, or written by both, is shared; a c4 mode variable has the same ownership as the variables written by the laws it switches.  
　Λ\_e^S: the members whose composition tree statically satisfies the following four conditions—(N1) a law that reads e writes only private variables of the path being passed; (N2) no law that writes a variable private to b or shared has among its reads (including gating variables) a variable private to a; (N3) for every c4 that switches a law writing a variable private to b or shared, and every c3 trace that drives b or shared variables, the event is not an event on a and the condition does not read variables private to a; (N4) inherent cross-path reads read only Y, or read the other path only when the pool contains several paths (in q14 the pool always consists of a single path), or read only variables unaffected by e. All four are static conditions on the laws, apply uniformly in both phases, and serve as class antecedents.  
Lemma N14-L1 (preservation of Λ\_e^S under the operators; sufficiency only): suppose all members entering the composition satisfy (N1)–(N4). (i) c1 preserves the class. (ii) c2: if the gated law writes a variable private to b or shared, the gating variable must not be private to a, and (under the strict reading) if it is e, the gated law must write only to the path being passed—under this condition c2 preserves the class. (iii) c3: preserves it if the events driving b's traces or shared traces satisfy (N3). (iv) c4: preserves it if (N3) is satisfied. ∎ A c4 that violates (N3) may still leave non-interference intact when the two switched laws are identical or the switch is unreachable; this lemma does not claim necessity.  
Theorem N14-4 (members of Λ\_e^S do not exhibit S14)  
　Conclusion: for every member of Λ\_e^S (any parameters, any initial state), on q14, if the first-opened sets of the two arms' pretraining are the same trial by trial and nonempty, then all test readings of b are the same in both arms; hence S14 does not appear.  
　Proof: only the class antecedents (N1)–(N4) are used. Let V\_B be the variables private to b together with the shared variables. By (N1)–(N4), the reads of the law of any variable in V\_B consist only of V\_B itself, the input channels that are the same in both arms (Y, Pres, u, P, z, Brk), and reads of e when the law “writes only to the path being passed”; in q14 b is passed only in the test phase, where e = 0 in both arms; the events that switch laws in V\_B are not events on a. So by induction over all trials: if V\_B is the same in both arms at the start of a trial, the updates and switches of V\_B in that trial are the same in both arms; in pretraining Y is the same by the premise, and in the tests only b is presented and b's first opening depends only on V\_B, so Y is again the same. Hence the readings are the same. ∎  
　Scope of the class: the broad reading has not been removed—members under the broad reading that do not gate b's laws or shared laws by variables private to a remain in Λ\_e^S; what is excluded is only members that “gate, switch or drive b's laws and shared laws by the state of a”. A dynamic non-interference condition that governs only the pretraining phase is not enough to support this conclusion (see out-of-class control (2) below); a semantic (dynamic, two-phase) version of non-interference remains open (U16-32). Members containing no c3 or c4 need only (N1), (N2) and (N4).  
　Out-of-class controls (retained): (1) c4 clipping switch: a's clipping event switches b's write law through c4 (violating (N3)); (2) delayed-transmission gating: a uses TF3e (initial state 0, capacity 1/2, d\_a ← clip(d\_a + e, 0, 1/2) when a opens), and b writes (1/2)·d\_a only when b opens (broad-reading c2: b's K1 gated by the state of a, violating (N2)); in pretraining a's first openings are the same in both arms, and neither b nor the shared state has changed; in the tests only b is presented, e = 0, P = 1, T = 100: b's first-opening steps are 100 80 \| 100 100, so S14 appears—the difference reaches b through the state of a only in the test phase (Appendix B.7 G1, Appendix B.8 H2).  
　Verification: Appendix B.6 F3 (300 random runs each; C16, TF3e, RWe, HS, STF and TF3e ⊕ HS ⊕ STF exhibit S14 in 0 runs each; N14 in 294).  
　Constructions outside Λ\_e^S (registered, not used as grounds for non-coverage): X1 = c2(HS, 1\[e = +1\]) (strict reading): it deepens b directly during pretraining, so b's first test usually already differs; this is the “offset type”; X2 = c2(STF, 1\[e = +1\]) (strict reading): 2 of 300 runs exhibit S14, and in the witness the first test already differs, so it is also of offset type; X3 (broad reading: each path has an RWe-type value state V\_x, updated V\_x ← V\_x + η\_v(e − V\_x) when that path is passed, and the K1 increment of either path is multiplied by clip(a₀ + b₁·V\_oth, 0, 1)): 291 of 300 runs exhibit S14; like N14 it is of “rate type”, but it is not trial-by-trial equal to N14 (its values are stored per path and updated only when that path is passed, whereas N14's Coloring is shared by both paths and accumulates by e in every trial with an opening) (Appendix B.6 F3, F4).  
Theorem N14-5 (relative to C\_cmp v0.4: N14 ≡ c1(C16 v0.2 core, IZH))  
　Construction: M\_I = c1(C16 v0.2 core, IZH), depth 1, 2 leaves, valid under the strict reading (IZH reads e and Y and uses no gating). Parameter map: (g0, c, α, λ) ↦ the parameters of the same names in the C16 core; α\_I = α·κ, ε\_D = β, D̄ = c̄, μ\_D = μ\_c; initial state (d, c = 0) ↦ (d, D = 0).  
　Conclusion: for every protocol on W16-E″ (arbitrary P, Pres, rest, e) and every initial state, N14 and M\_I have the same readings and states (d; c and D) trial by trial.  
　Proof: by induction on trials. The threshold is g(d\_x) in both; Y and τ\_min agree; the write is in both cases d\_x + α + ακ·c clipped to ≥ 0 (only for x ∈ Y; the single clipping of D4.2); c and D are updated by the same formula at the same point; the rest law is the same. ∎  
　Membership (D3.6): only those M\_I that correspond to an N14 falling inside the D4.3 box (ακ ≤ 2, β ≤ 1, c̄ ∈ \[1/2, 3\], and so on) are members of v0.4; outside the box the identity still holds.  
　Verification: Appendix B.7 G2 (1500 random runs, all identical).  
　Levels: constructive coverage at the W-instance layer is proved; literature-layer coverage is not—the three items of IZH “immediate eligibility, decay only at rest, clipping” are aligned with N14's laws (4.6), and the eligibility trace and moment-by-moment decay of the literature are taken in IZH as degenerate cases. A variant closer to the literature, IZHc, exhibits S14 (286 of 300 random runs) but is not trial-by-trial equal to N14 (witness: first-opening steps when only b is tested 8 times, N14 400 291 229 189 160 140 124 111, IZHc 400 305 245 206 178 158 142 130—IZHc's neuromodulator level decays trial by trial during the tests). The performance-type components TON and PIT exhibit S14 in 5 and 0 of 300 random runs respectively, and in their witnesses the first test already differs (they act on performance and do not change writing; finite verification, not a theorem).  
Definition 6-9 (broadcast-integrator family BIM): local writes modulated by a signed, saturating, event-driven broadcast integrator (a model family declared in this paper, not called the complete model of any work in the literature). Parameters (g0, c, α, λ; s, M\_lo, M\_hi, μ), M\_lo \< 1 \< M\_hi. States d\_x ≥ 0 and a shared M ∈ \[M\_lo, M\_hi\] (initial value 1). Each trial: threshold g(d\_x), Y as in W16-E; when x ∈ Y, d\_x ← max(0, d\_x + α·M) (reading the pre-trial M); when Y ≠ ∅, M ← clip(M + s·e, M\_lo, M\_hi); in each rest beat M ← 1 + (1 − μ)(M − 1), d ← (1 − λ)d.  
Theorem N14-6 (N14 ≡ the symmetric members of BIM): let M = 1 + κc. Then N14 (κ, β, c̄, μ\_c) and BIM (s = κβ, M\_lo = 1 − κc̄, M\_hi = 1 + κc̄, μ = μ\_c) agree trial by trial on every valid protocol.  
　Proof: c ↦ 1 + κc is an order-preserving affine bijection (κ \> 0) that maps \[−c̄, c̄\] onto \[M\_lo, M\_hi\] and commutes with interval clipping: 1 + κ·clip(c + βe, −c̄, c̄) = clip(1 + κc + κβe, M\_lo, M\_hi); at rest 1 + κ(1 − μ)c = 1 + (1 − μ)(M − 1); the write α(1 + κc) = αM; initial state c = 0 ↔ M = 1; the threshold and read-out do not involve c. Induction on trials, keeping the order “the write reads the old value, then the update”. ∎  
　Corollary (identifiability): N14's readings depend only on (g0, c, α, λ, κβ, κc̄, μ\_c); along the direction (κ, β, c̄) ↦ (tκ, β/t, c̄/t) (t \> 0) the model is not identifiable. Every member of BIM whose bounds are symmetric about 1 (1 − M\_lo = M\_hi − 1) is some N14; members with asymmetric bounds are not in N14.  
　Verification: Appendix B.8 H3 (e\_run, bim\_run and the independent implementation n14\_run agree on readings and final states in 1500 random runs, with M = 1 + κc). This theorem does not prove that N14 is the same as any complete biological model.  
Definition 6-10 (IZD: a fixed discretization of the Izhikevich 2007 model): the interface, sampling, time and parameters are fixed once and written down before the comparison.
