Time: one trial = one 100 ms window; one rest beat = one 100 ms window without activity; decay factors ρ\_c = 9048/10000 (≈ exp(−0.1/1)), ρ\_d = 6065/10000 (≈ exp(−0.1/0.2)). Interface: a “passage” is read as one pre-before-post pairing, giving E\_x += A₊ (A₊ = 1); the depression branch is not represented; when e = +1 and the trial has an opening, D += 1/2; e = −1 has no counterpart; tonic source s₀ = 1/1000 per window; the initial value of D is the tonic steady state D\* = s₀/(1 − ρ\_d). Write: in each window w\_x ← clip(w\_x + κ\_w·Δt·E\_x·D, 0, 4), κ\_w = 10, Δt = 1/10; depth is the weight, threshold g(w\_x). Order: ① threshold ② Y ③ eligibility jump and dopamine release ④ write (all paths) ⑤ E ← ρ\_c E, D ← ρ\_d D + s₀; a rest beat performs only ④⑤. Registered deviations: four-digit rational approximations in place of the exponentials; “passage” in place of STDP pairing; no dopamine-independent writing; “dislike” not represented; a single Euler step within a window. IZD exhibits S14 on q14 (Appendix B.8 H4: 300/300 random runs), so S14 does not separate N14 from IZD.  
Comparison of N14 with Izhikevich 2007 (local eligibility E\_x, shared neuromodulator D, write w\_x): shared historical state—N14's c (or M) is broadcast to all paths, accumulates βe within a trial only when there is an opening, and decays only in rest beats; Izhikevich's D is broadcast to all synapses, decays continuously and is driven by a tonic source: the structure “a shared neuromodulatory quantity with a history regulates local writes” is not unique to N14. Local eligibility—N14 takes this trial's 1\[x ∈ Y\], not retained across trials; Izhikevich's is per synapse and decays with τ\_c: they differ and cannot be interchanged without proof. Writing without local activity—N14 has none; Izhikevich's model keeps changing while E\_x and D are both nonzero: an explicit timing difference (protocol B). Dopamine-independent writing—N14 has the baseline α; Izhikevich has none. Sign—N14's M can lie on either side of 1; Izhikevich's d is a nonnegative concentration with no counterpart of “dislike”. “Like and dislike gate only through the current-beat signal” holds only for the W reading TF3e and for c2 gated by e; existing three-factor mechanisms cannot all be described as reading only the current-beat signal; Izhikevich's original model belongs to the group “a learned shared neuromodulator level gates writing”.  
Proposition N14-7 (protocol B: a waiting segment without local activity)  
　The two sides: N14 (any parameters; a window without presentation is a trial with Pres = ∅, which brings no decay; decay occurs only in rest beats); IZD (the fixed parameters of Definition 6-10, g0 and c arbitrary).  
　Protocol q\_B(K): zero initial state; trial 1 presents only b, e = +1, P ≥ g0 (b opens); then K windows without presentation; the final trial presents only b, e = 0, and τ\_b is read.  
　Conclusion: (1) N14: τ\_b(K) is independent of K (with Pres = ∅, Y = ∅: no write, no update of c, no rest); if rest beats are used for the wait instead, d\_b only decreases and never increases, and τ\_b(K) is nondecreasing. (2) IZD: before the wait E\_b = A₊ρ\_c \> 0 and D \> D\* \> 0; during the wait w\_b increases in each window by κ\_w·Δt·E\_b·D \> 0 (until the upper bound), and before the final test w\_b(K) = w\_b(0) + Σ\_{j=0}^{K−1} κ\_wΔt·E\_b^{(j)}D^{(j)} (ignoring the upper bound), where E\_b^{(j)} = ρ\_c^{j}·A₊ρ\_c, D^{(j)} − D\* = ρ\_d^{j+1}/2; as long as T/P·\[g(w\_b(0)) − g(w\_b(K))\] ≥ 1, the final test gives τ\_b(K) \< τ\_b(0).  
　Proof: (1) term by term from N14's laws; (2) term by term from IZD's window order; every term of the sum is positive. ∎  
　Verification: Appendix B.8 H5 (for K = 0, 3, 10, N14's final-test τ\_b is 320 in every case, IZD's is 267, 199, 189; in 500 random runs N14's τ\_b is unchanged in all, and IZD is strictly earlier in all 500 runs that satisfy the margin).  
　Breach: on a host satisfying the premises (the waiting segment really has no presentation and no other writing or shallowing, the local eligibility has not been cleared, and the shared quantity is nonzero), if waiting makes the final test earlier, N14 is refuted on that host; if the margin is satisfied and the final test is unchanged, “IZD with its listed parameters” is refuted (not every parametrization of the Izhikevich type). The conclusion is limited to the fixed IZD interface and does not extend to every discretization of the mechanism (U16-33).  
Proposition N14-8 (protocol C: selective reset of the shared state)  
　The three sides: N14; IZD; a c4 mode model (a uses TF3e: on passage d\_a ← clip(d\_a + e, 0, cap\_a); at a's clipping event (the raw increment exceeding cap\_a), c4 switches b's write coefficient from 0 to α\_on; no shared neuromodulatory state).  
　Protocol q\_C: the same initial state (d\_a0, d\_b0); pretraining presents only a m times (e = +1 and 0 in the two arms); before b's first test an intervention I is applied: only the shared neuromodulatory state is reset to baseline (N14: c ← 0; IZD: D ← D\*; the c4 mode model has no such state, and the intervention does not touch its mode variable), while depths, eligibility traces and mode variables are left untouched; thereafter only b is presented n times (e = 0).  
　(1) N14 and IZD: all test readings of b are the same in both arms. Proof: after the reset, the b-related states are the same in both arms (N14: d\_b and c = 0; IZD: w\_b, E\_b = 0, D = D\*), and the state of a does not enter b's reads or writes. ∎ This conclusion does not depend on the grid.  
　(2) The c4 mode model: the internal modes of the two arms differ (the like arm has a clipping event in pretraining and has switched; condition: a opens in pretraining and d\_a0 + 1 \> cap\_a); whether the finite readings differ must be decided separately. In the first test both arms give τ⁰ = ⌈T·g(d\_b0)/P⌉ (the mode does not change the threshold). After the first test (with the condition P ≥ g0 for b to open), the like arm has d\_b = d\_b0 + α\_on and the neutral arm d\_b = d\_b0, so the second test gives ⌈T·g(d\_b0 + α\_on)/P⌉ and ⌈T·g(d\_b0)/P⌉ in the two arms respectively: they differ if and only if these two ceilings differ; a sufficient condition is the grid margin T/P·\[g(d\_b0) − g(d\_b0 + α\_on)\] ≥ 1. The k-th test (k ≥ 2) gives ⌈T·g(d\_b0 + (k − 1)α\_on)/P⌉ and ⌈T·g(d\_b0)/P⌉ respectively, to be decided test by test from the actual accumulation. ∎ Coarse-grid example: d\_b0 = 0, α\_on = 1/4, g0 = c = 1, T = P = 1: the two arms differ in mode but both read 1 1; with T = 1 the first-opening step of every nonnegative depth is 1, so lengthening the test cannot separate them either. Fine-grid control, T = 100: 100 80 versus 100 100 (margin 20).  
　Reading: protocol C separates “the effect is stored in a shared neuromodulatory state” (N14, IZD) from “the effect is stored in an irreversible mode” (c4 type), on condition that the mode difference is resolvable on the grid used; it does not separate N14 from IZD (that is done by Proposition N14-7). Internal state difference, finite reading difference and certification of the material mechanism are kept apart: different modes do not mean different readings; different readings support only a compatible mode explanation and do not uniquely certify it.  
　Breach: only when all the exclusivity conditions (the intervention acts only on the shared neuromodulatory state, without erasing modes or changing depths and eligibility) and the resolvability condition (the ceilings differ, or the grid margin holds) are met do equal readings of the two arms after the reset breach this fixed c4 model; a general “the same after reset” cannot refute every mode-storage mechanism. If the readings of the two arms differ after the reset, N14 and the IZD type are refuted on that protocol, and the mode-storage type receives compatible (not unique) support.  
　Verification: Appendix B.8 H6 (witnesses for the three sides under both the no-reset and the reset condition; 300 random runs: N14 same in both arms 300, IZD same 300, c4 mode model different 300); Appendix B.9 I1 (two examples, coarse and fine grid; 3000 random runs with T ∈ {1, 2, 3, 5, 10, 40, 100}, P ∈ {1, 2}: “the second test differs ⇔ the two ceilings differ” and equality of the first test hold throughout; all 1025 runs in which the margin holds differ in the second test, and 477 of the 1975 runs in which it does not hold differ; in the reset branches of N14 and IZD (including T = 1), the two arms are identical in all 300 random runs); Appendix B.9 I3.  
Coverage verdict (N14): relative to Λ\_e^S of C\_cmp v0.3″: S14 does not appear (Theorem N14-4); relative to C\_cmp v0.4: constructive coverage at the W-instance layer is proved (Theorem N14-5, depth 1); literature-layer coverage is not proved (partially isomorphic; three W choices aligned with the candidate); N14 is a symmetric member of the declared family BIM (Theorem N14-6); it is separated from the fixed discretization IZD by protocol B (Proposition N14-7); shared state and mode storage are separated by protocol C under the resolvability condition (Proposition N14-8). Not reported as a new dynamics.  
Mechanism-hypothesis groups separated: H\_loc (Λ\_e^S: like and dislike gate the write of the path being passed only through the current-beat signal, as in the signed three-factor law and the W reading of per-path RW; together with cross-path crosstalk, competition and shared facilitation not driven by the history of like and dislike) versus H\_cowr (a learned shared neuromodulator level gates the writes of all paths: N14, IZH, IZHc, IZD, and X3 under the broad reading). The performance type H\_perf (TON, PIT) lies outside the conclusion on Λ\_e^S, and S14 does not exclude it as a theorem; within the range tried, H\_perf mostly shows a difference already at the first test (unproved).  
Conditional predictions (B): P-N14-1 (Theorem N14-3, Proposition N14-2): for pathways with two paths that can be presented separately for the same Imagery, where like/dislike input (reward and punishment, or their neuromodulatory surrogates) can be applied and first opening can be read: the first-opened sets of the two arms' pretraining are the same; thereafter only b is tested; the first test is the same in both arms, after which b's first-opening step advances sooner in the arm paired with “like”, and more slowly, or is even delayed test after test, in the arm paired with “dislike”; the two arms must be independent groups; excludes all of Λ\_e^S. P-N14-2 (divergence from the X3 type): first train m times on a paired with “like”, then m times on b paired with “dislike”, then test only b and compare with a control of the same depth that received no like/dislike training: in N14 the Coloring has cancelled out (c = 0 when c̄ is large enough and μ\_c = 0), and b's rate equals the control's; in X3 b's writing is still gated by the “like” on a. P-N14-3 (divergence from IZHc): after pretraining on a paired with “like”, in successive tests of b without like or dislike, the acceleration persists undiminished in N14 and IZH, whereas in IZHc it decays to baseline with the number of tests. Untested premises: neuromodulatory surrogates of like/dislike input; a waiting period without local activity can be imposed; the shared neuromodulatory state can be selectively reset without touching modes and depths.  
Breach: under the hypotheses, S14 does not appear (Theorem N14-3); a member of Λ\_e^S exhibits S14 on q14 (Theorem N14-4); N14 differs from M\_I or from a symmetric member of BIM on some protocol (Theorems N14-5, N14-6); the breaches listed under Propositions N14-7 and N14-8.  
6.9  Structural Position Generation When Full: Candidate N15, “Overflow Position Generation” (Φ17, on W16-F)  
Problem: the main book's *The Mathematical Form of the Field* v2.7: “When Domain Saturation is reached and new Trace-Responses still enter, unable to continue inward sinking, they overflow along the boundary: this is called Edge-Seepage”; “Successive Edge-Seepages generate new Structural Positions”; Axiom 16, the Edge-Seepage Axiom; Equation 10: “Full(𝒟) = 1, and π continues to enter 𝒟, and new Trace-Responses cannot continue inward sinking, then B(𝒟) = 1; new Trace-Responses overflow along the Domain boundary, and new Structural Positions are generated”; Definition 21: “The structural content carried by Edge-Seepage closely correlates with the structural content of the Domain core … Edge-Seepage requires: Domain Saturation already established, new Trace-Responses continuing to enter, and inability to continue inward sinking.” These provisions give the conditions for generation but no quantitative law (how much overflows, when generation occurs, how the new position relates to the original domain after generation). In C16 v0.2 a passage through a path that is already full leaves no trace and cannot generate a new position.  
Definition 6-11 (N15): parameter domain: thresholds and writes of a, b and ℓ as in C16 v0.2; σ \> 0, μ\_s ∈ \[0, 1\]; D̄\_a \< ∞ (generation after fullness requires that a can reach bottom), D̄\_b ∈ ℚ\>0 ∪ {∞}, D̄\_ℓ ∈ ℚ\>0 ∪ {∞}; birth-validity antecedent D̄\_ℓ ≥ α\_a (or D̄\_ℓ = ∞, a separate branch). Initial state: when ℓ does not exist initially, the generation amount is s₀ ∈ \[0, σ), and the counting theorem additionally requires s₀ = 0 (for the nonzero branch see Theorem N15-1 (2)); the readability Proposition N15-2 additionally allows ℓ to exist initially (E = 1, d\_ℓ ∈ \[0, D̄\_ℓ\], in which case no further generation occurs). Before ℓ is generated there is a generation amount s ≥ 0. In ③ of each trial: when x ∈ Y, d\_x ← min(d\_x + α\_x, D̄\_x); if a ∈ Y, d\_a = D̄\_a before the passage, and ℓ has not been generated, then s ← s + α\_a (overflow along the boundary); when s ≥ σ, ℓ is generated with d\_ℓ ← s − σ, and s ← 0. After generation ℓ follows C16 (α\_ℓ, D̄\_ℓ, λ) and is presented if and only if a is presented. ④ Rest: d shallows, and s ← (1 − μ\_s)s (Edge-Seepage must be successive; if it is interrupted, the overflow dissipates). N15 is registered as one candidate implementation model of the generation direction described by Axiom 16 and Equation 10; the quantitative law σ, where α goes, and the presentation linkage after generation are all model choices; whether this is the formalization of the main-book provisions is undetermined, and it is not included in K\_F.  
Theorem N15-1 (generation requires prior fullness and accumulates only through passages after fullness; the birth state is valid): assume the parameter domain of Definition 6-11, ℓ initially nonexistent, α\_a \> 0.  
　(1) Zero initial generation amount s₀ = 0: under any protocol, ℓ is generated in trial k only if, up to and including trial k, there have been at least ⌈σ/α\_a⌉ trials in which “a ∈ Y and a was already full before the passage”; when μ\_s = 0 and there is no rest between these trials, ℓ is generated exactly at the ⌈σ/α\_a⌉-th such trial.  
　(2) Nonzero initial generation amount 0 \< s₀ \< σ: the count above becomes ⌈(σ − s₀)/α\_a⌉; the rest is as in (1).  
　(3) Birth state: at birth 0 ≤ d\_ℓ = s − σ \< α\_a; hence when D̄\_ℓ ≥ α\_a (or D̄\_ℓ = ∞) the birth state is valid (d\_ℓ ∈ \[0, D̄\_ℓ\]).  
　(4) When α\_a = 0 and σ \> 0, ℓ is never generated.  
　Proof: s increases by α\_a only in trials in which “a ∈ Y and a was already full before the passage”, and does not increase at any other time (rest multiplies it by 1 − μ\_s ∈ \[0, 1\] and can only decrease it), and s ≥ σ is checked right after each increment. So before birth the cumulative increment of s is at least σ − s₀, which gives the lower bounds on the counts in (1) and (2); when μ\_s = 0 and there is no rest, s is exactly s₀ + jα\_a, which gives the exact equality. Birth occurs at the increment at which s ≥ σ first holds, and before that increment s \< σ (the initial value s₀ \< σ, and rest does not increase s), so s − σ \< α\_a, which gives (3). When α\_a = 0, s never increases, which gives (4). ∎  
　Out-of-domain and boundary controls: in Example J5a (s₀ = 9/10, σ = 1, α\_a = 1/10, μ\_s = 0, a initially full) birth occurs in trial 1—the count ⌈σ/α\_a⌉ = 10 of (1) does not apply when s₀ ≠ 0, while the count of (2) is 1, matching the run; in Example J5b (s₀ = 0, σ = 1, α\_a = 100, D̄\_ℓ = 1) the birth depth is 99 \> D̄\_ℓ, which violates the antecedent D̄\_ℓ ≥ α\_a and lies outside the valid domain of this theorem. The birth law and the clipping are unchanged; only the domain is restricted.  
　Verification: Appendix B.8 H1 (α\_a = 0, σ = 1/2, a already full, only a presented 30 times: no generation); Appendix B.10 J5 (Examples J5a and J5b; 2000 random runs in the valid domain: birth depths all in \[0, α\_a), birth trials never earlier than the count; 1000 random runs with μ\_s = 0 and no rest: exact equality). All runs for S15, Proposition N15-2 and Proposition N15-5 in Section 6 and the appendix take s₀ = 0, ℓ initially nonexistent (or, under Proposition N15-2, initially existing with d\_ℓ ≤ D̄\_ℓ), σ \> 0 and D̄\_ℓ ≥ α\_a, and so all lie in the valid domain.  
Proposition N15-2 (readability of the new position: how to read the discrete first-opening step)  
　Notation: the readable pool of this trial, Π, is the set of positions that exist, are presented (ℓ along with the presentation of a) and are not blocked; τ\_x = ⌈T·g(d\_x)/P⌉, written ∞ when \> T; q\_x = T·g(d\_x)/P.  
　(1) Characterization: ℓ ∈ Y if and only if ℓ ∈ Π, τ\_ℓ is reachable and τ\_ℓ = min\_{y∈Π} τ\_y (the minimum over all available positions, not merely a comparison with a). Proof: by the stop convention. ∎  
　(2) Rounding tie: if a and ℓ are both in Π, both reachable, τ\_ℓ = τ\_a, and this step attains the minimum over the whole pool (τ\_a ≤ τ\_y for all y ∈ Π), then Y ⊇ {a, ℓ}—this can happen even with d\_ℓ \< d\_a (the new position, though shallow, immediately ties for first opening); if a third path y has τ\_y \< τ\_a, neither a nor ℓ opens; if both lie outside the window, neither opens. Proof: by (1). ∎ Rounding-tie example (pool containing only a and ℓ): g0 = c = 1, D̄\_a = 1, D̄\_b = D̄\_ℓ = 2, α = (1, 0, 0), λ = 1/10, σ = 1/100, μ\_s = 0, initial state (1, 0), T = P = 1, only a presented in two consecutive trials: ℓ is generated in trial 1 with d\_ℓ = 99/100; in trial 2 g\_ℓ = 100/199 \> g\_a = 1/2, both ceilings are 1, and Y = {a, ℓ}. Third-path example: (d\_a, d\_b, d\_ℓ) = (1, 2, 99/100), T = 3, P = 1, two paths presented: first-opening steps (2, 1, 2), Y = {b}—a and ℓ tie but neither opens. Hence “ℓ never opens when d\_ℓ \< D̄\_a” is false.  
　(3) Per-beat sufficient conditions for hiddenness: in this trial ℓ ∉ Y if τ\_ℓ = ∞, or if there is y ∈ Π with τ\_y reachable and τ\_y \< τ\_ℓ; a sufficient condition for the latter, with grid margin, is q\_ℓ − q\_y ≥ 1 (Lemma 3-3). A continuous threshold difference g(d\_ℓ) − g(d\_y) \> 0 is not by itself enough to imply hiddenness.  
　(4) Long-run hiddenness: if ℓ already exists before the trial begins, no new positions are added thereafter and no other event changes the candidate pool (the blockade regime and the presentation set are fixed; W16-F has only one potential position, so an existing ℓ together with fixed presentation and blockade suffices), and Y = {a} (strictly) in that trial, with rest 0 in each subsequent trial and P unchanged, then ℓ never opens thereafter. Proof: when Y = {a}, only a is written; if a is full, d\_a is unchanged, and if not, d\_a increases and τ\_a does not increase; ℓ and b are not written and there is no rest, so τ\_ℓ and τ\_b are unchanged; the pool is unchanged; hence Y = {a} again in the next trial; induction. ∎ Example of birth changing the pool: (1, 0, 0), ℓ initially nonexistent, T = P = 1, only a presented twice: Y is {a}, then {a, ℓ}—the birth within trial 1 changed the pool, so the antecedent fails. With rest, the rounding relations can change and must be computed beat by beat; long-run hiddenness is not claimed.  
　(5) Visibility: when a is blocked and only a is presented, Π = {ℓ} (ℓ already existing), and Y = {ℓ} if P ≥ g0; when b is presented as well, compare by (1); ℓ is not necessarily visible. ∎  
　Birth, first entry into the readable pool and first opening are accounted for separately: k\_birth (ℓ is generated in ③ of trial k), k\_pool (at the earliest k\_birth + 1, and requiring that a be presented and ℓ not blocked), k\_open (the first opening of ℓ, k\_open ≥ k\_pool). With a rounding tie (T = 1) they are 1, 2, 2; with a strict step difference (T = 100), 1, 2, 6 (a blocked in trial 6). Generation can hold while ℓ remains invisible for a time, and ℓ can also become visible at once because of a rounding tie.  
　Verification: Appendix B.8 H1 (branches: rounding tie, strict step difference, outside the window, third path, a blocked; in 3000 random states (1) holds throughout; of these, 717 have Y = {a} strictly in the first trial, and ℓ opens 0 times in the subsequent 20 trials; accounting of the three events; n15\_run and t16g\_ext.s\_run identical on 1000 random runs); Appendix B.9 I2 (two examples; among 3000 random states with ℓ existing, 1169 are ties, and “both open ⇔ the tie step equals the whole-pool minimum” holds throughout; 1296 have Y = {a} strictly in the first trial, and ℓ opens 0 times in the subsequent 20 trials).  
Theorem N15-3 (signature S15)  
　Protocol q15(n): three arms, one independent run each, P ≥ g0, rest 0. Arm A: initial state d\_a = D̄\_a, only a presented n times; Arm B: the same initial state, only b presented n times; Arm C: initial state d\_a = 0 with D̄\_a \> n·α\_a, only a presented n times. In the final trial of each arm, a is blocked and only a is presented, and the first-opened set is read. S15: the final test of Arm A contains ℓ; those of Arms B and C do not.  
　Conclusion: members of N15 in the valid domain of Definition 6-11 (with ℓ initially nonexistent and s₀ = 0 in the initial states of all three arms) exhibit S15 when α\_a \> 0 and n ≥ ⌈σ/α\_a⌉.  
　Proof: in Arm A, before generation the pool contains only a, and a opens each trial and is already full before the passage, so ℓ is generated by Theorem N15-1; after generation ℓ may tie with a for first opening by rounding (Proposition N15-2 (2)), which does not affect the existence of ℓ; in the final test a is blocked and only a is presented, so Y = {ℓ} by Proposition N15-2 (5). In Arm B a is not presented, so s does not increase; in Arm C a is not full, so s does not increase; in both, ℓ does not exist, and the final test gives Y = ∅. ∎ The only dependencies are Theorem N15-1 (α\_a \> 0) and Proposition N15-2 (5).  
　Verification: Appendix B.7 G3 (300 random runs, n = 40, all exhibit S15).  
Theorem N15-4 (mechanism hypotheses excluded by S15): (1) every member containing no generation component (the lifting of v0.4 to W16-F): ℓ never exists, and none of the three arms' final tests contains ℓ. (2) C16 ⊕ GNG (without c4 arming) with λ\_G ≤ n: in Arm C a is passed each trial and err\_a \> 0 (θ\_a ≥ g(D̄\_a) \> 0), while b is never passed and err\_b = 0; at the λ\_G-th trial with a presentation the unit with the largest error is a, ℓ is inserted, Arm C's final test gives Y = {ℓ}, and S15 fails. (3) C16 ⊕ MSP with n ≥ ⌈1/ν\_M⌉: in Arm B a is not passed, A = 0, z increases by ν\_M each trial, and ℓ is generated within at most ⌈1/ν\_M⌉ trials; Arm B's final test gives Y = {ℓ}, and S15 fails. ∎  
　Reproducers within the class: c4(GNG@full of a) (the insertion law is enabled only after a's bottom-reaching event) and C16 ⊕ GWR (mismatch read as “still entering when already full”, firing counter habituated) both exhibit S15. So S15 separates the two classes of hypotheses “generation does not require fullness” (GNG) and “generation is driven by disuse” (MSP), but does not separate the various laws of “generation after fullness”.  
　Verification: Appendix B.7 G3 (GNG, MSP and C16 exhibit S15 in 0 of 300 runs each; c4(GNG@full) and GWR in 300/300 each).  
Proposition N15-5 (dependence of the generation time on the overflow amount): in protocols of the Arm A type (initially full, ℓ initially nonexistent, s₀ = 0, only a presented, rest 0), write k\_gen for the generation trial. N15: k\_gen = ⌈σ/α\_a⌉ (Theorem N15-1 (1)), stepwise nonincreasing in α\_a: values of α\_a with the same ⌈σ/α\_a⌉ give the same k\_gen (a plateau), and different rounded values give different k\_gen; C16 ⊕ c4(GNG@full): armed in trial 1 (being full before the passage is the bottom-reaching event), k\_gen is set by the insertion period λ\_G and is independent of α\_a; C16 ⊕ GWR: k\_gen is the first firing count of a at which h \< h\_T, and h varies only with the number of firings, independently of α\_a. Hence when two arms differ only in α\_a and ⌈σ/α₁⌉ ≠ ⌈σ/α₂⌉, N15's k\_gen differs between them, while the other two models give the same k\_gen. k\_gen is read out by the family of protocols “block a and test after trial n”. ∎  
　Verification: Appendix B.7 G3 (σ = 3/4; with α\_a = 1/4 and 1/8, N15 gives trials 3 and 6, c4(GNG@full) 4 and 4, GWR 3 and 3); Appendix B.10 J5, stepwise dependence (σ = 1: α\_a = 1/4 and 3/10 both give trial 4, 1/3 and 3/8 both give trial 3, 1/2 gives trial 2).  
Coverage verdict (N15): relative to the whole v0.4-F class: expressiveness undetermined—S15 excludes only two classes of generation, “not requiring fullness” and “driven by disuse”; GWR and c4(GNG@full) reproduce S15, and Proposition N15-5 separates N15 from these two, but there is no invariant for the whole v0.4-F class (for example, a composition that uses c2 to multiply GWR's habituation step by the overflow amount, or any self-written component of crevasse-splay type, could reproduce the dependence of Proposition N15-5) (U16-30). Literature layer: structural clues only (crevasse splays and avulsion; GWR; structural plasticity; GNG). Novelty not argued; not reported as a new dynamics.  
Conditional predictions (B): P-N15-1 (hydraulic hosts; Theorem N15-1, Proposition N15-5): before bankfull, no new channel forms however long the inflow lasts; after bankfull, under continued inflow, a new channel forms when the accumulated overbank spill reaches some threshold, the generation trial being k\_gen = ⌈σ/α\_a⌉ (σ the generation threshold, α\_a the overbank spill per passage after fullness), stepwise nonincreasing in the spill intensity; whether the new channel carries flow while the main channel is open depends on the discrete first-opening step (Proposition N15-2); when the main channel is blocked and the inflow enters only the domain of the main channel, the new channel is necessarily visible; excludes the GNG and MSP types, and is separated from the GWR and c4(GNG@full) types by Proposition N15-5. This reading corresponds structurally to the geomorphic phenomena of crevasse splays and avulsion; on hydraulic hosts it is a conditional prediction of a known type of phenomenon, and its quantitative law has yet to be aligned with the literature. P-N15-2 (neural hosts): new structure appears only after a pathway saturates, when its overflow drive has accumulated to a threshold, and the time of appearance varies with the drive intensity, not only with the number of firings; excludes the GWR and MSP types. Untested premises: readability of bankfull or saturation; readability of the new channel or new structure.  
Breach: under the hypotheses, S15 does not appear (Theorem N15-3); C16 ⊕ GNG (λ\_G ≤ n) or C16 ⊕ MSP (n ≥ ⌈1/ν\_M⌉) exhibits S15 on q15 (Theorem N15-4); N15 has the same k\_gen at two values of α\_a with ⌈σ/α₁⌉ ≠ ⌈σ/α₂⌉, or the k\_gen of c4(GNG@full) or GWR varies with α\_a (Proposition N15-5; an unchanged k\_gen on a plateau with the same rounded value is not a breach); within the valid domain, a birth depth outside \[0, α\_a) or a birth earlier than the count (Theorem N15-1); counterexamples to any clause of Proposition N15-2.

7\. Master Table of Instance Dynamics Types

### 7.1  Notes

Each row is one concrete instance, stating its boundary, Trace-Responses and time window; the dynamics law and its type (the codes of Section 5); the concrete process that carries the historical dynamics; mechanism coupling; proof or evidence; discrimination protocol; undetermined items. The “evidence level” column records either model item-by-item (item-by-item checking of the law text, and proof) or conditional application (reaching only conditional predictions). The verdicts in the duty column are all cited from other papers. Undetermined items are not read as negative determinations. Type codes are descriptive only; the same code does not automatically provide theorem transfer between instances (5.1).

### 7.2  Model Instances

\[I-1\] Frozen SNN instance s1 (the frozen Instance P of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts* v1.0; Host A of *The Mathematical Form of Sedimentation-Dynamics Concretization* v1.0; the 𝒦\_g member G\_SNN)  
　Boundary, Trace-Response, time window: 4 entry units, 2 gating LIF units and 8 synapses as Structural Positions; Trace-Response z\_ij = w\_ij − b\_ij (b = 1/4, state interval \[0, 2\]); declared history: formation period (pattern A with teaching current (1, 0), rest, rest) × 6, 8 rest beats, window W = beats 26–43 (*The Mathematical Form of Sedimentation-Dynamics Concretization* v1.0, 2.4).  
　Dynamics laws and types: membrane-potential integration of the gating units vt\_j = λ\_j·v\_j + Σ\_i w\_ij·s\_i + I\_j (λ\_j = 3/4; s\_i is the firing of entry unit i in the current beat, I\_j is the teaching current, injected into unit 0 only during the formation period) — Φ4a; gating g\_j = \[vt\_j ≥ θ\_j\] (θ\_j = 1) — Φ5a (single-threshold gated read-out); reset: v\_j ← 0 when g\_j = 1 (v\_reset = 0), otherwise v\_j ← vt\_j; eligibility trace x′\_i = d\_x·x\_i + s\_i, postsynaptic trace y′\_j = d\_y·y\_j + g\_j (d\_x = d\_y = 1/2); potentiation P⁺\_ij = A₊x′\_i (plastic ∧ bap ∧ g\_j) — Φ1a, conditioned on the gating event; depression P⁻\_ij = −A₋y′\_j (plastic ∧ bap ∧ s\_i) — Φ1b (MINUS; y′\_j contains the current term g\_j from postsynaptic firing in this beat and the residual term d\_y·y\_j from earlier postsynaptic firing); relaxation ρ = 0 (no Φ2 in the window); clipping as a position-wise projection — Φ3a.  
　Concrete process bearing the historical dynamics: synapse → output unit → backpropagation to the same synapse. Setting w00 ← b at beat 26 lowers the integration of output 0 below threshold, so g0 = 0 and the potentiation PLUS00 at beat 26 does not occur; the actual path is (w00, 26) → SYN00 → gating g0 → backpropagation → PLUS00 writes back to w00, with every carrier inside the boundary (*The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts* v1.0, Instance P).  
　Mechanism coupling: Φ1a ⊗g (writes gated by the gating event g\_j); Φ1b gated by the postsynaptic trace y; Φ4a and Φ5a coupled through the global gating event.  
　Proof or evidence: (A) model, item by item — the law texts of Definitions 1–3 of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts* v1.0; (B) program host, no material layer; (C) duty: the algorithmic-level verdict of that paper (cited, not re-issued).  
　Discrimination protocol: the stored membrane potential of a gating unit produces an earlier extra firing that resets v — Φ4a type (corresponding to I1′ of Theorem N3-1: an I1-type claim can be made only when the gating membrane potential at the start of the comparison is 0, for example after firing and reset in the previous beat; with a nonzero stored potential at the start, the first pair can fail). The current and residual terms of Φ1b must be kept apart:  
　　(1) Rest does not eliminate Φ1b. Example J1a: zero initial state, A with teaching current (1, 0) → none → A (no teaching): output 0 does not fire in the last beat, and MINUS00 = −1/64, not 0. Example J1b: after the frozen formation prefix (A+teach, none, none) × 6 and 8 rest beats, apply A@26, none@27, A@28: MINUS00@28 = −335581769/4294967296, not 0 (output 0 fires at beat 28, so the current term is present).  
　　(2) Upper bound on the residual term (conditional branch): let t₀ be the beat of the last postsynaptic firing of unit j, with the trace accumulated from a zero initial state before it, so that y\_j(t₀) \< 1/(1 − d\_y) = 2; suppose there is no postsynaptic firing in the r beats after it, and at the probe beat g\_j = 0, s\_i = 1 and plastic ∧ bap. Then at the probe beat MINUS\_ij = −A₋·d\_y^{r+1}·y\_j(t₀), and \|MINUS\_ij\| \< 2^{−(r+4)}. Proof: in each beat without postsynaptic firing y ← y/2, and at the probe beat y′ = y/2 + 0. ∎ Hence, for a declared decision resolution ε \> 0, a rest of r ≥ max(0, ⌈log₂(1/ε)⌉ − 4) beats brings the residual term below ε; the residual term does not reach 0 in finitely many beats. If there is postsynaptic firing at the probe beat, the current term −A₋ does not depend on the length of rest and cannot be removed by waiting.  
　　(3) No protocol has been established whose purpose is to discriminate Φ1b from interval-dependent shallowing: it would have to change only y in paired states with the same w, v and x, compare the difference of the residual terms, and give a decision resolution and an error bound (U16-13).  
　　Verification: Appendix B.10 J1 (Examples J1a, J1b; 3000 random histories each followed by a probe beat; all 1166 with no postsynaptic firing at the probe beat satisfy the equality and the upper bound of (2)).  
　Undetermined items: the protocol discriminating Φ1b from interval-dependent shallowing (U16-13); numerical verification of the I1′-type invariant on s1; the correspondence between the first-opening reading under the bifurcation reading and Theorem S-FORK of *The Mathematical Form of Sedimentation-Dynamics Concretization* v1.0.  
\[I-2\] Family A (*The Construction Form of Field Concretization* v1.0, 4.2; Host B of *The Mathematical Form of Sedimentation-Dynamics Concretization* v1.0)  
　Boundary, Trace-Response, time window: two channels with the same entry and the same head ΔP; the physical boundary contains the upstream inlet, the downstream outlet, the walls of the two channels and the carrier fluid inside them, and the loose-material store of each channel; Trace-Response z\_i (cross-section of above-baseline structural material on the wall), δmax ↔ z\_max = 10; parameters g₀ = 1, κ\_cons = 1, c = 1/5, φ = 1/2, ℓ = 1, γ = 1/10, e = 1/5.  
　Dynamics laws and types: flow rate Q\_i = (g₀ + κ\_cons z\_i)ΔP; write φcQ\_iΔt/ℓ — Φ1a (written according to the flux of the channel itself); relaxation −γz\_iΔt — Φ2; clipping at z\_max — Φ3a; structural material shed by relaxation enters the loose-material store L\_i of the same channel (entered at the end of the beat), and each beat the carrier fluid carries out min(L\_i, eQ\_iΔt) (using the store at the start of the beat; no output on zero-flow beats), so the carrier fluid carries the store out of the boundary — Φ4b (side-branch material store). The store does not feed back into the write or relaxation of the Structural Positions: neither the coefficients, nor the occurrence, nor the routes of write and relaxation read L (*The Construction Form of Field Concretization* v1.0, 4.2 C4, C4′; *The Mathematical Form of Sedimentation-Dynamics Concretization* v1.0, 2.4). Difference recursion d(t+1) = (1 + k\_t − γ)d(t), k\_t = φcκ\_consΔP(t)Δt/ℓ.  
　Concrete process bearing the historical dynamics: the retained cross-section z\_i determines the flow rate of its channel, and the flow rate determines the write in that channel (use dependence, through the route of material flow).  
　Mechanism coupling: under constant head the two channels are each linear, with no coupling between them (no Φ8; no Φ15 — at constant head, deepening of one channel does not change the flux of the other); Φ1a and Φ2 combine additively via c1; the store of Φ4b is a side-branch output quantity and does not enter the laws of Φ1a and Φ2.  
　Proof or evidence: (A) model, item by item — *The Construction Form of Field Concretization* v1.0, 4.2, and *The Mathematical Form of Sedimentation-Dynamics Concretization* v1.0, 2.4 and 4.3; (B) the slime-mould tube reading and the river-channel reading are conditional applications (under the river-channel reading the budget runs in the opposite direction, an open item of *The Construction Form of Field Concretization* v1.0); (C) duty: the conclusions of *The Mathematical Form of Sedimentation-Dynamics Concretization* v1.0 and *The Mathematical Form of Duty Correspondence in Field Concretization* v1.0 are cited (cited, not re-issued).  
　Discrimination protocol (model layer): physical coupling and reading normalization are written separately. Under constant head the structural law of Family A has no cross-path feedback: Q\_b = (g₀ + κ\_cons z\_b)ΔP does not change with z\_a. The relative share s\_b = Q\_b/(Q\_a + Q\_b) = G\_b/(G\_a + G\_b) (G\_i = g₀ + κ\_cons z\_i) nevertheless falls as G\_a increases; this is a relative change introduced by normalizing the reading, not a physical coupling. Under a constant total flow I (KIR type, Φ15), Q\_b = I·G\_b/(G\_a + G\_b) falls as G\_a increases, and the share is again G\_b/(G\_a + G\_b). A share change in a single state therefore cannot discriminate constant head from constant total flow: in Example J2a, (G\_a, G\_b) goes from (1, 1) to (2, 1); at constant head ΔP = 1, Q\_b goes 1 → 1 and the share 1/2 → 1/3; at constant total flow I = 2, Q\_b goes 1 → 2/3 and the share likewise 1/2 → 1/3. The usable reading is “whether the absolute flux of the other path changes,” with the total flux or the head measured at the same time and the corresponding boundary condition (constant head or constant total flow) fixed: under constant head Q\_b is unchanged while the total flux increases; under constant total flow the total flux is unchanged while Q\_b falls. A share change alone cannot certify physical resource competition or flow-division feedback.  
　　Verification: Appendix B.10 J2 (Example J2a; 2000 random sets: the shares are identical under the two boundary conditions, Q\_b never changes under constant head, and Q\_b always falls under constant total flow).  
　Undetermined items: a theorem for S13-type discrimination under absolute-flux readings (U16-35); whether Φ13 is present under the frozen blockade extension (only protocol-imposed release, no law of its own).  
\[I-3\] Family E (*The Construction Form of Field Concretization* v1.0, 4.3; Host C of *The Mathematical Form of Sedimentation-Dynamics Concretization* v1.0)  
　Boundary, Trace-Response, time window: a presynaptic neuron drives two postsynaptic neurons through two synapses; Trace-Response z\_i (above-baseline conductance); parameters w₀ = 1, β = 1, η = 1/10, γ = 1/10, g₁ = 1/10.  
　Dynamics laws and types: y\_i = β(w₀ + z\_i)x; write ηxy\_iΔt — Φ1a and Φ14 (the amount written grows with the square of the drive x, k\_t = ηβx(t)²Δt); decay −γz\_iΔt — Φ2; the receptor pool R\_i follows according to its budget.  
　Concrete process bearing the historical dynamics: the retained conductance z\_i determines the postsynaptic activity y\_i, and the product of this activity with the presynaptic activity determines the write (Hebbian type, through the synaptic route).  
　Mechanism coupling: Φ1a ⊗ x² at each synapse separately (gated by drive strength); no threshold (Φ5 has no object); only share readings are taken.  
　Proof or evidence: (A) model, item by item — *The Construction Form of Field Concretization* v1.0, 4.3; (B) conditional application (the firing-rate neural network as a model of a neural host); (C) duty: *The Mathematical Form of Duty Correspondence in Field Concretization* v1.0 is cited (principal pair s1 ↔ Family E).  
　Discrimination protocol (model layer): an I\_PD-type protocol — two histories that differ only in drive strength give different subsequent shares (Φ14), whereas every member of C\_cmp v0.1 is invariant to strength when the opening patterns are the same (Lemma N12-L2). No theorem has been established under share or absolute-amount readings; when shares are used, the total must be reported as well, so that a relative change introduced by normalization is not read as coupling.  
　Undetermined items: the I\_PD theorem for Family E under absolute-amount readings (U16-35).  
\[I-4\] Minimal two-path threshold model (Host D of *The Mathematical Form of Sedimentation-Dynamics Concretization* v1.0)  
　Boundary, Trace-Response, time window: two paths under entry e; Depth\_x is a state; θ\_x = g(Depth\_x) = 1/(1 + Depth\_x); p(t) is a declared non-decreasing sequence; Brk is frozen; two run conventions, stop and keep.  
　Dynamics laws and types: in that paper the state is frozen and only readings are taken — Φ5b; there is no write law. W16-E of this paper is its extension with laws (I-5).  
　Concrete process bearing the historical dynamics: the retained depth determines, through the threshold order, which path opens first (read-out layer).  
　Proof or evidence: (A) model, item by item; (B) and (C) not applicable. Discrimination protocol: see I-5. Undetermined items: none (read-out layer).  
\[I-5\] C16 v0.2 on W16-E (the frozen comparison model of this paper)  
　Boundary, Trace-Response, time window: Definitions 3-1 and 4-1; Trace-Response d\_x; the time window is the declared protocol.  
　Dynamics laws and types: Φ1a ⊕ Φ2 ⊕ Φ3a ⊕ Φ5b.  
　Concrete process bearing the historical dynamics: passage deepens → the threshold drops → the path opens first next time (the sole lead is absorbing, I1).  
　Proof: Proposition 4-1.  
　Discrimination protocol: each of the four invariants of Proposition 4-1 is an exclusionary reading for one kind of dynamics (the reading in 4.1).  
　Undetermined items: the K3, K11, K2 and K7b branches (2.4).  
\[I-6\] Candidate closed-law models on W16-E, W16-E″ and W16-F (discrimination samples; each mapped one by one to a W instance of a known mechanism)  
　N2: Φ1a ⊕ Φ2 ⊕ Φ3b (conserving outward seepage to the other path once full) ⊕ Φ5b, i.e. the spill-in type of Φ8; proven coverage at the W-instance layer — FS (qualitative alignment at the literature layer). Discrimination: Theorems N2-2, N2-3, N2-4.  
　N3: C16 ⊕ Φ4a (entry summation, reset to zero on opening, leakage); proven coverage at the W-instance layer — entry LIF (partial item-by-item alignment). Discrimination: Theorems N3-1–N3-7.  
　N5: Φ16 (short trace gates long-trace writes; parallel opposing layers) ⊗ u; with the *Drosophila* model of Huang et al., structural clue only. Discrimination: Theorems N5-1, N5-2, N5-4; Propositions N5-5, N5-6.  
　N11: C16 ⊕ Φ12 (integral feedback driven by an intention input); proven coverage at the W-instance layer — c1(C16, IFB). Discrimination: Theorem N11-1, Proposition N11-2.  
　N12: C16 ⊕ Φ13 (self-release of the blockade and release scour, with the scour amount set by a stock accumulated according to drive strength); not expressible relative to C\_cmp v0.1 (Theorem N12-4); relative to v0.2, proven coverage at the W-instance layer — c1(C16, DAM) (DAM is written from qualitative content and does not constitute independent literature coverage). Discrimination: Theorems N12-1–N12-5, Proposition N12-6.  
　N13: Φ15 (flow division by (1 + d)^ν, share-proportional write to the first-opened path); not expressible relative to v0.1 or v0.2 (Theorem N13-4); relative to v0.3, proven coverage at the W-instance layer — c2(KIR, 1\[Y\]) (the share equation and the linear reinforcement equation aligned equation by equation, three items being W choices). Discrimination: Propositions N13-1, N13-2; Theorems N13-3–N13-5.  
　N14 (W16-E″): C16 ⊕ Φ10 (a signed shared integrator attached to Imagery modulates writes); proven coverage at the W-instance layer — c1(C16, IZH); a symmetric member of the declared family BIM; separated from the fixed discretization IZD by protocol B. Discrimination: Propositions N14-1, N14-2; Theorems N14-3–N14-6; Propositions N14-7, N14-8.  
　N15 (W16-F): C16 ⊕ Φ17 (post-full increments accumulate along the edge and generate a new position on reaching a threshold, i.e. Φ3c); expressiveness relative to v0.4-F to be determined; a candidate implementation model. Discrimination: Theorems N15-1, N15-3, N15-4; Propositions N15-2, N15-5.  
　Evidence layer: all model, item by item (the theorems of Section 6); material layer: conditional applications, see 7.3; duty: not issued.  
\[I-7\] 𝒦\_LS family members of *The Necessity Form of Field-Concretization Qualification* v1.0 (event-gated subclass; Theorem 14-1⁺ of that paper)  
　Boundary, Trace-Response, time window: the subclass 𝒦\_LS(j, A) of 𝒦\_g, condition group C\_LS⁺; T = {(i, j) : i ∈ A}; the window W satisfies R2⁺ of that paper.  
　Dynamics laws and types: the same program laws as I-1 (Φ4a gated integration, Φ5a single threshold, Φ1a ⊗g, Φ1b); under C\_LS⁺ the historical dynamics passes only through threshold gating, and ρ = 0. Family 1ᵍ (threshold-scour pool-network reading, a model assumption): when the water level of a sink pool reaches the critical value, the pool scours and empties (Φ4a ⊕ Φ5a), scour widens the channel (Φ1a ⊗g), and inflow carries loose material that causes clogging (the hydraulic reading of Φ1b).  
　Concrete process bearing the historical dynamics: self-sustainment–pivot: at the probe beat the threshold-gating pivot makes potentiation occur (Lemma 14.5 of that paper).  
　Proof or evidence: (A) model, item by item (that paper); (B) Family 1ᵍ is a model assumption with no material certification; (C) the conditional conclusions of that paper under C\_LS⁺ (cited).  
　Discrimination protocol: 6.3 (Retentive Containment) and Proposition 4-1 apply to the part concerning its two-path reading and have not been transferred item by item. Undetermined items: the conditions for transferring the discrimination theorems of Section 6 to 𝒦\_LS (U16-36).  
7.3  Conditional Applications to Real Objects (only to the conditional layer; no certification of material evidence or duty)  
\[C-1\] *Drosophila* mushroom body (candidate 04 of the 17)  
　Proposed models: N5 type (Φ16) for the recurrent dopamine model of Huang et al. (equations not brought into the alignment), TF3, RW, TWO.  
　Conditional predictions: P-N5-1, P-N5-2, P-N5-4 (6.4).  
　Untested premises: the correspondence of compartments to layers; the correspondence of T-maze choice to the first-opening reading; the amount written by the test itself; the facts in the experimental reports relied on await independent checking.  
　Minimal supplementary protocol: non-reinforced tests on the short and long arms (the two-arm protocol of Theorem N5-4) and post-test read-out of the short-term units.  
\[C-2\] Riverbeds and flumes (candidate 12 of the 17)  
　Proposed models: the river-channel reading of Family A (budget in the opposite direction, pending verification); N12 ≅ DAM type (Φ13); N13 ≅ KIR-type channel splitting (Φ15; Kirchhoff flow division is a physical fact); N15 type (Φ17, corresponding to crevasse splays and avulsion structures).  
　Conditional predictions: P-N12-1, P-N12-2; P-N13-1; P-N15-1.  
　Untested premises: blockade can be applied while inflow is maintained; the first opening is readable; the bankfull state is decidable; the new channel is readable. The geomorphological literature relied on (the avulsion studies of Ganti et al. and Chadwick et al.) gives only the avulsion phenomenon, not experiments that erase or implant Trace-Responses.  
\[C-3\] Comparative and conditional-model material on mammalian cortex and hippocampus  
　Proposed models: N14 (BIM), IZD, TF3e (same-beat gating), c4 mode type.  
　Conditional predictions: Proposition N14-7 (waiting segment), Proposition N14-8 (shared-state reset, with a resolvability condition), P-N14-1–P-N14-3.  
　Untested premises: neuromodulatory surrogates of like/dislike input; a waiting period without local activity can be imposed; the shared neuromodulatory state can be selectively reset without touching modes and depths.  
　Status: C-3 is comparative or conditional-model material on mammals (mainly rodents); it does not bear the certification of human-brain qualification or of mechanisms the human brain uses; candidate 05 of the 17, the human brain, is an item not directly studied (Section 10).  
\[C-4\] Slime mould (candidate 02 of the 17)  
　Proposed models: Family A (constant head) and KIR (constant flow); the slime-mould tube-network literature relied on (Kramar and Alim 2021; Marbach et al. 2023).  
　Conditional predictions: with the entry boundary condition fixed and the total flux or the head measured at the same time, the entry condition determines whether the absolute flux of one tube falls after the other tube deepens — under constant head (Family A) it does not fall and the total flux increases; under constant total flow (KIR type, Φ15) it falls and the total flux is unchanged; the share changes are the same in both cases and are not used as a discriminating reading (7.2 \[I-2\]).  
　Untested premises: the entry boundary condition is controllable; the absolute flux of each tube and the total flux (or the head) are measurable.  
7.4  Mechanisms That Cannot Be Separated (range of equivalence, unknown causes, minimal supplementary protocol)  
　(1) N2 and FS: equal trial by trial at the W-instance layer; only qualitatively aligned at the literature layer. Range of equivalence: all protocols of W16-E. Unknown cause: the cited literature has no storage equation. Minimal supplement: fill-and-spill literature with a storage equation.  
　(2) N3 and entry LIF: equal at the W-instance layer; the absence of within-trial leakage is an approximation. Range of equivalence: W16-E. Minimal supplement: rewrite the within-trial leakage of N3 according to the exact LIF discretization. Fast-leak blind spot: members with ρ ≤ 1 − θ\_floor/θ\_min give ∅^n, as C16 does, under every constant-pressure protocol with P\_lo \< θ\_floor (the blind spot of Theorem N3-6); no minimal supplementary protocol has been established (U16-12).  
　(3) N5 and the model of Huang et al.: the equivalence between that paper's equations and N5 is undecided; minimal supplement: the equations in the supplementary appendix of that model, and the short-term-unit readings of P-N5-4.  
　(4) N13, the original KIR equation, and C16 ⊕ DN: all three exhibit S13; N13 and the original KIR equation are not equal trial by trial; no protocol discriminating N13 from DN has been established.  
　(5) N14, the broad reading X3, and IZHc: all exhibit S14; N14 and IZD are separated by protocol B (Proposition N14-7); for the divergent prediction P-N14-3 of N14 and IZHc no theorem has been established.  
　(6) N15, GWR, and c4(GNG@full): all exhibit S15; they are separated by overflow-amount dependence (Proposition N15-5); there is no invariant over the whole class v0.4-F.  
　(7) The symmetric blind spot of N1b and C16 v0.2 under Q₀ (Proposition 16-7, reading Y only): separable once W is enlarged with per-path presentation (Proposition 4-3); on a single-entry host, presentation is equivalent to blockade. The blind spot of the whole N1 class under the full reading is given separately by the first-grid-cell construction of Theorem 16-4; neither replaces the other.  
　(8) Share readings of Family A and the KIR type: a share change in a single state is the same (Example J2a); the two are separated by absolute-flux and total readings (7.2 \[I-2\]).  
　The unknown is not read as a negative determination.

8\. Bounds of Misjudgment and Blind Spots  
8.1  Tier A: each discrimination theorem has zero misjudgment under ideal readings; the conditions are the grid margins, window reachability and parameter boxes in the hypotheses of each theorem. Nothing is issued outside the hypotheses.  
8.2  Tier B (reading channel R\_ε, Definition 3-2): the statistic S is the number of trials in the declared tail segment reported as the specified pattern, and the decision rule is S ≥ τ; when the truth on one side never shows the pattern and the tail segment on the other side always shows it, α ≤ P(Bin(n′, ε) ≥ τ) and β ≤ P(Bin(n′, 1 − ε) \< τ) (proof as for Theorem 16-6). Explicit values (computed in exact rational arithmetic, then converted to decimal; τ is the value that minimizes the larger of the two; Appendix B.2 R6):  
　ε = 1/20: n′ = 10, τ = 5, α ≤ 6.369e-05, β ≤ 2.755e-06; n′ = 20, τ = 10, α ≤ 1.134e-08, β ≤ 5.380e-10; n′ = 40, τ = 20, α ≤ 4.960e-16, β ≤ 2.474e-17.  
　ε = 1/10: n′ = 10, τ = 5, α ≤ 1.635e-03, β ≤ 1.469e-04; n′ = 20, τ = 10, α ≤ 7.151e-06, β ≤ 7.089e-07; n′ = 40, τ = 20, α ≤ 1.872e-10, β ≤ 1.960e-11.  
　ε = 1/5: n′ = 10, τ = 5, α ≤ 3.279e-02, β ≤ 6.369e-03; n′ = 20, τ = 10, α ≤ 2.595e-03, β ≤ 5.634e-04; n′ = 40, τ = 20, α ≤ 2.169e-05, β ≤ 5.027e-06.  
　Application: Theorem N2-5 (N2₌ against C16 v0.2; pattern = both paths open simultaneously; tail segment = trials K to n, n′ = n − K + 1): with ε = 1/10, n′ = 20, τ = 10, α ≤ 7.151e-06 and β ≤ 7.089e-07.  
　Theorem N3-7 (N3 against the comparison subclass listed in Theorem N3-7, excluding STF; values for C16, N1 and HOM as examples; protocol q\_N3 extended to n = 8m, j*\_max = 8, S = number of trials reported as non-∅, α ≤ P(Bin(n, ε) ≥ τ), β ≤ P(Bin(m, 1 − ε) \< τ)): for ε = 1/20 and m = 10, 20, 40, respectively α ≤ 4.659e-02, 6.707e-03, 1.810e-04 and β ≤ 1.150e-02, 2.574e-03, 1.296e-04; for ε = 1/40 and m = 10, 20, 40, respectively α ≤ 8.930e-04, 1.376e-05, 9.742e-10 and β ≤ 1.643e-03, 6.994e-06, 2.818e-09 (τ = 15 for m = 20, n = 160). A positive gap requires ε to be below about 1/(j*\_max + 1).  
　Theorem 16-6 (C16 v0.2 against N1\[φ ≥ φ\_min\]): α ≤ P(Bin(n, ε) ≥ τ), β ≤ P(Bin(n − L, 1 − ε) \< τ), with values computed by the same method.  
　The remaining discrimination theorems (6.4–6.9) are issued at Tier A only; Tier B has not been established.  
8.3  Quantifiers over the channel class: an upper bound ε alone cannot guarantee zero misjudgment uniformly over the whole channel class (Proposition 16-6′).  
8.4  Discretization and grid: every discrimination by first-opening readings is constrained by the grid P/T; a continuous threshold difference does not by itself guarantee a reading difference (Proposition N13-2 (3), Proposition N14-8 (2), Proposition N15-2 (2) (3)). Wherever the hypotheses of a theorem contain a grid margin, the readings may or may not coincide when the margin fails; nothing is issued.  
8.5  Summary of blind spots: the whole N1 class has no uniform finite Tier A protocol under the full reading, and for each finite protocol there is an inseparable member given by the first-grid-cell construction (the blind-spot paragraph of Theorem 16-4); the symmetric members of N1b under Q₀ (Proposition 16-7); the first-opening reading of N2 when the capacity of the sole-lead path is large (closed by the probe of Theorem N2-4); the fast-leak members of N3 (Theorem N3-6); N5 and the model of Huang et al. (equations not brought into the alignment); N13 and DNC; N14 with X3 and IZHc; N15 against the whole class v0.4-F beyond GWR and c4(GNG@full).  
8.6  Finite verification: the appendix programs cover only their generating domains and are not exhaustive over all combinations; universal conclusions come only from proofs.

9\. Domain of Validity  
　(1) Working domain: W16-E v0.1, W16-E″, W16-F; rational parameters, finite windows, deterministic inputs. The per-path presentation used by Proposition 4-3 and by N13, N14 and N15 is an input operation only on hosts that have per-path inputs.  
　(2) Comparison classes: the versions referred to by each theorem (v0.1–v0.4, v0.3″, v0.4-F) and their parameter boxes; the equalities hold for all parameters, while membership holds only inside the box (D3.6).  
　(3) Branches: those listed in 2.4; the constant-shallowing-rate antecedent (Proposition N5-6); the K3 discard-increment antecedent (I1 of Proposition 4-1); the h ≡ 0 antecedent (∅-absorption).  
　(4) Instances: as listed row by row in Section 7; on the instances of other papers (s1, Family A, Family E, 𝒦\_LS) only their frozen laws and their correspondence with the readings of this paper are used, and duty verdicts follow the original papers.  
　(5) Material: conditional application throughout.

11\. Breach Modes (Summary)  
　Core breach: a run within the hypotheses of a theorem of Section 6 that contradicts its conclusion (the breach conditions of each are given at the end of its subsection).  
　Coverage breach: a run on some protocol in which a candidate and the member that constructively covers it (FS, LIF, IFBC, c1(C16, DAM), c2(KIR, 1\[Y\]), c1(C16, IZH), the symmetric member of BIM) differ in reading or state.  
　Classification breach: the law text of an instance in Section 7 does not match the dynamics types registered for it (the law lacks that process, or has an unregistered process).  
　Reading breach: under the reported grid and window, a discrimination of continuous quantities that has not been turned into a discrimination of finite readings is taken as a discrimination.  
　Level breach: a model conclusion is substituted for material evidence or for a duty verdict; “not expressible relative to some frozen comparison class” is substituted for “unique in reality” or “new dynamics discovered.”  
　Version breach: the versions of W, C16 or C\_cmp used in a comparison differ from those the theorem refers to; the branch choices for K3, K7b, K11 or K2 are changed without re-proving the invariants they support.  
　Not a breach: a candidate is covered (already reported); a blind spot (already registered); no existing experiment on some object; items not directly studied.

　U16-35  Versions of S13- and I\_PD-type discrimination under absolute-flux (or absolute-conductance) readings, which must measure the total at the same time and fix the boundary condition (Family A, Family E); share readings alone do not discriminate constant head from constant total flow.  
　U16-36  The conditions for transferring the discrimination theorems of Section 6 to 𝒦\_LS.

13\. Interfaces

　To Chapter 21 of the Field Concretization Group Form, *The Mathematical Form of Field-Law Generation in Geomorphic Concretized Fields* (river-channel geomorphic hosts): C-2; Theorems N12-1, N12-2, N12-4; the four-arm diagnosis of Theorem N13-3; Theorem N15-3 and Proposition N15-5.  
　To Chapter 20 of the Field Concretization Group Form, *The Discrimination Form of Trace-Response Transformation in Artificial Neural Networks* (spiking-neural-network hosts): Φ1b and I1′ of I-1; the conditions for rewriting Proposition 4-1 and Section 6.3 on gated-integration hosts.

　Completing the formal law terms of the main book (K3 outward seepage, K7b tuning-controlled writes, K11 the law of Retentive Containment, the functional form of K2, Coloring, the quantitative law of Edge-Seepage) belongs to the change procedure of the main book; the candidate closed laws of this paper serve only as optional formal readings and discrimination samples and are not merged into K\_F.

## Appendix A  Program Source

Runtime environment: Python 3.10 or later, using only the standard library and exact rational arithmetic with fractions. The following 17 files are placed in the same directory: A.1–A.7 are the libraries of this paper, A.8 is identical word for word to Appendix A.1 of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts* v1.0, and A.9–A.17 are run entries; the run entries read only the Appendix A files in the same directory (to print the SHA-256 of the library files) and no other files. The programs give finite verification and exact witnesses; universal conclusions rest on the proofs in the main text.  
A.1  t16b\_core.py  Core library: trials on the working domain W16-E, C16 v0.2 and the L₁ components (N1, STF, HOM, HS, HSsat, TF3, RW, TWO, FS, LIF), the forward relations of N2, N3 and N5, and the invariant-check functions

~~~python
# -*- coding: utf-8 -*-
"""The Discrimination Form of Sedimentation Dynamics v1.0, appendix core library: forward relations of the working domain W16-E and of the comparison components.
Standard library only; all numerical values are fractions.Fraction.
Input of one trial: (P, u, Pres, r)
  P    peak entry flow pressure; the entry flow pressure rises on the grid p_t = h + t·P/T (t = 0..T), where h is the entry store (h ≡ 0 for members without a store);
  u    reinforcement input ∈ {0, 1}; Pres presentation set ⊆ {0, 1} (0 = a, 1 = b); r number of rest beats after the trial.
Opening: τ_x = min{t : x ∈ Pres and p_t ≥ θ_x} (None if there is none); Y = {x : τ_x = min τ}, and Y = ∅ if no path opens. Reading: (Y, τ_min) trial by trial.
Members (kind): 'C' comparison model C16; 'N1' fatigue; 'N2' conserving outward seepage once full; 'FS' fill-and-spill (independent implementation); 'HS' crosstalk; 'HSsat' switches to crosstalk at the clipping event;
  'N3' entry accumulation and release; 'LIF' leaky integration at the entry (independent implementation); 'STF' shared facilitation; 'HOM' rest-driven homeostasis; 'TF3' three-factor; 'RW'; 'TWO' two-timescale; 'N5' layered writes.
Threshold family: g(d) = g0/(1 + c·d), 0 < g0 ≤ 1, c > 0.
"""
from fractions import Fraction as Fr
import math
BOTH = frozenset((0, 1))

def F(v):
    return v if isinstance(v, Fr) else Fr(v)

def g(g0, c, d):
    return g0 / (1 + c * d)

def open_step(theta, h, P, T):
    """Smallest t (0 ≤ t ≤ T) on the grid p_t = h + t·P/T with p_t ≥ θ; None if there is none."""
    if h >= theta:
        return 0
    t = math.ceil((theta - h) * T / P)
    return t if t <= T else None

class M:
    """Member: kind and parameter dict p; per-path parameters are written as pairs (a, b)."""
    def __init__(self, kind, **p):
        self.kind = kind
        self.p = {}
        for k, v in p.items():
            if isinstance(v, (tuple, list)):
                self.p[k] = tuple(None if x is None else F(x) for x in v)
            elif v is None:
                self.p[k] = None
            else:
                self.p[k] = F(v)
    def __getitem__(self, k):
        return self.p[k]
    def get(self, k, default=None):
        return self.p.get(k, default)
    def describe(self):
        items = []
        for k in sorted(self.p):
            v = self.p[k]
            if isinstance(v, tuple):
                items.append('%s=(%s)' % (k, ', '.join('∞' if x is None else str(x) for x in v)))
            else:
                items.append('%s=%s' % (k, v))
        return self.kind + ' ' + ' '.join(items)

def new_state(m, d=(0, 0), **kw):
    st = {'d': [F(d[0]), F(d[1])], 'h': F(kw.get('h', 0)),
          'f': [F(x) for x in kw.get('f', (0, 0))],
          'F': F(kw.get('Fac', 0)),
          'H': [F(x) for x in kw.get('H', (0, 0))],
          's': [F(x) for x in kw.get('s', (0, 0))],
          'L': [F(x) for x in kw.get('L', (0, 0))],
          'E': [F(x) for x in kw.get('E', (0, 0))],
          'df': [F(x) for x in kw.get('df', (0, 0))],
          'ds': [F(x) for x in kw.get('ds', (0, 0))]}
    if m.kind == 'TWO':
        st['d'] = [st['df'][0] + st['ds'][0], st['df'][1] + st['ds'][1]]
    return st

def copy_state(st):
    return {k: (list(v) if isinstance(v, list) else v) for k, v in st.items()}

def cap_of(m, x):
    c = m.get('cap')
    return None if c is None else c[x]

def clip(v, cap):
    return v if cap is None else min(v, cap)

def theta(m, st, x):
    k = m.kind
    g0, c = m['g0'][x], m['c'][x]
    if k in ('C', 'N2', 'HS', 'HSsat', 'N3', 'TF3', 'RW'):
        return g(g0, c, st['d'][x])
    if k == 'TWO':
        return g(g0, c, max(Fr(0), st['df'][x] + st['ds'][x]))
    if k == 'N1':
        return g(g0, c, st['d'][x]) + st['f'][x]
    if k == 'STF':
        return g(g0, c, st['d'][x]) * (1 - st['F'])
    if k == 'HOM':
        return g(g0, c, st['d'][x]) * (1 - st['H'][x])
    if k == 'N5':
        de = st['s'][x] + st['L'][x] - st['E'][x]
        return g(g0, c, de if de > 0 else Fr(0))
    if k == 'FS':
        return g(g0, c, st['d'][x])   # FS has its own independent implementation fs_trial; used here only to read the threshold
    if k == 'LIF':
        return g(g0, c, st['d'][x])
    raise ValueError(k)

def trial(m, st, T, P, u=0, pres=BOTH):
    """One trial: return (Y, τ_min) and update st in place by the member's law."""
    P = F(P)
    if m.kind == 'FS':
        return fs_trial(m, st, T, P, pres)
    if m.kind == 'LIF':
        return lif_trial(m, st, T, P, pres)
    h = st['h'] if m.kind == 'N3' else Fr(0)
    taus = [open_step(theta(m, st, x), h, P, T) if x in pres else None for x in (0, 1)]
    fin = [t for t in taus if t is not None]
    if fin:
        tmin = min(fin)
        Y = frozenset(x for x in (0, 1) if taus[x] == tmin)
    else:
        tmin = None
        Y = frozenset()
    passage(m, st, Y, u, P, pres)
    return Y, tmin

def passage(m, st, Y, u, P, pres):
    k = m.kind
    if k in ('C', 'N1', 'STF', 'HOM', 'N3'):
        for x in Y:
            st['d'][x] = clip(st['d'][x] + m['alpha'][x], cap_of(m, x))
        if k == 'N1':
            for x in Y:
                st['f'][x] += m['phi']
                if m.get('fcap') is not None and st['f'][x] > m['fcap']:
                    st['f'][x] = m['fcap']
        if k == 'STF' and pres:
            st['F'] = st['F'] + m['kF'] * (m['Fbar'] - st['F'])
        if k == 'HOM':
            for x in Y:
                st['H'][x] = (1 - m['nu']) * st['H'][x]
        if k == 'N3':
            if Y:
                st['h'] = Fr(0)
            elif pres:
                st['h'] = m['rho'] * (st['h'] + P)
    elif k == 'N2':
        new = list(st['d'])
        exc = [Fr(0), Fr(0)]
        for x in Y:
            cp = cap_of(m, x)
            tot = st['d'][x] + m['alpha'][x]
            new[x] = clip(tot, cp)
            exc[x] = tot - new[x]
        for x in Y:
            o = 1 - x
            if exc[x] > 0:
                new[o] = clip(new[o] + exc[x], cap_of(m, o))
        st['d'] = new
    elif k == 'HS':
        new = list(st['d'])
        for x in Y:
            new[x] = clip(new[x] + m['alpha'][x], cap_of(m, x))
        for x in Y:
            o = 1 - x
            new[o] = clip(new[o] + m['kappa'], cap_of(m, o))
        st['d'] = new
    elif k == 'HSsat':
        new = list(st['d'])
        for x in Y:
            cp = cap_of(m, x)
            if cp is not None and st['d'][x] >= cp:
                o = 1 - x
                new[o] = clip(new[o] + m['alpha'][x], cap_of(m, o))
            else:
                new[x] = clip(new[x] + m['alpha'][x], cp)
        st['d'] = new
    elif k == 'TF3':
        if u:
            for x in Y:
                st['d'][x] = clip(st['d'][x] + m['alpha'][x], cap_of(m, x))
    elif k == 'RW':
        for x in Y:
            st['d'][x] = st['d'][x] + m['eta'] * (u * m['Vbar'] - st['d'][x])
    elif k == 'TWO':
        for x in Y:
            e = u * m['Vbar'] - (st['df'][x] + st['ds'][x])
            st['df'][x] += m['Bf'] * e
            st['ds'][x] += m['Bs'] * e
            st['d'][x] = st['df'][x] + st['ds'][x]
    elif k == 'N5':
        for x in Y:
            if u:
                st['s'][x] = min(st['s'][x] + m['alpha_s'], m['sbar'])
            elif st['s'][x] >= m['sigma']:
                st['L'][x] += m['beta']
            else:
                st['E'][x] += m['betaE']
    else:
        raise ValueError(k)

def rest(m, st, r):
    k = m.kind
    for _ in range(r):
        if k in ('C', 'N1', 'N2', 'HS', 'HSsat', 'N3', 'STF', 'HOM', 'TF3', 'RW', 'LIF'):
            for x in (0, 1):
                st['d'][x] = (1 - m['lam'][x]) * st['d'][x]
        if k == 'N1':
            for x in (0, 1):
                st['f'][x] = (1 - m['mu']) * st['f'][x]
        if k == 'N3':
            st['h'] = (1 - m['muh']) * st['h']
        if k == 'STF':
            st['F'] = (1 - m['muF']) * st['F']
        if k == 'HOM':
            for x in (0, 1):
                st['H'][x] = st['H'][x] + m['kH'] * (m['Hbar'] - st['H'][x])
        if k == 'TWO':
            for x in (0, 1):
                st['df'][x] = (1 - m['lf']) * st['df'][x]
                st['ds'][x] = (1 - m['ls']) * st['ds'][x]
                st['d'][x] = st['df'][x] + st['ds'][x]
        if k == 'N5':
            for x in (0, 1):
                st['s'][x] = (1 - m['mus']) * st['s'][x]
                st['E'][x] = (1 - m['muE']) * st['E'][x]
                st['L'][x] = (1 - m['muL']) * st['L'][x]
        if k == 'FS':
            for x in (0, 1):
                st['S'][x] = (1 - m['leak'][x]) * st['S'][x]
        if k == 'LIF':
            st['V'] = (1 - m['muV']) * st['V']

# ---------------- Independent implementations: fill-and-spill (hydrology) and LIF (neural) ----------------
def fs_state(S=(0, 0)):
    return {'S': [F(S[0]), F(S[1])]}

def fs_trial(m, st, T, P, pres):
    """fill-and-spill: two storage depressions S_x (capacity Cap_x); the entry inflow V_x goes to the first opener; a full depression spills over into the neighboring one.
    First-opening competition: threshold g(S_x) (the larger the storage, the lower the outflow threshold); grid as in W16-E."""
    taus = []
    for x in (0, 1):
        if x not in pres:
            taus.append(None)
            continue
        th = g(m['g0'][x], m['c'][x], st['S'][x])
        t = math.ceil(th * T / P)
        taus.append(t if t <= T else None)
    fin = [t for t in taus if t is not None]
    if not fin:
        return frozenset(), None
    tmin = min(fin)
    Y = frozenset(x for x in (0, 1) if taus[x] == tmin)
    fill = list(st['S'])
    spill = [Fr(0), Fr(0)]
    for x in Y:
        room = m['Cap'][x] - st['S'][x]
        take = m['V'][x] if m['V'][x] <= room else room
        fill[x] = st['S'][x] + take
        spill[x] = m['V'][x] - take
    for x in Y:
        o = 1 - x
        if spill[x] > 0:
            room = m['Cap'][o] - fill[o]
            fill[o] += spill[x] if spill[x] <= room else room
    st['S'] = fill
    return Y, tmin

def lif_state(V=0, d=(0, 0)):
    return {'V': F(V), 'd': [F(d[0]), F(d[1])]}

def lif_trial(m, st, T, P, pres):
    """The entry is a leaky-integrator unit: within a trial the membrane potential V rises linearly with the input as V + t·P/T and fires as soon as it first reaches either output threshold (the exit is the one reached first);
    after firing it resets V ← 0; without firing, one leak step at the end of the trial gives V ← ρ_V(V + P). The output synapses deepen according to K1 (clipped at capacity)."""
    taus = []
    for x in (0, 1):
        if x not in pres:
            taus.append(None)
            continue
        th = g(m['g0'][x], m['c'][x], st['d'][x])
        if st['V'] >= th:
            taus.append(0)
        else:
            t = math.ceil((th - st['V']) * T / P)
            taus.append(t if t <= T else None)
    fin = [t for t in taus if t is not None]
    if not fin:
        if pres:
            st['V'] = m['rhoV'] * (st['V'] + P)
        return frozenset(), None
    tmin = min(fin)
    Y = frozenset(x for x in (0, 1) if taus[x] == tmin)
    for x in Y:
        cp = m['cap'][x]
        st['d'][x] = st['d'][x] + m['alpha'][x] if cp is None else min(st['d'][x] + m['alpha'][x], cp)
    st['V'] = Fr(0)
    return Y, tmin

# ---------------- Runs ----------------
def run(m, st0, T, trials):
    """trials = [(P, u, pres, r), ...]; return [(Y, τ_min), ...] and the final state."""
    st = copy_state(st0)
    out = []
    for (P, u, pres, r) in trials:
        out.append(trial(m, st, T, P, u, pres))
        rest(m, st, r)
    return out, st

def tr(n, P=1, u=0, pres=BOTH, r=0):
    return [(F(P), u, frozenset(pres), r) for _ in range(n)]

def ystr(Y):
    if not Y:
        return '∅'
    return '{' + ','.join('ab'[x] for x in sorted(Y)) + '}'

def seqstr(out, with_tau=False):
    if with_tau:
        return ' '.join('%s@%s' % (ystr(Y), '-' if t is None else t) for (Y, t) in out)
    return ' '.join(ystr(Y) for (Y, _) in out)

# ---------------- Invariants (reading only the readings) ----------------
def viol_I1(out, trials):
    """I1: for two adjacent trials with rest 0, the same P and both paths presented, a sole lead {x} in the former ⇒ {x} in the latter. Return the violating k or None."""
    for k in range(len(out) - 1):
        P, u, pres, r = trials[k]
        P2, u2, pres2, r2 = trials[k + 1]
        if r != 0 or P != P2 or pres != BOTH or pres2 != BOTH:
            continue
        if len(out[k][0]) == 1 and out[k + 1][0] != out[k][0]:
            return k
    return None

def viol_I2(out, trials):
    """I2: P ≥ 1 and a nonempty presentation set ⇒ Y ≠ ∅."""
    for k, ((Y, _), (P, u, pres, r)) in enumerate(zip(out, trials)):
        if P >= 1 and pres and not Y:
            return k
    return None

def viol_absorb(out, trials):
    """∅ absorption: violated if, from some ∅ trial on, with the same P in every trial and both paths presented (rest arbitrary), a non-∅ appears again later. Return the violating k or None."""
    seen = None
    for k, ((Y, _), (P, u, pres, r)) in enumerate(zip(out, trials)):
        if pres != BOTH or (seen is not None and P != seen):
            seen = None
        if seen is not None and Y:
            return k
        if not Y and pres == BOTH:
            seen = P
    return None

def viol_I1p(out, trials, h0=0):
    """I1′: as I1, with the additional requirement that the entry store at the start of trial k is h_k = 0. Sufficient sources of h_k = 0: k is the first trial and the initial store is h0 = 0,
    or the previous trial is non-∅ (opening resets it to zero, and rest keeps it at 0). The first pair is included only when h0 = 0; when h0 > 0 the first pair lies outside the declared domain and is not counted."""
    for k in range(len(out) - 1):
        P, u, pres, r = trials[k]
        P2, u2, pres2, r2 = trials[k + 1]
        if r != 0 or P != P2 or pres != BOTH or pres2 != BOTH:
            continue
        if k == 0 and h0 != 0:
            continue
        if k > 0 and not out[k - 1][0]:
            continue
        if len(out[k][0]) == 1 and out[k + 1][0] != out[k][0]:
            return k
    return None

def viol_tau_nondecr(out, trials, x):
    """In a test segment (u = 0, rest 0, P unchanged, both paths presented), τ does not decrease while x is the sole lead; return the violating k (τ strictly decreases) or None."""
    prev = None
    for k, ((Y, t), (P, u, pres, r)) in enumerate(zip(out, trials)):
        if u != 0 or pres != BOTH:
            prev = None
            continue
        if Y == frozenset((x,)):
            if prev is not None and t < prev:
                return k
            prev = t
        if r != 0:
            prev = None
    return None

# ---------------- Exact binomial tails ----------------
def binom_tail_ge(n, p, k):
    p = F(p)
    q = 1 - p
    tot = Fr(0)
    for j in range(max(k, 0), n + 1):
        tot += math.comb(n, j) * p ** j * q ** (n - j)
    return tot

def binom_tail_lt(n, p, k):
    return 1 - binom_tail_ge(n, p, k)
~~~

A.2  t16c\_ext.py  Extension library: N11 and IFBC, N12 and its controls C12 and HOMB

~~~python
# -*- coding: utf-8 -*-
"""Appendix extension library of The Discrimination Form of Sedimentation Dynamics v1.0: N11 (goal feedback) and an independent implementation of the integral-feedback component IFBC; N12 (the blockade's own law) and its controls C12 and HOMB.
Standard library and Fraction only.
  N11: intention input z ∈ {0, 1, None}; on each path, d (the law of C16) and effort e ∈ [0, ē]; θ_x = max(0, g(d_x) − e_x);
       after the trial: if z = x, then e_x ← max(0, e_x − κ_d) when Y = {x}, and e_x ← min(ē, e_x + κ_u) otherwise; if z ≠ x, e_x ← max(0, e_x − κ_d); at rest d shallows and e is unchanged.
  IFBC: an independent implementation of the same law, written as integral feedback m ← clip(m + k_R(1 − a) − k_B·a, 0, m̄) subtracted from the threshold of C16 via c1.
  N12: blockade Brk (set by the protocol) and the head H impounded behind the dam. At the start of a trial: if Brk_x = 1 and (H_x ≥ B̄_x or the protocol lifts the blockade in this trial), release: Brk_x ← 0, d_x ← min(d_x + κ_s·H_x, D̄_x), H_x ← 0;
       during the trial a path with Brk_x = 1 does not open; after the trial, if Brk_x = 1 and x was presented, H_x ← H_x + ρ_H·P; at rest d shallows and H ← (1 − μ_H)H.
  C12: under the same protocol, Brk is set and lifted only by the protocol; there is no head. HOMB: in a rest beat a blocked path has H_x ← H_x + κ_H(H̄ − H_x), and its threshold is multiplied by (1 − H_x).
"""
from fractions import Fraction as Fr
import math
import t16b_core as tb
from t16b_core import g, open_step, BOTH
F = tb.F

# ---------------- N11 and IFBC ----------------
def n11_new(d=(0, 0), e=(0, 0)):
    return {'d': [F(d[0]), F(d[1])], 'e': [F(e[0]), F(e[1])]}

def n11_theta(m, st, x):
    v = g(m['g0'][x], m['c'][x], st['d'][x]) - st['e'][x]
    return v if v > 0 else Fr(0)

def n11_trial(m, st, T, P, z, pres=BOTH):
    P = F(P)
    taus = [open_step(n11_theta(m, st, x), Fr(0), P, T) if x in pres else None for x in (0, 1)]
    fin = [t for t in taus if t is not None]
    Y = frozenset(x for x in (0, 1) if fin and taus[x] == min(fin)) if fin else frozenset()
    tmin = min(fin) if fin else None
    for x in Y:
        cp = m['cap'][x]
        st['d'][x] = st['d'][x] + m['alpha'][x] if cp is None else min(st['d'][x] + m['alpha'][x], cp)
    if m.kind == 'N11':
        for x in (0, 1):
            if z == x:
                if Y == frozenset((x,)):
                    st['e'][x] = max(Fr(0), st['e'][x] - m['kd'])
                else:
                    st['e'][x] = min(m['ebar'], st['e'][x] + m['ku'])
            else:
                st['e'][x] = max(Fr(0), st['e'][x] - m['kd'])
    elif m.kind == 'IFBC':
        # Integral-feedback component: one methylation-like variable mm_x per path (stored in st['e']); activity a_x = 1 if and only if “x is not the target” or “x is the target and achieves the sole lead”;
        # discretization mm ← clip(mm + k_R(1 − a) − k_B·a, 0, m̄), k_R = κ_u, k_B = κ_d, m̄ = ē; c1: threshold offset = mm.
        for x in (0, 1):
            a = 0 if (z == x and Y != frozenset((x,))) else 1
            v = st['e'][x] + m['ku'] * (1 - a) - m['kd'] * a
            st['e'][x] = min(m['ebar'], max(Fr(0), v))
    return Y, tmin

def n11_rest(m, st, r):
    for _ in range(r):
        for x in (0, 1):
            st['d'][x] = (1 - m['lam'][x]) * st['d'][x]

def n11_run(m, st0, T, trials):
    st = {'d': list(st0['d']), 'e': list(st0['e'])}
    out = []
    for (P, z, pres, r) in trials:
        out.append(n11_trial(m, st, T, P, z, pres))
        n11_rest(m, st, r)
    return out, st

# ---------------- N12, C12, HOMB ----------------
def n12_new(d=(0, 0)):
    return {'d': [F(d[0]), F(d[1])], 'H': [Fr(0), Fr(0)], 'brk': [0, 0], 'Hm': [Fr(0), Fr(0)], 'events': []}

def n12_theta(m, st, x):
    th = g(m['g0'][x], m['c'][x], st['d'][x])
    if m.kind == 'HOMB':
        th = th * (1 - st['Hm'][x])
    return th

def n12_trial(m, st, T, P, pres=BOTH, block=(), unblock=(), k=0):
    P = F(P)
    for x in block:
        st['brk'][x] = 1
    for x in (0, 1):
        if st['brk'][x] == 1:
            if m.kind == 'N12' and (st['H'][x] >= m['Bbar'][x] or x in unblock):
                st['brk'][x] = 0
                cp = m['cap'][x]
                nd = st['d'][x] + m['ks'] * st['H'][x]
                st['d'][x] = nd if cp is None else min(nd, cp)
                st['events'].append((k, 'breach' if x not in unblock else 'release', x, st['H'][x]))
                st['H'][x] = Fr(0)
            elif x in unblock:
                st['brk'][x] = 0
    taus = []
    for x in (0, 1):
        if x in pres and st['brk'][x] == 0:
            taus.append(open_step(n12_theta(m, st, x), Fr(0), P, T))
        else:
            taus.append(None)
    fin = [t for t in taus if t is not None]
    Y = frozenset(x for x in (0, 1) if fin and taus[x] == min(fin)) if fin else frozenset()
    tmin = min(fin) if fin else None
    for x in Y:
        cp = m['cap'][x]
~~~
